## 2026-11-09: Characters, the gun and the helmet lit by where they are (rc_level_viewer, rc_level_plugin, rc_game_core, rc_combat, rc_squad_plugin, rc_enemies, rc_game, rc_menus)

- **Changed:** New `crates/rc_level_viewer/src/probe.rs` (`LightProbe`: light at any point from the level's baked light) and example `probe_check`. New `crates/rc_game_core/src/place_light.rs` (`PlaceLight` resource, `LitByPlace` tag). New `crates/rc_level_plugin/src/place_light.rs`: builds the probe at level load, gives tagged entities their own materials and sets them to the light where they are every 0.1 s, logs the light at the player's eyes every 5 s. Tagged: squad mate pieces, droids and other enemies (glow), first-person arm pieces and helmet pieces (colour). New data keys: `light_scale` in `squad/looks.toml` (1.1), `enemies/*.toml` (1.1), each weapon's `arms.light_scale` (0.85), `ui/helmet.toml` (0.8, on top of its own `brightness` 0.5). `docs/DESIGN.md` "Light by place", README.
- **Why:** Matching brightness with the original. The level itself already matched within about 20% on the two original screenshots (GEO_04A canyon, YYY_06A tunnel), but everything that moves used one fixed brightness everywhere: in the tunnel the gun was 2 to 3.5 times brighter than the original and the squad about twice as bright, and the helmet frame was too dark.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud with the game files copied read only from the PC: `rc_game --map yyy_06a` and `--map geo_04a` under Xvfb at 1600x1200 with an F12 screenshot, middle values of fixed areas compared with the user's original screenshots (helmet bars, gun body and side, walls, floor, ceiling); `probe_check` on yyy_06a, geo_04a, geo_01a, ras_01a, yyy_01b; `cargo test` for rc_level_viewer, rc_game_core, rc_menus, rc_ai, rc_combat, rc_squad_plugin, rc_level_plugin, rc_enemies; `rc_game --script`.
- **Result:** Original / before / after (0-255): tunnel gun body 22 / 76 / 25, gun side 56 / 113 / 43, helmet top 58 / 35 / 59, helmet lower left 66 / 40 / 68. Canyon (our view is not the same spot): gun 43 / 76 / 34 and 63 / 113 / 55, helmet 69 / 35 / 65 and 110 / 40 / 74. Squad mate brightest 2%: original 67-95, before 153, after about 105 (another spot). Level surfaces unchanged. Probe: 0.3 to 1.1 million samples per map, built in 30-140 ms. Light at the tunnel start 0.31 0.34 0.41. All unit tests pass (5 new). `rc_game --script`: 105 checks passed, 2 failed; both failures are the level-script sound checks, because the sound files were not copied to the cloud (the helmet check now paints the frame at the brightness it is drawn at).
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing. The scales come from two screenshots, one at an unknown spot; not the original's formula. No light direction (a character's sunny side isn't brighter), no per-light shading. The tactical visor's glow on squad mates stays at its fixed strength, so it now stands out more in dark places (it starts on in our game). The probe uses 6-22 MB per level; not measured on a phone.
- **Next:** Compare the starts of GEO_01A, GEO_03A, GEO_05A, RAS_01A, YYY_01B and YYY_04A with the user's original screenshots and tune the scales and the level's own brightness.

## 2026-11-10: Grievous's bodyguards appear and fight, so yyy_01e can end (data/enemies, data/weapons, data/grenades, rc_ai, rc_enemies)

- **Changed:** New `data/enemies/grievous_guard.toml` (claims `GrievousGuard*`), `data/weapons/grievous_missile_rack.toml` and `data/grenades/grievous_missile.toml` (a lobbed shot with no gravity, so it flies straight and explodes on touch). Enemy shields (`combat.shields`) came from main's Trandoshan work when merging; this branch's own copy of the same setting was dropped. New tool `rc_guard_check` (example in `rc_enemies`): runs yyy_01e's own scripts with its spawners and a stand-in player that kills each guard. `crates/rc_combat/tests/range.rs` lists the new weapon and grenade files. `docs/RESEARCH.md` gains the guard's numbers; `docs/DESIGN.md` and README updated.
- **Why:** The whole-campaign audit found yyy_01e can never end: it ends when `ThreeDeadGuards` is sent, which only the death of a GrievousGuard sends, and no enemy file claimed that class, so no guard ever appeared.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud with game files copied read only from the PC (yyy_01e, System and Properties packages, droid.ukx, droidtextures.utx, gen_sound). `RC_GAME_DIR=... cargo run --release -p rc_enemies --example rc_guard_check`; `rc_enemy_look_check`; `cargo test --release --workspace` (no game files); `cargo test -p rc_combat --test range real_game_sounds_and_model` (the sound part).
- **Result:** rc_guard_check 8 of 8: at 2 s `Spawn_Guard` makes guard 1 (tag Guard1); killed at 5 s it sends `GrievousGuard_dead`, the map's Counter1 sends `Spawn_Guard_Three`, which makes guard 2 (tag Guard3); killed at 8 s it sends `ThreeDeadGuards`; Dispatcher7 plays its lines and sends `EndLevel` at 31 s, and at 32 s the level script leaves for YYY_35A. Each guard has 2000 health and 250 shield. Look check: model, its own 1024x1024 skin and all 11 named animations found, 185 uu tall. The fire sound `wep_GEN_dSpidMisFire_01` and the placeholder blast sound are found in gen_sound. All workspace tests pass.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not played or tested on Windows. rc_guard_check stands in for the player and the landing pad door (it sends `Spawn_Guard` itself and kills guards by decree), so the fight itself isn't tested. Missiles fly straight instead of homing; no electro-staff shock; guards walk instead of leaping between perches; shield hits use the guard's table, not its shield table; a loaded save refills a guard's shield. The tower guard (`SeeGrievousGuard`) is made invulnerable by its script and waits for `GreivousVerified`, which a LineOfSightTrigger sends; without LineOfSightTrigger (PR #93, now in main) it would stay and fights and can't be killed. rc_guard_check now also sends `GreivousVerified` itself at 14 s: the tower guard's script then removes it at 15.6 s (9 of 9 checks). The thrown guard (`ThrownGrievousGuard`) appears standing instead of as a thrown body. Grievous himself (`Grievous`, a factory) isn't spawned. The two guards placed in the Tarfful scene are left to rc_cast (cutscene characters) as before; not checked.
- **Result on Windows (2026-11-10):** the user ran `rc_guard_check` on the PC: 9 of 9 passed (log read from `logs/guard_check.log`). Not played yet.
- **Next:** A playtest of yyy_01e on Windows (LineOfSightTrigger, PR #93, is now in main).

## 2026-11-10: Squad mates leave and rejoin the squad as the level scripts say (rc_squad, rc_squad_plugin, rc_scripts_plugin, rc_game_core)

- **Changed:** The squad now comes from the map (`spawn = "from_map"`, new default in `data/squad/squad.toml`): squad mates placed in the map start there, in the squad; mates made by a map's spawner arrive when its event is sent, out of the squad. New `rc_squad::Presence` (in the squad, away, not in the level) in `rc_squad/src/presence.rs`; `rc_squad::map` reads placed mates and the spawners' `PawnTagFromFactory` / `AIScriptFromFactory`. Level scripts' `ChangeSquadMember` now works (`rc_scripts_plugin/src/characters.rs` sends the new `rc_game_core::SquadMembership`). New `rc_squad_plugin/src/membership.rs`: spawner arrivals, joining and leaving, and away mates following script orders (walk, go to the player, turn, wait for the player, teleport, hide, leave the level). Bodies are made and removed as mates come and go (`sync_bodies`). Saves keep `presence` and `spawners_used` per mate. `rc_game --script` and `rc_squad_sim` keep all three with the player (`SquadSpawnOverride`). New test `tests/rejoin_room.rs`, new tool `examples/rejoin_survey.rs`. `docs/DESIGN.md`: "Where the squad comes from, leaving and rejoining".
- **Why:** The whole-campaign audit: 30 script steps on 16 maps take squad mates out of the squad and put them back (geo_01a-01c find Scorch, Fixer and Sev one by one; ras_02b's Fixer opens the final door and rejoins), and the port ignored them, always putting all three next to the player.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud, with the maps copied read only from the PC. `cargo test --release -p rc_squad -p rc_squad_plugin -p rc_scripts -p rc_scripts_plugin -p rc_game_core`; `rejoin_survey` on all maps; `rc_script_check ras_02b --fire PlayerAtFinalDoor@5`.
- **Result:** All tests pass. `rejoin_room` 2 of 2: Sev placed in the map comes to the player; Fixer arrives from his spawner out of the squad and stays put, walks to a door when his script says and the script hears he got there; Sev leaves and stays behind when the player walks off; both rejoin and follow; saving keeps who is where; a script removes Sev and his body goes. `rejoin_survey`: 51 maps, 30 `ChangeSquadMember` actions, 0 name nobody. ras_02b's script (director only) opens `FinalDoor` at 63.8 s and adds Fixer at 69.3 s.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not played, not run inside the full game (`rc_game --map ras_02b` not run), not tested on Windows. The player now starts some maps alone, as the map says (ras_01a, ras_01c, ras_02a, ras_02b, geo_05a, and geo_01a until Scorch is found); geo_05a having no squad mates at all is what the map file says but is not checked against the original. Squad mates' scripted animations (Fixer slicing the door) aren't played: logged and skipped. Squad mates in the squad ignore scripted walks. yyy_06d's second copies of Fixer and Scorch reuse the first body.
- **Next:** The user plays geo_01a (Scorch should join at the bunker) and ras_02b on Windows.

## 2026-11-09: Music follows the original's rules (rc_music, rc_game, rc_level, rc_scripts_plugin, rc_enemies)

- **Changed:** New crate `rc_music`: `library.rs` reads every music piece and group from the game's `*_music.uax` and `params_mus.uax`, and room music from `Properties/audioenv.u`; `imuse.rs` is the director (script music state, room music and overlays, the battle engine with its threshold, radii and criteria, victory flourish, player-down music, fades) as play and stop commands on three layers; `rc_music_check` checks every map's music against the install. `rc_level::music` reads a map's battle settings and audio volumes. `rc_game_core::music`: `MusicCue` messages and the `MusicThreats` resource. `rc_scripts_plugin`: the seven music actions become `MusicCue`s (counted as `music_cues`). `rc_enemies`: lists its droids in `MusicThreats`. `rc_game/src/music.rs` rewritten to carry out the commands with Bevy audio; menu music comes from each menu class's `BackgroundMusic`. `data/music/music.toml` now holds only our timings.
- **Why:** Thread "Play the game's music": menu and level music loaded from the install at runtime, following the original's rules for when pieces play. Before, music was a hand-written calm and battle list per map prefix.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_music -p rc_scripts_plugin -p rc_game` in the cloud. `rc_music_check` with the game's music packages, `audioenv.u`, the script packages and all 57 campaign maps copied read only from the PC (and the PC's list of `.ogg` names). `rc_game --frames 400` under xvfb with the menu packages and 14 real `.ogg` files.
- **Result:** 13 new director tests, the script cue test and the music settings tests pass. rc_music_check: 57 maps, 92 rooms with music, 726 script music actions, 0 problems; 274 pieces, 92 groups, 67 room classes; every piece has its `.ogg`. In the game run: "menus play: main menu musUI_mainmenuVOX_lp, loading nothing, pause nothing" and "musUI_mainmenuVOX_lp started on the sound card".
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested in a level, on Windows, or by listening. When a fight starts and ends, and the fade curves, are our reading of setting names (the original decides in compiled code). Not done: `BattleVolume`, the death stinger, debriefing, credits and title-card music, music state in save games, `MusicTrigger` (no map uses it).

## 2026-11-10: Barrel fireballs, explosion sounds and broken models (rc_effects, rc_effects_plugin, rc_level, rc_scripts_plugin, rc_game)

- **Changed:** New crate `rc_effects`: reads the game's particle effects (`Emitter` classes and their sprite and mesh emitters, with `engine.u`'s defaults) and runs them without Bevy; tool `rc_effect_check`. New crate `rc_effects_plugin`: `SpawnEffect` / `PreloadEffect` messages, draws sprites with the emitter's blend mode and flying debris models, plays the effect's sound, settings in `data/effects/effects.toml`. `rc_level::breakables` now reads each step's effect offset, model path and sound path, and the prop's rotation and scale. `rc_scripts_plugin` shows a breaking prop's effect and sound, and leaves its broken model for the step's life span. `rc_game --script`: 2 new checks. `docs/DESIGN.md`: new "Particle effects" section; `docs/RESEARCH.md`: effect format notes.
- **Why:** User asked for the barrel's fireball, sound and broken model after barrels started breaking.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_effects -p rc_effects_plugin -p rc_level -p rc_scripts_plugin` with `RC_GAME_DIR` set to the game files copied read only from the PC; `rc_effect_check` (575 effects read, 0 failed); `rc_game --script` in the cloud on top of main: 115 checks passed, 0 failed.
- **Result:** geo_01a's barrel shows `Explosion_Barrel_GEO` (up to 170 squares and 5 debris pieces at once, over after 9 s) and plays `exp_barrels` and `exp_GEN_barrel_01`; the scripted barricade blast plays `Dirt_Explo_Lrg`. geo_01b's barrels leave no broken model because the map itself says `Mesh=None`; geo_03a's leave `GEO_EplBarrel_Dest`.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows, by playing or by looking at it next to the original. Effect sounds aren't placed in 3D yet (they get quieter with distance instead; the level scripts' 3D sound from #70 could be reused). Beam and trail emitters aren't drawn. Debris bounces on a flat floor, not the level. The damaged step's model and the broken look in save files aren't done. Shots, grenades and other things don't spawn effects yet.
- **Next:** Effects for grenades and shot impacts; bacta dispenser effects (`BactaZap_Dispenser`).

## 2026-11-10: Gunship cannons and core ship turrets (rc_turrets, rc_turrets_plugin, data)

- **Changed:** New turret files `data/turrets/gunship_cannon.toml` (claims `GunshipCannon`) and `data/turrets/core_ship_turret.toml` (claims `CoreShipTurret`), with their weapons `data/weapons/gunship_turret.toml` and `core_ship_turret.toml`. `rc_turrets`: a turret file may have an `[automatic]` section (a turret that runs itself: folded until an event with its tag, then unfolds, fires at the nearest enemy it can see, cools down, can be shot to pieces), placed turrets now read `Event`, `FirstDamagedEvent`, `bDeployed` and `Health`, and turrets on walls or ceilings are placed and aimed with their full rotation. `rc_turret_check` gains `--hurt NAME@T:AMOUNT` and switches automatic turrets on and off. `rc_turrets_plugin`: the `automatic` system, a target entity for each automatic turret (what shots hit), its folded, unfolding and destroyed poses, and saving its state. New test `rc_turrets_plugin/tests/automatic.rs`. Notes in `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Mounted turrets"), `docs/RESEARCH.md`, README.
- **Why:** Thread "Make gunship cannons and core ship turrets work", from the level audit: geo_04a and geo_04d wait for the player to use a gunship's ball turret (`ClearTurretTracker`, `Conversation1454`), and geo_05a waits for its core ship turret to be shot (`TurretsShot`, then `EliteStart`). Built on PR #92 (mounted turrets).

- **Result:** All tests pass except `rc_combat`'s `real_game_sounds_and_model`, which fails in the cloud because the sound packages weren't copied (it lists every weapon's sound as missing, old and new). All placements found and claimed: 1 gunship cannon each on geo_04a and geo_04d, core ship turrets on geo_04b (2, on a wall), geo_05a (2) and geo_05c (3). geo_05a: `BDTurrets` switches `CoreShipTurret1` on, shooting it sends `TurretsShot` then `Security00`, and `ScriptedTrigger42` goes on to `EliteStart`. In the Bevy test the turret stays folded until `BDTurrets`, unfolds for 1.17 s, hits the player 5 at a time, and stops firing once destroyed. A test turns the wall-mounted turret and checks its barrels point at the target.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not run in the game or on Windows: nothing has been seen by eye. Firing sounds not heard (sound packages not copied). No death explosion, broken pieces or damage effects, `SetThreat` and the game's armour factor aren't done. Squads can't man the gunship cannon (`MarkerTurret`). `RASCeilingTurret`, `GeoAATurret` and `AATurret` aren't claimed yet.

## 2026-11-10: Cutscene characters cast shadows (rc_shadows, rc_cast, rc_game, rc_level_viewer)

- **Changed:** New crate `rc_shadows` (`ShadowsPlugin`, `CastsShadow`, `CharacterShadows`, `ShadowMaterial` + `shadow.wesl`, example `shadow_check`) and data file `data/shadows/shadows.toml`. `rc_cast` reads `bActorShadows` (`CastMember::shadow`) and tags marked characters. `rc_game` adds the plugin and sets it from SQUAD SHADOWS (`graphics.rs`). Separate fix in `rc_level_viewer/src/bump.rs`: the bump-mapped prop material no longer changes the vertex layout of Bevy's depth-only (shadow map) pipeline. DESIGN ("Character shadows"), README.

The bump fix: with SQUAD SHADOWS on, `rc_game --map pro` quit at once with `CRASH: graphics error (Validation) ... prepass_pipeline ... Location[7] Float32x4 ... not provided` (before this change too; any map with bump-mapped props).

With the depth trick switched off the overlap check fails (0.004 vs 0.044), so it catches double-dark patches. pro: `21 characters, 26 copies` (the 11 marked plus the commandos), no crash with SQUAD SHADOWS on; the gym screenshot shows the Kaminoan's and troopers' shapes on the floor. Before the bump fix: crash; after: none.

The final darkness is our reading of the original's numbers, not compared with an original screenshot (the original doesn't run on the PC now). Squad and enemies not given these shadows.

## 2026-11-10: Simple collision shapes, and walls that stop shots but not people (rc_package, rc_level, rc_level_plugin, rc_game)

- **Changed:** `rc_package::static_mesh` now keeps the reference to each mesh's simple collision shape (`collision_model`; it was read and thrown away). `rc_level::collision::read_simple_shape` reads that shape (a `Model`, like a map's BSP) and `solid_local_triangles` uses it for meshes with collision off on every section. `rc_level::class_look::blocks` tells whether an actor stops people and whether it stops shots. `map_collision` keeps props that stop shots but not people apart (`shot_triangles`, `shot_part_triangles`), and `rc_level_plugin::LevelParts::with_shots_only` adds them to `LevelCollision` for shots only (sweeps with no size). The barrels' "drawn triangles" stand-in from PR #78 is now only a last resort. New counts in `LevelInfo` and the log line `level: N props collide through their simple collision shape; M props stop shots but not people`. 2 new `rc_game --script` checks, 2 new unit tests. `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Simple collision shapes", "Shots and people"), README.
- **Why:** Thread "Fix walls you can walk through". geo_04c's 89 shield wall pieces (`K_GEO04_WallComponent`) let shots through, and models with collision off on every part had nothing to collide with.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud with game files copied read only from the PC. Read all 2,447 static meshes: 1,720 have a simple shape, 1,714 read (6 Karma test meshes use an older layout), they sit on their meshes. Checked the game's own collision triangle list matches the sections with collision on for 2,295 meshes, and is empty for every mesh with all sections off. Read the class defaults: `K_GEO04_WallComponent` has `bBlockPlayers`, `bBlockActors` and `bBlockNonZeroExtentTraces` off but is a shot target. `rc_walkcheck --fps 240` before and after on geo_01a, geo_04c, geo_03a, ras_01a. `cargo test -p rc_level -p rc_package -p rc_level_plugin`. `rc_game --script` in the cloud.
- **Result:** `rc_game --script`: 115 checks passed, 2 failed (the level sound checks: sound files not copied); new checks: geo_01a has 10 props colliding through their simple shape and 5 that stop shots only; at 4 of the 5 a line stops while a small body walks on. geo_04c: 98 props now collide through their simple shape, 89 wall pieces stop shots (5,874 triangles) but are still walked through, as in the original. geo_03a: the real jamming device (`PrimaryTarget`) was already solid; the one with no collision is its small hologram (DrawScale 0.04, every collision switch off in the map), which is right. Walk check: geo_01a 9 failed walks before and after, geo_04c 52 and 52, geo_03a 98 and 97, ras_01a 8 and 12 (the 4 new ones run into Raxus Prime floor and ceiling pipes that were walk-through before and are solid now). Unit tests pass.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing. Whether the original uses the simple shape for walking against meshes that also have collision on can't be read from the files (it's in the engine's C++ defaults), so those are unchanged. The 4 new ras_01a walk-check failures may be real places where the player now bumps a pipe the original also blocks, or our shapes being slightly bigger than the original's handling; not checked against the original. Shield wall pieces don't react to shots by moving (Karma physics).
- **Next:** The user shoots a geo_04c shield wall piece and walks through one on Windows, and walks the start of ras_01a past the pipes.

## 2026-11-10: Hologram briefings start and let the player go (rc_model, rc_cast)

- **Changed:** `rc_model::Model::triggers` reads the events an animation sends (`AnimNotify_Trigger`: `EventName`, or `TriggerSelf`), for the animations a model reads and, with `keep_sets`, every kept one (`Model::triggers_between`). `rc_cast`: an animated prop now reads its `AnimTriggered`; a level event named by its tag plays it (or stops it if playing), as the original's `AnimProp.Trigger` does (`trigger_props`). While any cast member's animation plays, `animate_cast` sends the events it passes to the level scripts as `RaiseEvent`s. New test `rc_model` `projector_throw_starts_the_briefing` (needs RC_GAME_DIR, else it says skipped).
- **Why:** yyy_04b, ras_03a and geo_03a start with a hologram briefing that holds the player. The scripts send `HoloProjector`; in the original that makes the projector play `ThrowHoloProjector`, and a marker in that animation sends `StartBriefing`, which runs the briefing and finally lets the player go. The port never played the throw, so nothing sent `StartBriefing` and the player stayed held.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test --release -p rc_model -p rc_cast` (with and without RC_GAME_DIR); `rc_game --map <map> --frames 700` in the cloud for each map, with the game's files copied read only from the PC, reading `logs/rc_game.log`.
- **Result:** All three: `HoloProjector3 triggered by HoloProjector ... plays ThrowHoloProjector`, then `animation ThrowHoloProjector sends event StartBriefing` about 2 s later, and `the briefing is over; the player can move` at 36.7 s (yyy_04b), 25.5 s (ras_03a) and 29.5 s (geo_03a). Before: no `StartBriefing`, held forever. A first try drew the projector a second time through `rc_level_plugin::anim_props`; the cast already draws every `AnimProp`, so the change went there instead.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing. The throw's own sounds (`AnimNotify_Sound`) aren't played by the cast. The commando who throws the projector (Commando07Delta / Commando62Delta in these maps) isn't drawn, so the projector flies on its own. Glow and flash effects (`SpawnActor Holo_Glow`) and hologram materials aren't done. Other `AnimProp`s with an `AnimTriggered` (gunship doors, cameras) now answer their events too; not checked one by one.
- **Next:** User playtest of yyy_04b on Windows 2026-11-10: the briefing played and they could walk after it. The briefing holograms drawn solid are fixed in PR #84.

## 2026-11-10: Line-of-sight triggers work (rc_scripts, rc_scripts_plugin)

- **Changed:** `rc_scripts`: the director reads `LineOfSightTrigger`s (new `SightTrigger`, `sight_candidates`, `sight_candidates_live`, `seen`, `look`, `sight_triggers`, `Stats::sight_triggers`), switches them on by tag, keeps them in saves (`DirectorSave::sight`); new `sight.rs` (`clear_line`); `[sight]` settings; new example `sight_triggers`. `rc_scripts_plugin`: new `sight.rs` (`look` system, before the scripts run each frame), `PlayStats::sight_seen`, the load log line counts them. `data/scripts/scripts.toml` `[sight]`. `docs/DESIGN.md`: "Line-of-sight triggers".
- **Why:** The level audit found these triggers did nothing, so 45 scripted moments in 25 maps never happened (Grievous seen on yyy_01e, the Trandoshan draggers on ras_03c, the Wookiee scenes on yyy_01d/04a/05c, hack terminals ...).
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_scripts -p rc_scripts_plugin`; `cargo run --release -p rc_scripts --example sight_triggers` on the 58 campaign maps copied read only from the PC; `rc_probe --class LineOfSightTrigger`.
- **Result:** Engine.u's defaults match ours (3000 uu, 15 degrees, on). 45 triggers in 25 maps, 39 with something waiting for their event. Looking from each trigger's own spot at what it watches, through the real walls and props: 40 clear, 5 blocked (yyy_01d's two Wookiees in the pit below, ras_03c, yyy_04a and yyy_06c; the player is meant to stand somewhere else there). rc_scripts 25 tests, rc_scripts_plugin 19 tests pass (3 new ones run the Bevy system with a camera: seen, looking away, a wall in between, a character that moved).
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing. The original also checks the thing was drawn recently; we use a line check instead. Several scripts these triggers start then need things the port doesn't have yet (for example placed Wookiees and Trandoshan variants that aren't spawned), so the scene may still stall later.
- **Next:** The user plays yyy_01e to the tower and checks the log for `the player sees what LineOfSightTrigger2 watches for`.

## 2026-11-10: Every Trandoshan appears and fights (data/enemies, data/weapons, rc_ai, rc_combat, rc_enemies)

- **Changed:** Seven new enemy files: `trandoshan_kashyyyk`, `trandoshan_grenadier`, `trandoshan_slaver`, `trandoshan_slaver_berserker`, `trandoshan_slaver_grenadier`, `trandoshan_heavy` and `trandoshan_scavenger`, and three weapons: `trandoshan_shotgun`, `trandoshan_rifle`, `scav_beam`. `trandoshan.toml` now names its two classes exactly instead of `TrandoshanMercenary*`. Code: enemies can have a shield (`[combat] shields`, `shield_recharge_rate`, `shield_recharge_delay`, using `rc_combat::Shield`); weapons can fire several pellets at once (`[shot] pellets`, `spread`; `rc_combat::shot::pellet_directions`). Tests: every Trandoshan class has exactly one file, Kashyyyk mercenaries have a shield, pellets stay inside the spread. Numbers in `docs/RESEARCH.md` "The other Trandoshans"; `docs/DESIGN.md` and README updated.
- **Why:** Thread "Add the missing Trandoshan enemies", from the whole-campaign audit: 685 spawners of nine Trandoshan kinds made nothing, so 12 Prosecutor and Kashyyyk maps had no enemies at all and scripts waiting for them to die couldn't go on.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud with game files copied read only from the PC. `rc_enemy_look_check`; `rc_enemy_spawners` on all Prosecutor and Kashyyyk maps; `cargo test -p rc_ai -p rc_combat -p rc_enemies --release`; `rc_droidview ras_02c --seconds 60 --check` and `rc_droidview yyy_04b --seconds 60 --check`.
- **Result:** Every Trandoshan spawner is claimed (slaver 227, Kashyyyk mercenary 225, scavenger 76, berserker 64, grenadiers 84, slaver grenadier 22, heavy 15). Look check: all 8 Trandoshan files load with all named animations and their real skins (24 of 24; the 4 failures are the droid and Geonosian models, whose packages weren't copied). ras_02c: slavers, a slaver grenadier and a heavy spawned and fought; the stand-in killed the grenadier, the heavy's repeater hit, 9 of 10 checks (only 144 pixels of Trandoshans in view, under the 300 the picture check wants). yyy_04b: 12 Kashyyyk mercenaries, grenadiers and slavers drawn (9793 pixels, not magenta, lit), shotgun pellets hit (two 33.6 hits in one blast), 24 of 35 shots hit; the stand-in died in 3.5 s before it hurt anyone, so 10 of 11 checks.
- **Still broken / not tested:** On Windows (2026-11-10) the user ran `rc_enemy_look_check`: 36 of 36 passed, all 8 Trandoshan files included. Not seen in a level on Windows or played. Enemy shields not seen taking a hit in a level (the stand-in died first); shield recharge delay is 0 because no class sets one (not sure). Scavengers walk instead of flying, don't blow up, have no hum. Slavers don't go berserk. The player can't pick up the shotgun or heavy repeater. Weapon sounds not listened to (gen_sound not copied). `TrandoshanMercenaryConc` uses the ordinary repeater instead of its concussion rifle. Squad mates and Wookiees not done.
- **Next:** Play a Kashyyyk level on Windows; flying for scavengers with the Geonosian flying work.


## 2026-11-10: Ammo boxes play their pickup sound and show what you took (rc_level, rc_game_core, rc_combat, rc_menus, rc_game)

- **Changed:** `rc_level::pickups::read_box` also reads each box's `PickupMessage` and `PickupSound` (with its `...Defaults` class on top). New message `rc_game_core::pickups::PickedUp { text, sound }`, sent by `take_boxes` for every box taken. New `rc_combat::pickup_sound` reads the sounds a level's boxes use when the level loads and plays them. `rc_menus::hud`: new `{pickup}` text, `pickup` condition and fraction, an element can `fade`, and `[pickup_message]` (seconds 3, fade 1). `data/ui/hud.toml`: a `pickup` line centred 35% down the screen. `rc_game`: the original HUD shows and fades it; the placeholder HUD adds it to its message list. New tests: `taking_a_box_says_what_it_was_and_plays_its_sound` (rc_combat), `the_pickup_message_fades_at_the_end` (rc_menus), and a self-test check in `rc_game --script`. Notes in `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Pickup sound and message") and README.
- **Why:** Thread "Add pickup sounds and messages": boxes refilled silently with nothing on screen.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud with the game files copied read only from the PC: `rc_probe --class` for all 7 box classes and their Defaults; `cargo test -p rc_level -p rc_game_core -p rc_menus -p rc_combat -p rc_level_plugin -p rc_game -p rc_probe`; `rc_game --script` on geo_01a; `rc_probe --hud` preview pictures.
- **Result:** The game's words: "DC-17m Blaster Clips", "DC-17m Sniper", "DC-17m Anti-Armor Charge Packs", "Thermal Detonators", "Sonic Detonators", "EMP Grenades", "Flashbang Detonators". Sound `GEN_Sound.Weapons.GenericWeaponPickup` (EMP box: `GEN_Sound.Interface.int_GEN_wepPickup_01`). Log: `pickups: 1 of 1 pickup sounds read from gen_sound: GenericWeaponPickup`, `pickups: played sound GEN_Sound.GenericWeaponPickup`. Self-test: "taking it shows its pickup message and plays its pickup sound: 20 letters drawn in the pickup line, centred 35% down" PASS; 114 passed, 2 failed (the two level voice checks: RAS_Voice etc. weren't copied, same as before this change). Picture: project files `pickup-feedback/pickup_message_preview_16x9.png`.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows, and the sound was not listened to (the cloud has no speakers; it was read and started). The original draws this message in native code, so its exact size and fade are our choice; position, colour, 3 seconds and one-at-a-time come from the game's `PickupMessagePlus`. The EMP box's sound wasn't heard in a level (geo_01a has none).
- **Next:** User takes a box on Windows and listens. Weapon pickups (another thread) can send the same `PickedUp` message.

## 2026-11-10: Optional squad ammo and box fetching, off by default (rc_squad, rc_squad_plugin, data/squad)

- **Changed:** New `rc_squad/src/ammo.rs` (`AmmoSettings`, `SupplyBox`, `Squad::take_box`, `think_fetch`) and `[ammo]` in `data/squad/squad.toml` (`limited = "no"`). Shots and grenades count down only when `limited` is on; `shoot`, `pick_target`, `throw_on_own` and grenade posts check it. New `rc_squad_plugin/src/ammo.rs` (`gather_boxes`, `take_boxes`) uses the player's touch test and marks a taken box like the player's (hidden, saved). Squad mates' counts saved in `[squad]` (`ammo`). New test `rc_squad_plugin/tests/ammo_room.rs` and 7 tests in `rc_squad`. Notes: `docs/DESIGN.md` "Optional squad ammo", README.
- **Why:** Thread "Let the squad use ammo boxes": the user picked "optional setting, off" after hearing the original squad never takes boxes.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_squad -p rc_squad_plugin` and clippy in the cloud (no game files). Then `rc_game --map geo_01a --frames 1300` (game files copied read only from the PC) with a mod `squad/squad.toml` holding only `[ammo] limited = "yes"`, `fetch_below = 0.5` (so they go at once with 120 of 300), `fetch_range = 6000`.
- **Result:** rc_squad 57 pass, ammo_room 2 of 2: switched off, nobody counts shots or goes near the boxes; switched on, Sev (20 of 300) and Fixer (0 grenades) fetch the right box in about 2 s, end with 300 and 5, and are back by the player. geo_01a: the mod is read (`ammo: limited, ...`); the nearest boxes to the start are 3700-3900 uu away, so with the shipped 1500 uu range nobody would go there. With 6000 uu, Scorch walked 2745 uu, took `DC17mBlasterBox6` (+180, 300 of 300) and came back to the player. Sev and Fixer got stuck on the way at about (830, 230) (the squad's usual path finding, not new) and gave up after the time limit; they then hopped from box to box, so a wait after giving up was added (test `after_giving_up_on_a_box_they_go_back_before_trying_another`). Re-run with the wait: all three give up at t=40 s, walk back toward the player, wait 40 s (the mod's give-up time), then try other boxes; Scorch took `DC17mBlasterBox6` at t=95 s and came back to 153 uu from the player; Sev and Fixer got stuck at the same spot again.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing. No HUD for squad ammo. Squad mates don't play a reload or say anything about ammo. Fetching only happens with no job; a squad mate on a sniper or anti-armour post who runs out just stops firing that gun. A box on a shelf the squad can't reach costs `fetch_give_up` seconds.
- **Next:** If wanted, the user tries it with the mod above on Windows.

- **Changed:** `rc_model::Model::sounds`: the sounds an animation names (`AnimNotify_Sound`) and when. New message `rc_game_core::PropAnim` (actor, animation). `rc_level_plugin::anim_props` reads the props' `Load` and `Holster` animations and their sounds, and `animate` plays an asked-for animation once (re-posing the model each frame) with its sounds from the prop. The bacta system sends `Load` when the player starts healing and `Holster` when they stop.

- **Still broken / not tested:** Not heard or seen on Windows. Which animation the original plays when is our reading of the names (Load on start, Holster on stop). The zap effect (`DispenserEffectClass` BactaZap_Dispenser, and BactaZap_Dispenser_Body on the player) waits for the particle effects system being built in its own thread. Dispensers still aren't solid.

## 2026-11-10: Dispensers stand on the floor; F uses them instead of ordering the squad (rc_level, rc_scripts_plugin, rc_squad_plugin)

- **Changed:** Animated props are drawn lower by their `CollisionHeight` (`class_look::anim_mesh_of` returns it; `map_collision` moves them down). New shared resource `rc_game_core::UseClaimed`: the scripts' bacta system sets it while the player stands at a switched-on dispenser, and the squad leaves that F press alone (F is both Use and the squad's context order).
- **Why:** The user's playtest: the dispenser floated about 2.4 m up (the map places these actors by the middle of their collision cylinder, the model's origin is its base: all five on geo_01a stood 116 to 121 units above the floor, the class height is 120). Their log also showed each F press sent the squad to "secure position" at the aim, and they let go of F at 195/200 health, so the dispenser never finished and the barricade stayed.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test --release -p rc_scripts_plugin -p rc_squad_plugin -p rc_level -p rc_game_core`; `rc_game --script` in the cloud.
- **Result:** Tests pass. 110 checks passed, 2 failed (the same sound checks). The picture shows the dispenser standing on the floor; the log says `context order left alone: the player is using something` when Use is held at it, and the barricade opens 3.7 s later.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows. Use still has to be held until health is full (as in the original); nothing shows healing except the health bar. Dispensers aren't solid and don't animate.
- **Next:** The user holds F at the dispenser until health is full.

## 2026-11-10: Bacta dispensers are drawn (rc_level, rc_level_plugin)

- **Changed:** `rc_level::class_look::anim_mesh_of` finds props the player uses (`ActivateItem`: bacta dispensers) whose class draws an animated model (`DrawType` DT_Mesh). `map_collision::load` lists them (`anim_props`). New `crates/rc_level_plugin/src/anim_props.rs` reads each model once with `rc_model` and draws every copy where the map puts it (`AnimProp` component; `LevelInfo::anim_props`). `rc_game --script` has 2 new checks and a picture `logs/bacta_dispenser.png`.

Their log showed the dispenser switched on and "Hold F" shown, but the player never got within reach.

A first try drew every actor with an animated model and put frozen clone troopers, droids and a gunship around the level, so it is limited to things the player uses.

## 2026-11-09: Level prompts name the real Use key (rc_scripts_plugin)

- **Changed:** `crates/rc_scripts_plugin/src/lib.rs`: a script prompt's `@` (the Use button) is now replaced with the button's name on the device used last (`InputPrompts`), not the word "USE".
- **Why:** The user couldn't use geo_01a's bacta dispenser: the prompt said "Hold USE to use Bacta Dispenser", but Use is F on the keyboard (E does nothing), so the barricade never came down.

- **Result:** The log shows `ShowPrompt: shows "Hold F to use Bacta Dispenser"` at t=47 s, about 16 s after the shell hits the player. 107 checks passed, 2 failed (the same two level-sound checks as before, sound packages not copied).
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows. Gamepad names come from `button_names.toml` and weren't checked here.

## 2026-11-09: Charges, slicing and breaching set off the level (rc_scripts, rc_scripts_plugin, rc_squad_plugin, rc_movers)

- **Changed:** New shared message `rc_game_core::MarkerJob` (anchor, step Started/Armed/Done). `rc_squad_plugin` sends it when a squad mate starts a marker job or takes a post, lights a fuse, and finishes. New `rc_scripts::markers` reads every squad marker's anchor (DemolitionCharge, HackTerminal*, HackDoor*, BreachDoor*) and the map events it sends; new `rc_scripts_plugin/src/markers.rs` sends them to the level scripts, blows away a charge's `PropToDestroy` (`Director::destroy_prop_named`) and opens the locked double doors (`MoverSet::open_named`, `LevelMovers::open_named`). `rc_script_check` lists each map's anchors. `rc_game --script` has 4 new checks (geo_01a's bunker charge, geo_01b's hangar door).
- **Why:** An audit of the prologue and all 13 Geonosis maps found this the biggest thing stopping play: the squad did demolition, slicing and breach jobs, but nothing listened (`SquadJobDone` had no reader), so bunker doors, barricades, shields and locked doors never opened and the scripts waiting for them (about 45 places) stood still.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test --release -p rc_scripts -p rc_scripts_plugin -p rc_movers -p rc_movers_plugin -p rc_squad_plugin -p rc_game_core` (3 new tests in markers.rs). `rc_script_check` on pro and the 13 Geonosis maps with the game's files copied read only from the PC. `rc_game --script` in the cloud (see Result).
- **Result:** All tests pass. rc_script_check: `57 marker anchors; 61 of their events are waited for by scripts; 38 of 38 doors they open are doors the game moves`. `rc_game --script` (cloud, software drawing): 107 checks passed, 2 failed. The 4 new ones passed: geo_01a's charge sent DemoUnderway, SpawnDemoDroids, DemoChargeArmed and BunkerDoorDestroyed, the bunker door prop (LargeBunker) was blown away and ScriptedTrigger4 moved on; geo_01b's hangar door MoverDB_GEO6 started shut and opened 0.12 s after the lock was sliced. The 2 failures are the level-sound checks: this run didn't have the big sound packages (character_voice, gen_sound, geo_sound) copied over, which those checks need.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing a level through. The charge, console and lock don't change their look or play their sounds. The player can't do these jobs with Use yet. Sliced doors stay open instead of working as automatic doors again. Which steps were sent isn't saved.
- **Next:** The user orders the squad to blow geo_01a's bunker door. Separate threads handle ammo boxes, breakable objects, missing enemies and squad lifts.

## 2026-11-09: The original debriefing screen at the end of each campaign (rc_game, rc_menus, rc_scripts)

- **Changed:** `rc_scripts`: `Effect::ChangeLevel` carries a `debriefing` (menu class, title, text, picture) when `ACTION_ShowDebriefingScreen` sent it. `rc_game_core`: new `ShowDebriefing` message holding the `ChangeLevel`. `rc_scripts_plugin` sends it instead of the `ChangeLevel` for the debriefing. `rc_menus`: new `debriefing.rs` fills the original `CTDebriefingInfo` page in (title, text, picture, CONTINUE on a PC); `Library::resolve_loose` finds a picture named without its group. `rc_game`: new `debriefing.rs` pauses play, opens the new `debriefing` page (`[pages.debriefing]` in `data/ui/original_menus.toml`) and sends the held `ChangeLevel` at CONTINUE; back does nothing there; a marked placeholder page if the original can't be drawn. `rc_probe --menu`/`--pages` fill the page with sample words. `rc_game --script`: 5 new checks.
- **Why:** The end of each campaign went straight on to the next one; the original shows its debriefing screen first. User asked for one thread per remaining item.
- **Tested how:** Read the three campaign endings from geo_05c, ras_04d and yyy_06d with `rc_script_check` (staged copies of the maps). `rc_probe --pages` with the install's files. `cargo test -p rc_scripts -p rc_scripts_plugin -p rc_menus -p rc_game_core -p rc_save`. Full `xvfb-run rc_game --script` in the cloud with about 860 MB of staged game files: it asks for a debriefing in geo_01b the way geo_05c does, then presses Back and CONTINUE.
- **Result:** `rc_probe --pages`: `page debriefing: USES THE ORIGINAL (1 buttons), 0 overlapping texts`. The geo_05c and ras_04d pictures are named without their group (`GUIContent.GEO_Debrief`) and are found as `GUIContent.LoadInfoScreens.Geo_Debrief`. Self-test `SCRIPT DONE: 109 checks passed, 0 failed`; the debriefing screenshot matches the software painting (mean difference 5.3 of 255, 2.8% of pixels off by more than 40); Back leaves it showing; CONTINUE loads geo_01b in 0.6 s.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing a campaign to its end (the real screens were only read from the maps, not reached in play). The GEO/RAS/YYY classes' own music (`musGEO_cadence_lp` and so on) isn't played; the level's music carries on. Hint screens (`bIsForHintText`) still aren't shown (the original shows them only with a debug switch). Other languages: the original hides "YOU HAVE UNLOCKED" outside English; we always show it.
- **Next:** Play the debriefing's own music; check the three real endings by playing to them.

## 2026-11-09: Low light mode gets the original's night-vision look (rc_visor)

- **Changed:** New `crates/rc_visor/src/night_original.rs` (reads the original's low light vision mode `FrameFX.VisionModes.VisionNight`, its shader's window and grain pictures and numbers from the install), `night_vision.rs` and `night_vision.wesl` (one full-screen pass on the player's camera: black and white from the sum of squared colours, the original's visor window, its film grain, fade in from black), `lib.rs` (the pass, the mode's black distance fog, log lines), `rules.rs` (`night_brightness`, `window_u` and their tests), `[low_light]` in `data/visor/tactical.toml`, `visor_check` (black and white, dark corners outside the window, picture back to normal after), the `rc_game --script` visor checks (night vision and fog on in low light, off after). rc_visor now uses rc_menus and rc_image.
- **Why:** Low light mode was only a weak helmet lamp (a placeholder). The original also changes the whole picture.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud with the game files copied read only from the PC. `RC_GAME_DIR=... cargo test -p rc_visor`; `visor_check` with and without the game folder; `rc_game --script` on geo_01a.
- **Result:** Read from the install: window HUD_GlassNight 512x512, grain FilmGrain 128x128, grain strength 0.118, grain repeats 9.22 x 6.54, keeps 0.2 of the last picture, fog 555 to 4500. rc_visor 13 tests pass. visor_check: 20 passed, 0 failed with and without the game folder (low light: colour spread 5.0 against 17.6 in tactical, top left corner 0.9 of 255 behind the window).
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing. Not compared with the real game (it doesn't run on the user's PC right now). The original's 20% trail of the last picture and the fade out are not done. The black-and-white sum darkens dim places, as the original's does; whether ours matches its brightness is not checked.
- **Next:** The user tries low light in a dark place (X twice from the start of a level) and says whether it is too dark; `boost` in the data file brightens it.

## 2026-11-09: The cutscene characters appear (rc_cast, rc_model, rc_game)

- **Changed:** New crate `rc_cast` (`CastPlugin`, `read::find_cast`) and data file `data/cast/cast.toml`. `rc_model::ModelSpec::keep_sets`, `Model::sets`, `Model::named_clip`, `Model::local_clip`. `rc_game` adds `CastPlugin`. `docs/DESIGN.md`: new "The cast" section and crate table row.
- **Why:** User playtest of the prologue: "no other NPC's of any kind". Characters placed in maps that weren't droids or Delta were never spawned (158 "no character" lines in the prologue's log).

- **Result:** pro: 54 cast members, all drawn with their models: Taun We and the second Kaminoan talking in the nursery, the young clones sitting at their desks and looking about on cue, the holograms hidden and shown on cue, troopers in the helmet room, and Sev, Fixer and Scorch in their own colours at the end. geo_01briefing: 15 (troopers, the gunship, walkers, spider droids); geo_01a: 14 (the gunship troopers, the rope, walkers). "no character" lines in a full prologue-to-geo_01a run: 38, from 192.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows. Characters brought in by `PawnFactory`s aren't spawned (the prologue's walking young clones). Hologram materials are plain grey. The cast doesn't collide, fall, fight or get hurt. Lit with a steady ambient share, not the zone's light.

## 2026-11-09: Geonosian elites appear and fight (data/enemies, data/weapons, rc_enemies)

- **Changed:** New `data/enemies/geonosian_elite.toml` (claims `GeonosianElite*`) and `data/weapons/elite_beam_enemy.toml` (the Elite Beam as an instant-hit weapon fired in one-second bursts of 10 hits). No engine code changed. New tool `rc_enemy_spawners` (example in `rc_enemies`): lists which enemy file claims each map's spawners and which classes no file claims. New test `a_geonosian_elite_burns_the_player_with_its_beam`. `docs/RESEARCH.md` gains the elite's and the beam's numbers; `docs/DESIGN.md` and README updated.
- **Why:** Thread "Add missing enemies and clone allies": blocker 4 of the level audit. 19 elite spawners on 6 Geonosis maps made nothing, so level scripts waiting for elites to die (DeadElite, PreventChaos, EliteWave02Dead on geo_03c) could never go on. Elites were the missing type on the most maps.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud with game files copied read only from the PC. `rc_enemy_look_check` (15 of 15); `rc_enemy_spawners` on all 14 Geonosis maps; `cargo test -p rc_enemies -p rc_ai -p rc_combat`; `rc_game --map geo_03c --frames 1500`; `rc_droidview geo_01a --seconds 60 --check` from a copy of `data` where the elite file claims the battle droids' spawners (so elites fight the stand-in on a real level).
- **Result:** All 19 `GeonosianElite` spawners (geo_03c 3, geo_04a 2, geo_04b 1, geo_04c 2, geo_05a 8, geo_05b 3) are now claimed. Model loads with all 11 named animations and its own 1024x1024 skin. rc_droidview: 5 elites drawn (3726 pixels, not magenta, brightness 78 vs skin 80), 382 beam hits fired, 112 hit the stand-in for 302 damage (2.7 each: 18 x the player's 0.15 for CTDamageEnergyBeamEnemy), 60 of the stand-in's shots hit them and none died (1000 health): 7 of 7 checks passed. rc_game geo_03c: the 3 elite spawners load and wait for the level's events (not reached in 1500 frames). Picture: project files `enemies/geonosian_elites_geo_01a_test.png` (level textures were not copied, so walls are grey).
- **Still broken / not tested:** On Windows (2026-11-10) the user ran `rc_enemy_look_check`: 15 of 15 passed. Not seen in a level on Windows or played. Elites walk (200 uu/s) instead of flying and dodging; on geo_03c, where they start high up, a stand-in test found they couldn't reach the player on foot. Wings use the body's skin. How fast the original's beam does damage is our guess (it fires every frame). No beam charge-up, beam sound not listened to (sound packages not copied), the beam is drawn as the tracer, and the player can't pick it up.
- **Next:** Flying for Geonosians (warriors and elites), then droidekas, dwarf spider droids and Phidna, one PR each.

## 2026-11-10: Shots now hit the Geonosian explosive barrels (rc_level)

- **Changed:** `rc_level::map_collision` gives a breakable prop its drawn triangles (`collision::drawn_triangles`) when its model has collision switched off on every part, as the explosive barrels' model does (the game uses the model's simple collision shape there, which we don't read).
- **Why:** The user shot an explosive barrel in geo_01b and nothing happened. All 20 of geo_01b's barrels had nothing to hit, so shots flew through them and landed on the sturdier canister behind (the log showed `K_GeoBarrel4` taking the hits).
- **Tested how:** Read geo_01b, 03a, 03c, 03d, 04c, 04d and 05b with the game files copied read only from the PC and counted breakable props with nothing to hit: geo_01b 21 -> 1 (hidden), geo_04d 15 -> 6 (all hidden; the rock covers only needed their model packages copied), geo_05b 0. `cargo test -p rc_level` passes. `rc_game --script` (cloud, game files copied read only): a geo_01a barrel breaks after 3 shots, explodes 0.3 s later and hurts the stand-in next to it; 108 checks passed, 2 failed (the level sound checks: sound files not copied).
- **Still broken / not tested:** geo_04c's 89 `K_GEO04_WallComponent`s and geo_03a's `JammingDevice` are props players walk through, which we leave out of collision, so shots still pass through them. Not tested on Windows yet.
- **Result:** User playtest on Windows (geo_01b): barrels break, explode, hurt what's near and disappear. The self-test now also checks the broken barrel is hidden.
- **Next:** The barrel's fireball, sound and broken model.

## 2026-11-09: Breakable props: barrels, clamps, holo machines, rock cover (rc_level, rc_scripts, rc_scripts_plugin, rc_combat, rc_level_plugin, rc_level_viewer, rc_game)

- **Changed:** New `rc_level::breakables` reads every prop's health, damaged and destroyed steps, explosion and events (map first, then class defaults). `map_collision` and the level viewer keep them as separate parts. `rc_scripts::Director::add_breakables` / `hurt_prop` run the game's break rules and send the props' events (and a prop's own `Event` now comes before `DestroyedState.Event`); damaged state is saved. New `rc_scripts_plugin/src/breakables.rs`: shots (`ShotLanded`, which now carries damage and damage type) and grenade blasts hurt props; props with an explosion go off 0.1-0.2 s after breaking. New `rc_combat::Explode` (radius damage for anything that isn't a grenade). `rc_game --script`: 4 new checks (shoot a geo_01a barrel). `docs/DESIGN.md`: "Breakable props".
- **Why:** Blocker 3 of the level audit: barrels, geo_05b's clamps and dispensers, holo machines and rock cover took no damage, and about 25 script steps wait for one to break.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_scripts -p rc_scripts_plugin -p rc_level` (new: shot props break and tell the scripts; damaged step rules; broken props saved; three shots break a barrel and it sets off the next). Read all 13 Geonosis maps with the game files copied read only from the PC.
- **Result:** Geonosis: 838 breakable props, 53 of them send an event the scripts use (geo_05b: 5 clamps `Clamp00`-`Clamp40`, 5 dispensers `DispenserDead..`; geo_04c: 2 holo machines; geo_04d: 9 rock covers; geo_01b/03a/03c/03d: barrels). Unit tests pass.
- **Still broken / not tested:** `rc_game --script` with the new checks not run yet (needs more game files copied; the PC went offline). Not tested on Windows or by playing. Broken and damaged models, effects and sounds not shown (props disappear), after-effects (fire, electricity) not done, loose props not pushed. Prop damage tables (`MyDamageVariance`) aren't found in the class defaults, so all damage counts in full. Rock cover follows the game's rule of vanishing in its damaged step without sending its event.
- **Next:** Run the full self-test, then the user shoots a barrel on Windows.
## 2026-11-09: Squad mates ride lifts (rc_movers, rc_movers_plugin, rc_game_core, rc_squad_plugin)

- **Changed:** `rc_movers::MoverSet::step` remembers which movers moved and from where; new `MoverSet::carry` (and `forget_moves`) carries or pushes any other body by those moves, with the same "when blocked" rule as for the player. `rc_movers_plugin`: `LevelMovers::carry`, `LevelMovers::floors`, forgets the moves at the start of each frame, inserts the new `rc_game_core::MovingFloors` resource (trait `CarryBodies`). `rc_squad_plugin`: new `ride_movers` system before the squad moves, `SquadState::riding`, logs `Sev stands on Mover37`. New test `rc_squad_plugin/tests/lift_room.rs`; 4 new tests in `rc_movers`. Notes in `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Squad mates (and other bodies) ride too"), `docs/PLUGINS.md`, README.
- **Why:** Blocker #5 of the level audit: squad mates didn't ride lifts, so on geo_05a, b and c (which start on a lift) the squad was left behind. In the original every pawn standing on a mover rides it.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_movers -p rc_movers_plugin -p rc_squad_plugin -p rc_game_core` in the cloud, no game files.
- **Result:** `lift_room`: Sev, Fixer and Scorch stand on a 400 uu lift with the player and all arrive at z 410.1 with the player, never more than 0.1 uu off the lift top on the way; without `MovingFloors`, 3 of 3 are left at the bottom (the old behaviour). `rc_movers`: a squad mate rides up and back down, a closing door turns back for a squad mate in the way, nobody is moved after `forget_moves`. 17 of 17 `rc_movers` tests pass.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on a real map (geo_05a, b, c) or on Windows. Squad mates don't set off triggers or bump-opened doors (only the player does, as before). Squad mates who aren't on a lift when it leaves don't wait or catch up. Droids don't ride lifts yet (they can use `MovingFloors` the same way).
- **Next:** Play geo_05a on Windows and check the log for `squad: ... stands on Mover...` and the squad arriving at the top.

## 2026-11-09: Moving and changing lights now light props (rc_level_viewer)

- **Changed:** New `crates/rc_level_viewer/src/prop_light.rs` and `prop_light.wesl`: `PropMaterial` (Bevy's standard material plus our shader) draws props and terrain as `texture x baked corner colour + texture x real-time lights`. `draw.rs` uses it for every prop and terrain batch outside the sky; `DrawReport` counts them (`coloured_live`). New `live_light_on_props` setting in `lighting.cfg` (1 = like walls, 0 = off, the old look). `PropLightPlugin` added in `LevelPlugin`, `rc_level_viewer`, `rc_walk` and `rc_droidview`. After the bump maps (#68) reached main: bump-lit props get real-time lights too (`bump_lit.wesl` adds Bevy's lighting on the bump-tilted surface; `BumpParams::live_light`). New check program `cargo run -p rc_level_viewer --example prop_light_check`.
- **Why:** Props were drawn with an "unlit" material, so real-time lights (the map's bDynamicLight lights, the low-light helmet lamp, later muzzle flashes) lit the walls but not the crates, pillars and terrain next to them.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud: `prop_light_check` (two identical props, one with real-time light on props and one without, a map light switched off, on, moved away and moved over). `rc_level_viewer geo_01a` and `yyy_06a` captured with `live_light_on_props = 1` and `= 0` and compared pixel by pixel (game files copied read only from the PC). `rc_game --map geo_01a --frames 400`. `cargo test -p rc_level_viewer`.
- **Result:** After merging main with bump maps: `PROP LIGHT CHECK: 9 passed, 0 failed` (bump-mapped test prop 97.0 -> 160.7 with the light on, back to 97.0 when it moves away); yyy_06a start view with its 11 bump-mapped batches identical to the pixel with real-time light on props on or off. Before that: `PROP LIGHT CHECK: 7 passed, 0 failed`: light off, both props 66.0 (same as the old way); light on, lit prop 97.9, the other 66.0; light far away 66.0; light moved to the other side 72.4. geo_01a and yyy_06a start views: mean difference 0.000, no pixel changed (no real-time light in view there), so baked light, holes, terrain layers and see-through surfaces look as before. The game log says `76 props/terrain lit by real-time lights too (strength 1)` on geo_01a; no errors.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing. Not looked at in the game near one of geo_01a's 2 real-time lights or with the low-light helmet lamp. Light strengths are still the placeholders walls use. Whether the original lit props per corner or per pixel is not known. Shadows from moving lights are still off.
- **Next:** The user turns on low-light mode (X) in a dark corner and checks that crates and pillars light up like the walls.

## 2026-11-09: 3D SOUND played sounds in the wrong ear (rc_game sound_options)

- **Changed:** `crates/rc_game/src/sound_options.rs`: the listener's ears are given to Bevy swapped (`ears_swapped`), with a test.
- **Why:** The user couldn't hear geo_01a's opening explosion. Looking into it showed that Bevy's 3D sound (rodio 0.22 `Spatial`) makes the ear further from a sound the louder one. This affected every placed sound with 3D SOUND on (enemies too), not only script sounds.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud, recording Bevy's sound output to a file (ALSA file device) with a scratch program that plays the game's `exp_GEN_tanker_01` through the same 3D path, and rodio's `Spatial` alone on a test tone. The user's `logs/rc_game.log` (read from the PC) was checked too.
- **Result:** Sound 20 m to the right, before: left 0.98, right 0.49; after: left 0.49, right 0.98. The opening explosion at the user's geometry came out at peak 0.73/0.74 (flat: 0.98), so it is not silent in the cloud. The user's log shows it started on the sound card at loudness 1.00, 8,341 uu away.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Why the user heard nothing is **not found**. The cloud recording plays it. Not listened to on Windows.
- **Next:** The user listens again; if still silent, record a run with 3D SOUND off to compare.

## 2026-11-09: Level script sounds come from their place in the world (rc_scripts_plugin, rc_package, rc_combat, rc_game)

- **Changed:** `rc_package::sound`: `AudioParams` (Radius, NonPositional, bDisable3DFalloff), `read_all_audio_params`, `params_of`. `rc_combat`: `GameSound.params`, `read_audio_params`. `rc_scripts_plugin/src/sounds.rs`: each played sound is heard everywhere or placed at its character (followed), tagged actor or script spot, with loudness by distance updated every frame (`Placed`, `follow`); the log line says where from, how far, and how loud. `rc_scripts` settings: `[sound] default_radius = 2000`. `rc_game/src/sound_options.rs`: placed sounds get a `SpatialScale` of 1/1000 so Bevy's own distance fading no longer applies. `rc_script_check` counts where every script sound would be heard from. `rc_game --script`: the script-lines check also wants a placed sound.
- **Why:** Script sounds all played flat at full loudness. The game's own sound settings (`params_vox.uax`, `params_sfx.uax`) say which ones are radio and which come from a place.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud with the game files copied read only from the PC: `cargo test -p rc_package -p rc_combat -p rc_scripts -p rc_scripts_plugin` and `rc_game`'s sound_options tests; `rc_script_check` over the 56 campaign maps; `xvfb-run -a -s "-screen 0 1280x1024x24" rc_game --script` (geo_01a) on a silent sound card.
- **Result:** All tests pass (3 new: where sounds come from, loudness with distance, reading Volume above 1). Self-test 106 checks passed, 0 failed; geo_01a reads settings for all 106 of its script sounds (94 radio, 12 placed) and placed 21 sounds in the run, e.g. `exp_GEN_tanker_01 ... from ScriptedTrigger30 at (1806, -6506, -2409), 8341 uu away, reach 500 uu, loudness 1.00` (an explosion that doesn't fade) and `D38X5054 ... 1135 uu away, reach 600 uu, loudness 0.53`. Over all maps: 1,404 radio, 58 at the player, 23 local, 464 placed.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not listened to, not tested on Windows. The distance rule (full to Radius, then Radius/distance) is our reading, not checked against the original. Tagged movers (dropships, lifts) stay where the map put them instead of moving. No pitch or volume variation from AudioParams yet (Min/MaxPitch, Min/MaxVolume). Enemy and squad sounds with 3D SOUND on are louder at a distance than before, since Bevy's extra fading is gone (not listened to).
- **Next:** The user listens on geo_01a (the explosion at the start, droids and squad lines); weapons and enemies could use the same AudioParams instead of their own distance rules.

## 2026-11-09: Ammo and grenade boxes in the levels (rc_level, rc_level_viewer, rc_level_plugin, rc_game_core, rc_combat, rc_game)

- **Changed:** New `rc_level::pickups` (reads every `Ammo` box in a map with its amount, ammo kind, touch cylinder and model, with the "...Defaults" class in `Properties/properties.u` on top; drops boxes onto the ground). `map_collision` leaves all pickups out of collision and returns `ammo_boxes`. `rc_level_viewer::scene` draws each box on its own (`MoverPart`), dropped the same way; `rc_level_check` and the viewer log the count. New `rc_game_core::pickups::LevelAmmoBoxes`, filled by `rc_level_plugin`. New `rc_combat/src/ammo_box.rs` (`AmmoBox`, `AmmoBoxTaken`, `[pickups]` save section) and data file `data/pickups/ammo_boxes.toml`. `rc_game --script` gets 5 new checks on geo_01a. `docs/DESIGN.md`: new "Ammo and grenade boxes" section.
- **Why:** Level audit blocker #2: the maps place about 477 boxes and none worked, so the player ran dry partway through a level and could never refill the sniper, anti-armour or grenades. They were also solid, like walls.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud, with the maps, script packages and pickup models copied read only from the user's PC: all 56 campaign maps read; `cargo test -p rc_level -p rc_combat -p rc_level_viewer -p rc_level_plugin -p rc_game_core` (9 new tests: amounts, full player, reload when empty, grenades, touch cylinders, falling onto the floor, and in a Bevy app: walking into a box, a full rifle leaving it until you step off and on, the crate coming back, the save list); `rc_level_check geo_01a geo_04d yyy_06d`; `rc_game --map geo_01a --frames 400`.
- **Result:** 477 boxes across the campaign (225 DC-17m, 64 anti-armour, 62 thermal, 50 EMP, 42 sniper, 17 sonic, 17 flash). geo_01a in the game: "22 ammo boxes placed: 21 x DC17mBlasterBox -> dc17m_blaster (300 each), 1 x ThermalDetonatorBox -> thermal_detonator (5 each)", 13 moved down onto the ground (furthest 19 uu); rc_level_check draws 22, 20 and 34 boxes on geo_01a, geo_04d and yyy_06d with no material failures. All tests pass (rc_combat range test: 24 of 24).
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing. The full `rc_game --script` route was NOT run: the user's PC went offline before the menu and level packages it needs could be copied, so its 5 new checks are untested. No pickup sound, no "picked up" message on screen, the squad doesn't take boxes, and weapons lying in maps (40 across the campaign) aren't pickups yet. Ten boxes sit 50 to 745 uu above the ground the port knows (on shelves or crates that aren't in the collision) and are left where the map puts them (our choice). The 300-shot DC-17m amount comes from `DC17mBlasterBoxDefaults`; the class itself says 120.
- **Next:** Run `rc_game --script` once the files can be copied again; then the pickup sound and message.

## 2026-11-09: Cutscene maps play to the end (rc_level, rc_scripts, rc_scripts_plugin, rc_game_core, rc_game)

- **Changed:** `rc_level::scripts` reads arrays of named-value structs (`Val::Structs`). `rc_scripts` runs `Dispatcher` actors as scripts of our own making (`dispatcher_actions`, `Stats::dispatchers`). New `rc_game_core::cutscene` (`Cutscene`, `HeldView`, `TeleportPlayer`) and `ActionState::hold_still`. New `rc_scripts_plugin/src/cutscene.rs`: `ACTION_Briefing` (hold, look limits, HUD), `ACTION_TeleportToPoint` and `ACTION_SetPhysics` on the player, `ACTION_ScreenFade`, `ACTION_AnimateVisorFX`, `ACTION_ShowVisor`. New `rc_game/src/cutscene.rs` (holding, HUD hiding, full-screen fade layer); `player.rs` (teleports, look limits, frozen body), `helmet.rs` (frame hidden), `hud.rs` and `original_hud.rs` (`HudRoot`). `docs/DESIGN.md`: new "Cutscenes" section.
- **Why:** Thread "Play the game's cutscenes". The original's cutscenes are in-engine scenes in the prologue, briefing and epilogue maps, timed by `Dispatcher` actors that nothing ran, so those maps got stuck.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_scripts -p rc_scripts_plugin -p rc_game_core` (new tests: a dispatcher sends its list in time and ignores its tag while running; briefing hold and limits; fades out and back; white flash; player teleport and physics; look limits wrap around). `rc_script_check pro geo_01briefing epilogue --secs 400` `rc_game --map pro --frames 3000`, and `rc_game --script` (new check: geo_01a's opening holds the player still), in the cloud with the game files copied read only from the PC.
- **Result:** rc_script_check: the prologue reaches `ChangeLevel` at 166 s, the Geonosis briefing at 53 s, the epilogue its credits at 75 s (before: stuck at the first dispatcher). rc_game on pro: the player is frozen and held in the nursery, then moved on cue to the school (t=18.95 s), gym (42.08), helmet room (59.38, 68.85) and battle (79.97), with white flashes and fades between; let go at 83.02 s; the game then loads geo_01briefing on its own, and geo_01a 53 s later, where the opening holds the player in the gunship for 11 s as in the original. `rc_game --script`: 107 checks passed, 0 failed ("moved 0 uu while walking; looks -103 to -4 deg", let go after 8.6 s).
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows, by playing or by looking. The player doesn't ride the gunship in briefings (stands at the map's start in the sky), the baby's and boy's hands aren't shown, many cutscene characters aren't in the game yet, hologram text isn't hidden or shown, no bloom, no view shake, no title card, no credits. The flash is a colour laid over the screen, not the original's add and multiply. The hold isn't kept in a quick save. The logo videos aren't played (MPEG-4 part 2, no Rust decoder).
- **Next:** Riding the gunship in the briefings (`ACTION_AttachToGunship`).

## 2026-11-09: Bump maps drawn on level props (rc_level_viewer)

- **Changed:** `materials.rs` finds each material's bump map (`NormalMap` on `HsBump...`, `Bumpmap` on `Shader`, with `BumpUVScale`) and decodes it next to the picture. `scene.rs` turns the light directions the map stores for bump-mapped props into per-corner light direction and colour (`bump_corners`). New `bump.rs` + `bump_lit.wesl`: our own small shader that lights each pixel by how its bump-map tilt faces that light. `draw::spawn_level` takes the bump materials (the game, `rc_walk` and the viewer pass them; `rc_droidview` passes `None`). `lighting.cfg` gained `bump_maps` and `bump_strength`. Viewer: `--view-bump`. New tool `rc_capture_compare` (compares two captures by colour per grid square and by detail). `rc_level_check` logs bump counts.
- **Why:** Bump maps were decoded but not drawn, so walls looked flat.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud with game files copied read only from the PC: measured which way the bump maps point (slope fit on every bump map, main-picture darkness for bumps vs dents; numbers in `docs/DESIGN.md` "Drawing bump maps"). `rc_level_check` on yyy_06a, geo_01a, ras_01a, geo_04a. `rc_level_viewer MAP --view-bump --capture` with `bump_maps = false`, `bump_strength = 0` and `bump_strength = 1` on yyy_06a, geo_01a and ras_01a, compared with `rc_capture_compare`. 4 new unit tests.
- **Result:** Bump-lit props drawn with a bump map: yyy_06a 193 of 193, geo_01a 59 of 59, ras_01a 61 of 61 (geo_04a has none). Strength 0 vs flat: identical on all three (biggest square difference 0.0, 100% of pixels within 3). Bumps on vs flat: square colours within 0.9-3.8 of 255, detail +20.5% (ras_01a), +178% (geo_01a rock wall), +38.6% (yyy_06a); all PASS. yyy_06a from the player start: colours within 1.6, detail +5.8%. Pictures: project files `bump-maps/`.
- **Still broken / not tested:** On the user's Windows PC (yyy_06a `--view-bump`): 193 of 193 drawn, 144 fps, on-screen check PASS, and the user said it looked good. Not tested on a phone, and not compared side by side with the original game. How the original's shader mixes the stored light is compiled code; our rule fits the stored numbers but is our own. BSP walls with bump materials (1-50 polygons per map) stay flat (no stored direction). Shine (specular) not drawn. Characters and weapons have no bump maps yet. The V8U8 bump format's orientation is mixed in the data.

## 2026-11-09: Walking no longer gets stuck at high frame rates (rc_movement, rc_walkcheck)

- **Changed:** `crates/rc_movement/src/lib.rs`: (1) after a hit, the rest of the move counts from where the box really stopped, so the 0.1 uu gap isn't lost from it; (2) that gap is measured straight out from the surface, not along the move; (3) walking up a floor-like slope keeps the move's direction across the ground and only adds the rise (it used to slide along the surface, which turned each move a little downhill); (4) the floor check looks from 0.1 above, so a box that crept closer than 0.1 to the floor is lifted back; (5) a step up goes forward at least 8 uu (`MIN_STEP_FORWARD`). New tool `crates/rc_nav/src/bin/rc_walkcheck.rs`. Three new tests in `crates/rc_movement/src/tests.rs`.
- **Why:** The user had trouble moving in some places, for example the first small ramp in Extreme Prejudice (geo_01a). Their log shows the game running at 240 frames a second. At 240 fps a frame's move is under 2 uu, and each of the problems above took a fixed bit off every frame or hit, so the player slowed to a stop. At 60 fps the same spots were fine.
- **Tested how:** `rc_walkcheck` on geo_01a, geo_01b, geo_01c, geo_03a, geo_03d, geo_04a, geo_04c with the game files copied read only from the PC, at 60 and 240 fps, before and after; and geo_01a at 30, 144, 500 and 1000 fps. `cargo test --release -p rc_movement` (25 tests). The 3 new tests fail on the old code and pass on the new.
- **Result:** Runs that never arrived at 240 fps, before -> after: geo_01a 104 -> 9, geo_01b 115 -> 38, geo_01c 59 -> 21, geo_03a 543 -> 81, geo_03d 125 -> 31, geo_04a 285 -> 97, geo_04c 104 -> 50. After the fix 240 fps gives the same results as 60 fps on every map (it was 2 to 6 times worse). geo_01a's first ramp near the start (PathNode228 -> PathNode54): stopped dead at 240 fps before, arrives in 1.1 s after. 60 fps also improved a little (geo_03d 58 -> 35).
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing. The remaining failures are the same at every frame rate; the ones looked at are paths for smaller characters (a low overhang on geo_01a PathNode71 -> 264, a crawl space at PathNode295) and were not changed. Spawning on geo_03d hovered and crept up 0.1 uu at a time in the user's log; not reproduced in the cloud (the floor there was 23 uu lower without the class packages), so not fixed. Squad mates and droids use the same movement, so they change too; their tests pass (rc_ai 33, rc_squad 43, rc_combat 32, rc_movers 13 and 21) but their sims were not run.
- **Next:** The user walks the first ramp in Extreme Prejudice. Then look at the remaining `rc_walkcheck` failures map by map.

## 2026-11-09: Marked-job voice lines said at all; marker-job tour in rc_squad_sim (rc_squad, rc_squad_plugin, data/squad/voice.toml)

- **Changed:** `rc_squad/src/voice.rs`: squad events name a job as the squad panel does ("Breach door", "Sniper position"), but the rules in voice.toml use the job's key (`job_assigned:breach`), so no marked-job rule ever matched. `Chatter` now turns the name into the key. A player (`who = "leader"`) rule now speaks at most once per frame: a breach gives all three squad mates a spot in the same frame, which would have made the player say "breach" three times at once. New rule setting `optional = "yes"`: if the speaker has no recording of the line in the game, they stay quiet and it isn't counted as missing (used for bacta's confirm and the grenade/anti-armour/sniper "ready" lines: Sev has no GrenadeReady or AntiArmorReady, Fixer and Scorch no AcknowledgeOrder, nobody a BactaConfirm). `rc_squad_sim`: new `--jobs` run, a tour that orders every job kind on the map in turn and checks the job, the player's line and an answer.
- **Why:** Thread "Test squad marker jobs and specialities": PR #60's job lines and PR #61's specialities had never been tried.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud with the game files copied read only from the PC (System .u, Properties, character_voice.uax, all campaign maps, StaticMeshes): `cargo test --release -p rc_squad -p rc_squad_plugin` (1 new test: a breach is asked for once and confirmed once); `rc_squad_sim MAP --jobs` on yyy_05d, yyy_06a, yyy_35c, yyy_01d, ras_04c, ras_02e; `rc_squad_sim geo_01a` (the full run, for the specialities).
- **Result:** With main's code the tour on ras_02e did 4 of 5 jobs but logged no job line at all (10 voice checks failed). After: rc_squad 50 tests, jobs_room and fight_room pass. With only the map's own collision (no StaticMeshes), yyy_05d passed 22 of 22: the player said BactaInitiate, HackDoorInitiate, BreachInitiate (once), DemolitionInitiate, SnipeInitiate, GrenadeInitiate, AntiArmorInitiate, and squad mates answered (for example "Fixer says HackDoorConfirm", "HackDoorProgress", "HackDoorComplete"; "Sev says BreachSetCharge", "BreachGo"; "Sev says SnipeReady"). Whenever a job was reached, both voice checks passed on every map; 0 lines missing. geo_01a full run: 39 of 39 (Sev 1 sniper shot of his own, Fixer 1 grenade of his own).
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by listening. With StaticMeshes staged, many tour jobs aren't reached in 45 s: squad mates get stuck or give up ("can't get there"), e.g. on yyy_05d all three stop at (-2046, -8834, 1104) where the path steps up 104 uu, and one stand spot was projected to z -9060. Doors and lifts aren't in the sim, which may explain some. On yyy_06a, aiming at a door's slice marker ordered the breach on the same door instead. Hacking a terminal uses the door-hacking lines (the game also has HackTerminal* lines). Scorch's anti-armour speciality is not reached in the geo_01a run (no armoured droids there).
- **Next:** The user plays a ghost-ship level (yyy_01b) and orders each kind of job; look at the squad's pathing to markers in its own thread.

## 2026-11-01: Squad mates use their specialities on their own (rc_squad, rc_squad_plugin)

- **Changed:** `rc_squad`: each `[member.*]` has a `speciality` (a `[job.*]` key) and each job an `auto` rule (`far`, `armoured`, `group`, `no`) with `auto_range`, `auto_interval`, `auto_min_enemies`, `auto_clearance`; `[fight]` gained `use_specialities`, `armoured_below`, `switch_time`. `fight.rs`: on every think Sev and Scorch pick their gun for the target (Sev's sniper sees 8000 uu; Scorch prefers armoured targets and never fires anti-armour with a friend near the target); Fixer throws a grenade on his own at bunched enemies (`throw_on_own`). `Threat` gained `blaster_share`, which the plugin works out from each enemy's damage table. New `Speciality` event; `Throw` gained `own`. The `fight:` log line counts speciality shots and grenades.

The old fight_room check "at least 15 hits for 3 droids" was lowered to 3, because Sev's sniper now kills a far droid in one hit; the same for rc_squad_sim's shot and hit counts.

When and at what the original uses specialities is compiled code; every rule and number here except the borrowed post numbers is our own guess. No ammo limit on specialities.
