## 2026-11-01: Squad mates take cover in fights (rc_squad, rc_squad_plugin)

- **Changed:** New `crates/rc_squad/src/cover.rs`: squad mates who have fought for their order's wait go to a free map cover point (the `CoverPoint` actors the droids use) within the order's reach, facing the enemy and with a clear shot from it, crouch there between bursts and stand up to fire, and leave when the enemies are gone, the player moves on, a new order comes, or after 15 s for another point. `nav.rs`: `Navigator::cover_near`, `CoverSpot`, `StraightWithCover`. `settings.rs`: new `[cover]` section and `seek_cover_after` / `cover_near_tether` per stance. `fight.rs`: crouch between bursts, see from standing height in cover. `rc_squad_plugin`: crouched squad mates are a smaller target (`Hittable`), play `CrouchBreathe` (`crouch` in `looks.toml`), covers counted in the `fight:` line, cover points counted in the `path network:` line. `rc_squad_sim`: a cover part with tough droids in front of the cover point nearest the player, and the stale "20 shots" check lowered to 15.
- **Why:** The user said the squad doesn't behave like the original and picked "take cover in fights" as the first gap: the squad stood in the open.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test --release -p rc_squad -p rc_squad_plugin` (5 new tests: take cover and crouch, leave when the fight is over, leave to follow the player, new order calls them out, switch off in settings). `rc_squad_sim` on geo_01a in the cloud with the game files copied read only from the PC.
- **Result:** All 39 rc_squad tests pass. rc_squad_sim: 36 checks passed, 0 failed. On geo_01a (13 cover points) Sev ran 953 uu to CoverPoint11, crouched between bursts, kept firing from it, and left 4 s after the droids were removed (`Sev leaves cover: no enemy in sight`). Read from the game for this: TimeBeforeSeekingCover (SquadStance 6, FormUp 2, SecurePosition 0, SearchAndDestroy 0), FavorCombatNearestTether, CoverPoint FOVdegrees 140, CTBot CoverBoredomTime 15. The "squad shots fired" check failed on main too (15 shots, want 20): 3 droids need 15 hits and the squad now hits with nearly every shot.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing, and not looked at. Only Sev found usable cover in the sim spot (the other points there face the other way). Which point they pick and when they leave are our own rules. They don't lean or step out to the side (the map stores `StrafeDistLeft/Right`, not used yet). Scorch still holds fire a lot when the player is in his line.
- **Next:** The user tries a fight on geo_01a. Further squad gaps (the original's voice lines, specialities) belong in their own threads.

## 2026-11-01: Kashyyyk tunnel (YYY_06A) no longer 3 times too dark (rc_level, rc_level_viewer)

- **Changed:** `crates/rc_level/src/actors.rs`: new `read_baked_light` reads the whole `StaticMeshInstance` (colours, baked light list, and a second per-corner list of light directions that bump-mapped props have); `read_vertex_colours` now uses it. `crates/rc_level_viewer/src/scene.rs`: new `prop_zone`: a prop whose position is inside solid (zone 0) takes the zone most of its corners face; bump-mapped props get the zone's whole ambient instead of half (`BUMP_PROP_AMBIENT`). Both logs count props whose zone came from their corners and bump-mapped props.
- **Why:** The user's original screenshot of YYY_06A's start tunnel was about 3 times brighter than the port. 245 of yyy_06a's 556 props (the tunnel walls, floors and ceilings) have their position inside the rock, which is zone 0 with no ambient light, so everything away from a lamp was black. Every real zone in the map has a ZoneInfo with ambient 17. With that fixed, the parts lit only by ambient were still half the original's brightness. Those props use bump-mapped materials and store a light direction per corner, so the game draws them with its bump shader; giving them the whole ambient matched.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud with the game files copied read only from the PC: `xvfb-run -a -s "-screen 0 1280x1024x24" rc_level_viewer yyy_06a --frames 150 --capture after.bmp`, and middle colours of six areas compared with the user's 1600x1200 screenshot (centre 4:3 of ours); `rc_level_check yyy_06a geo_04a`; `cargo test -p rc_package -p rc_level -p rc_level_viewer`. The light-direction reading was checked over 62,507 corners against the lights each prop lists.
- **Result:** Middle colour (0-255), original / before / after: near floor 27 / 0 / 29; middle floor 41 / 21 / 46; far tunnel 43 / 16 / 39; left wall 17 / 0 / 22; right wall 24 / 2 / 27; ceiling 28 / 0 / 27. geo_04a unchanged except 2 props now get its ambient (it has no bump-mapped props). Both maps load.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing. The whole-ambient rule for bump-mapped props comes from one screenshot. Bump maps themselves are still not drawn (surfaces look flatter than the original). Other maps with bump-mapped props or props inside solid are not compared with the original.
- **Next:** Draw the bump maps using the stored light directions; compare another Kashyyyk or ship level with an original screenshot.

## 2026-11-01: Falling rocks rest on the ground (rc_level, rc_level_viewer)

- **Changed:** New `crates/rc_level/src/settle.rs`: physics props (`KarmaProp`) whose Karma settings start them awake (`KStartEnabled`, and the map doesn't switch `Physics` off) are moved straight down onto whatever is under them when the level loads. `collision.rs`: new `add_meshes`. `map_collision.rs` and `scene.rs` both do it, so what you see and what you bump into match. Both logs count the props moved and the furthest move.
- **Why:** The user's GEO_04A screenshot: rocks floating in the air near the start. The map leaves geo_04a's `K_GEO4_FallingRock` rocks 40 to 125 units above the ground. Their physics is on from the start, so in the game they drop as the level begins. The port has no physics for them yet. The ground height itself is right: the terrain's own heightmap (`TerrainMap`) matches the points we draw to 0.1 unit.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud with the game files copied read only from the PC: `rc_level_check` on geo_01a, geo_03a, geo_04a, ras_01a, yyy_01b, yyy_06a; `cargo test -p rc_level` (new `rock_drops_onto_floor`); `cargo check --workspace --exclude rc_soundtest`.
- **Result:** geo_04a: 16 rocks moved down, the furthest 124 units. On the other 5 maps, nothing moved: their physics props start asleep. That includes ras_01a's 47 cans hung near the ceiling, which a first try wrongly dropped up to 500 units.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing. Not checked with a picture of the exact spot. Rocks don't turn to lie flat on a slope. geo_04a's other 17 rocks (`K_GEO4Rock`, physics switched off in the map) stay where the map puts them, 20 to 180 units up in places, which may also be how the original shows them until something hits them; not compared.
- **Next:** The user checks the rocks at the GEO_04A start.

## 2026-11-01: Levels have the right colours and ambient light (rc_level, rc_package, rc_level_viewer)

- **Changed:** `crates/rc_level/src/actors.rs`: props' stored light is read red, green, blue, alpha (was blue, green, red). `crates/rc_package/src/light.rs`: `hue_saturation_to_rgb` uses Unreal's hue model (straight fades between red, green and blue); new `brightness_curve`; `ZoneAmbient` keeps the stored `AmbientVector` and gains `rgb()`. `crates/rc_level_viewer/src/scene.rs`: new `zone_ambients` finds each zone's ambient by where its `ZoneInfo` stands, and half of it is added to every prop in that zone; terrain is lit by its zone's ambient plus the sun on the props' own scale instead of being scaled to the props' average (maps without a sun keep the props' average, flat). Both logs report zones with ambient and props it was added to.
- **Why:** The user's original-game screenshots of GEO_04A and YYY_06A showed the port 3 to 6 times darker, and grey-blue where the game is orange-brown. Measured: props lit only by geo_04a's orange sun store 75:62:46 in the order we read as blue, green, red; ras_01a and yyy_06a lights matched the same way, so the order was backwards. Corners facing away from every light store 0 (no ambient), while the lightmaps hold half the ambient.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud with the game files copied read only from the PC: `xvfb-run -a -s "-screen 0 1280x720x24" rc_level_viewer geo_04a --frames 60 --capture` and the same for yyy_06a, with average colours sampled in the same areas as in the user's screenshots; `rc_level_check` on geo_01a, geo_03a, geo_04a, geo_05a, ras_01a, ras_02a, yyy_01b, yyy_04a, yyy_06a, yyy_35a; `cargo test -p rc_package -p rc_level -p rc_level_viewer` (new `ambient_matches_stored_vector`).
- **Result:** geo_04a start, average colour on screen: ground 17,12,5 before, 118,84,54 after (original 110,82,56); canyon walls 23-29 grey before, 40-74 brown after (original 20-77). Every map checked loads (exit 0). `AmbientVector` matched our hue, saturation and brightness formula to 3 decimals on every zone of 6 maps.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing. YYY_06A's tunnel is still about 3 times too dark (floor 15,13,6 vs 40,38,31): its props are in zone 0 (no ambient) and store almost no light even next to lights, so the game must light them another way (likely at run time); not worked out yet. Terrain on maps without a sun is still the props' average.
- **Next:** Find how the game lights props like YYY_06A's tunnel walls.

## 2026-11-01: Sky domes are no longer drawn black (rc_level_viewer)

- **Changed:** `crates/rc_level_viewer/src/scene.rs`: a prop in the sky zone whose stored light is black on every corner is drawn at full brightness instead (new count `sky_props_unlit`, in the `rc_level_viewer` and `rc_level_check` sky lines).
- **Why:** The user's GEO_04A screenshot: black above the canyon where the sky should be, with only a sliver of yellow. The sky dome models store 0,0,0 light on every corner, so multiplying by it drew them black. geo_04a's sky zone has ambient brightness 255, which fits the game lighting them fully.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud with the game files copied from the PC: `rc_level_check geo_04a yyy_04a yyy_06a`; `xvfb-run -a -s "-screen 0 1280x720x24" rc_level_viewer geo_04a --pitch 45 --capture`.
- **Result:** Every sky dome checked was all black: geo_04a, yyy_04a, yyy_05a, yyy_06a (1 each). Looking up in geo_04a now shows an orange-yellow cloudy sky over the canyon (before: black).
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing. Full brightness is our reading of the sky zone's ambient 255; yyy_04a's sky zone sets no ambient, so it's a guess there.
- **Next:** Brightness of the rest of the level (waiting for original-game screenshots).

## 2026-11-01: Outdoor ground (terrain) is drawn and solid (rc_level, rc_level_viewer, rc_level_plugin)

- **Changed:** New `crates/rc_level/src/terrain.rs` reads `TerrainInfo` actors: the grid's points (stored already placed in the world after the properties), which squares are holes, which way each square is split, and the painted layers. `collision.rs`: `add_terrains`, used by `map_collision.rs` (so `rc_game` and `rc_walk` can stand on it; `MapCollision` gains `terrains`, `terrain_triangles`, `terrain_failures`). `scene.rs`: draws each terrain as one batch per painted layer (later layers see-through by their alpha map), with light from the sun and zone ambient scaled to match the map's props; checkerboard (`Chex`) decoration layers are left out. `draw.rs`: terrain layers blend by corner alpha and get a small depth bias. Logs: `rc_level_check`, `rc_level_viewer` and the game's level part report terrains, triangles, layers and the terrain's light next to the props'.
- **Why:** In the playtest, outdoor maps showed rocks floating over a black or yellow void (the sky box seen through the missing ground). The port didn't read terrain at all.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud with the game files copied read only from the PC: `rc_level_check geo_03a geo_04a yyy_01b yyy_35a`; `xvfb-run -a -s "-screen 0 1280x720x24" rc_level_viewer geo_04a --pitch -15 --capture`; `rc_walk --map geo_04a --frames 200`; `cargo test -p rc_level -p rc_level_viewer` (3 new terrain tests); `cargo check --workspace --exclude rc_soundtest`.
- **Result:** 10 terrains read on 4 maps, none failed. geo_04a: path points with a floor 0-150 units below went from 4 of 101 to 100 of 101; 40,196 terrain triangles; the player now lands on the ground at the start (`event Landed { z: 380.1 }`). Terrain light vs props (0-255): geo_04a 14,14,13 vs 10,14,17; yyy_01b 23,23,23 vs 23,23,22. The capture shows reddish gravel ground with rocks standing on it.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing. The terrain's light is our estimate, not the game's. Decorations scattered on terrain (bushes, palms) aren't placed. Leaving out `Chex` layers is a guess from their alpha map names, not compared with the real game. A first try used the texture's size for how often it repeats; the layers' stored matrix showed it repeats every `UScale` grid squares instead.
- **Next:** Brightness (levels are much darker than the original) and black skies.

## 2026-11-01: Solid walls and floors no longer have holes cut in them (rc_level_viewer)

- **Changed:** `crates/rc_level_viewer/src/materials.rs`: a picture now records two things: whether some pixels have low alpha (`alpha_pixels`), and whether the material says to use that alpha for see-through holes (`has_alpha`). Holes are cut only when the texture has `bMasked` or `bAlphaTexture`, a `FinalBlend` wrapper has `AlphaTest` or alpha blending (`FrameBufferBlending` 2), or a `Shader` has an `Opacity` map or masked output. `scene.rs`: BSP surfaces flagged "masked" (0x2) still cut holes. Both logs count see-through materials and materials whose alpha was ignored.
- **Why:** The user's playtest showed floors, walls and props missing on every level. Every prop's model was loading; the problem was that any texture with some low-alpha pixels was drawn with holes, but many Republic Commando textures store a shine (specular) mask in alpha, so whole surfaces vanished.
- **Tested how:** `rc_level_check` on 12 maps and `xvfb-run -a -s "-screen 0 1280x720x24" rc_level_viewer yyy_06a --capture` before and after, in the cloud with the game files copied read only from the PC; `cargo test -p rc_level_viewer`.
- **Result:** 1 to 30 materials per level were wrongly see-through (for example geo_03a 30, geo_03d 27, ras_01a 21, yyy_06a 20, geo_05a 17); they are now drawn solid. Kashyyyk leaves stay see-through (4 to 8 materials per Kashyyyk map). On yyy_06a's start, the floor in front of the player went from 94% black (average colour 1,1,1) to 33% black (15,20,16), and the metal grate floor shows.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing. Outdoor ground (terrain) isn't drawn at all on geo_03a, geo_04a, yyy_01b and yyy_35a. Levels are still darker than the original, and some skies show black.

## 2026-11-01: Drag the list's scroll bar; "EVERY MAP +" fits its button (rc_menus, rc_game)

- **Changed:** `rc_menus::lists::drag_to` and `handle`: a scroll bar's handle can be moved to any height and the list follows. `original_menu.rs`: each list's track gets a mouse target (`BarHit`, under the scroll arrows); new system `drag_bars`: click and hold the handle to drag it with the grip kept, or click the track elsewhere to bring the handle there and drag from there, as in a browser. `original_lists::follow_selection`, `drag`. `rc_menus::screens::fit_button_to_text`: this port's added buttons widen their background on whichever side their text runs past (only "EVERY MAP +" needs it; "PORT +" fits). `rc_game --script`: new `Pointer` step (the script holds a pretend mouse pointer) and 2 new checks.
- **Why:** The user: the campaign page's "EVERY MAP +" and "MISSIONS +" text ran out of its button, and they want to click and hold the scroll bar to move it, like in a browser.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_menus` (new `dragging_the_handle_scrolls_the_list`); `xvfb-run -a -s "-screen 0 1280x1024x24" target/debug/rc_game --script` in the cloud with the game files copied read-only from the PC.
- **Result:** Script: pressing on the handle at the top of the track and moving the pointer to the bottom scrolls EVERY MAP's 15 Geonosis maps to show the last 6 (`first shown 9 of 15`); "EVERY MAP +" text 0.387..0.613 inside its button 0.347..0.653. `SCRIPT DONE: 98 checks passed, 0 failed`.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested with a real mouse on Windows. Clicking the track jumps the handle to the pointer rather than paging one screen at a time.
- **Next:** The user tries the drag on the PC.

X (CYCLE VISOR MODE, our `Action::Flashlight`, the original's ToggleHeadlamp) steps through the original's three modes: normal, tactical and low light, and shows the original's "TACTICAL MODE" / "LOW LIGHT MODE" words on the HUD. ## 2026-10-30: GRAPHICS and SOUND option rows work (rc_game, rc_package, rc_audio)

- **Changed:** New `rc_game/src/graphics.rs`: BRIGHTNESS (exposure, one stop down to one stop up), BLUR (Bevy motion blur on the level view), BLOOM (Bevy bloom, wide colour range), SQUAD SHADOWS (the level's moving lights cast shadows), FSAA (MSAA 1, 2 or 4; 8 counts as 4), and GRAPHICS QUALITY presets that set those rows (CUSTOM after a hand change). TEXTURE QUALITY and CHARACTER DETAIL pick a smaller mipmap (`rc_package::texture::mip_for`, half or quarter size) from the next level load. New `rc_game/src/sound_options.rs` (replaces `music.rs`'s effects volume): VOICES VOLUME for sounds tagged `rc_game_core::Voice` (level script speech, enemy voice packages), 3D SOUND for sounds tagged `rc_game_core::SoundAt` (enemy sounds), AUDIO CHANNELS (the oldest effect stops when too many play; speech and music never do), SOUND QUALITY low (`rc_audio::for_quality`: 44 kHz sounds halved to 22 kHz, from the next level load). New settings in `settings.toml` `[video]` and `[audio]`. `data/ui/original_menus.toml` names them. Example `rc_game/examples/graphics_check.rs`.
- **Why:** User: "all of the setting functional and working".
- **Tested how:** `cargo test` on rc_game, rc_package, rc_audio, rc_combat, rc_enemies, rc_scripts_plugin, rc_game_core, rc_level_viewer, rc_character, rc_model. `xvfb-run -a -s "-screen 0 1280x1024x24" cargo run -p rc_game --example graphics_check` (a stand-in scene with the game's stack of four cameras; no game files).
- **Result:** All tests pass (rc_game 41). `GRAPHICS CHECK: 34 passed, 0 failed`: level brightness 158 -> 199 at brightness 1, 124 at 0; the same 199 with bloom on (applied once, not once per camera); shadows 158 -> 143; FSAA 8 asked for gave no graphics card errors (8 had failed on the cloud's card before being capped at 4); the display box drawn in every step.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested in a real level or on Windows (no game files in this cloud session). Blur not measured (no error, but its look not checked). Bloom barely shows on this game's dark levels at the threshold used. Not tested by ear: voices volume, 3D sound, channels, sound quality. The meaning of each row is ours where the original's engine differs (its presets, its 3D sound kinds). Still greyed: BUMPMAPPING (bump maps aren't drawn yet), PROJECTORS (no projected decals or blob shadows yet), EAX (Creative's Windows-only sound card effects). The original's item lists for these rows weren't seen in this session; the code reads their words (ON/OFF, LOW, 2X, numbers), not their order.

## 2026-10-31: Stuck-frame watchdog; choosing the graphics API (rc_game)

- **Changed:** `display::start_watchdog`: a thread logs "display: WARNING: no new frame for 5 s, the game is stuck" when frames stop after the first one, and "frames again" when they start. New `video.graphics_api` (`auto` default, `vulkan`, `dx12`, `gl`), passed to Bevy's `RenderPlugin` (`display::wgpu_settings`).
- **Why:** The user's game froze again with the 240 cap. This time the log stopped at 66 s with nothing after, so the game itself stopped. Card: NVIDIA RTX 4080 SUPER, Vulkan, driver 617.42, borderless 3440x1440, vsync off.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_game`; `rc_game` under Xvfb with `graphics_api = 'vulkan'`.
- **Result:** Tests 36 pass. Log: "display: graphics API vulkan (video.graphics_api)". The watchdog fired on llvmpipe's 11 s first frame before the "after the first frame" rule was added, then said "frames again", so it works; now quiet while starting.
- **Still broken / not tested:** The freeze's cause is still unknown. `dx12` not tested (no Windows here).
- **Next:** The user tries `graphics_api = 'dx12'`, then vsync on, to find which setting the freeze follows.

## 2026-10-30: Frame rate cap with vsync off; window events in the log (rc_game)

- **Changed:** New `video.max_fps` (default 240, 0 = no limit): with vsync off, `display::limit_frame_rate` waits at the end of each frame. `display::log_window_events` logs the graphics card, backend and driver once, and every focus, hidden/shown and resize of the window.
- **Why:** The user's picture froze in borderless full screen at 3440x1440 with vsync off while the game kept running (sound, music, menu). Their log: about 1,300 frames a second on the main menu, then a steady 60, no error, no display change. Cause unknown; the uncapped frame rate was the one unusual thing.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_game`; `rc_game` under Xvfb with `max_fps = 10`.
- **Result:** Tests 36 pass. With `max_fps = 10` the log reads "speed: 10 frames per second, average 100.1 ms". Log shows "display: graphics card llvmpipe ... (Cpu, Vulkan)" and the window events.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows. Not known whether this stops the freeze; the cloud's software renderer can't reach 240 frames a second, so the default cap itself is only checked at 10.
- **Next:** The user plays in borderless again; if it freezes, the new log lines show the card, driver and what the window did.

## 2026-10-30: Menus spread out on wide screens; windowed, borderless and true full screen (rc_menus, rc_game)

- **Changed:** New `rc_menus::fit`: on screens wider than 4:3 the original menus keep their size and spread out (groups of touching widgets move apart; the options boxes widen with names kept at the left and values at the right; tabs stay joined to their box; the main menu's MAIN MENU bar stretches to keep joining its button box; backdrops cover the screen), up to `[fit] widest_shape = 2.4` in `data/ui/original_menus.toml`. `original_menu::draw` spreads a copy of each page every time it draws (the drop-down list follows its value), and `screens::fit_row_labels` now runs only after that, so names are shrunk only on 4:3 and taller screens. New `rc_game/src/display.rs`: `video.display_mode` (`windowed`, `borderless`, `fullscreen`) replaces `video.fullscreen` (an old `fullscreen = true` loads as borderless); true full screen at another shape than the monitor's draws in a centred box with black bars (`PlayArea`, every camera's viewport, a black `BarsCamera`); the field of view, Panini and menus use the box's shape. FULL SCREEN removed from the VIDEO RESOLUTION list; PORT + has DISPLAY MODE (WINDOWED, BORDERLESS, FULL SCREEN). In borderless, picking a size of another shape than the monitor's switches to a window of that size. `menu::apply_video` moved to `display::apply_video`. `rc_probe --menu/--pages` also paints each page at 16:9, 21:9 and 32:9. Tests: 4 in `rc_menus::fit`, 2 in `display`, 2 in `option_settings`, 1 in `settings`.
- **Why:** The user (thread "Fit the UI to every screen shape"): fit the UI to every supported screen shape by moving things apart rather than shrinking them; undo FULL SCREEN in the resolution list; three display modes with these resolution rules. Phones only need landscape.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_menus -p rc_package -p rc_game`; `rc_probe --pages` and `--menu` paintings; `rc_game` under Xvfb with openbox (a window manager, needed for full screen) and the game files copied read only from the PC: menus at 16:9, 21:9, 19.5:9 (phone held sideways), 32:9 and 5:4; PORT + switching WINDOWED to BORDERLESS to FULL SCREEN; true full screen at 1920x1080 on a 3440x1440 screen in the main menu and in geo_01a.
- **Result:** Tests 36 + 15 + 19 pass. `rc_game --script` (1280x1024): `SCRIPT DONE: 73 checks passed, 0 failed`. Options pages at 16:9 and wider: "widened OptionsBorder, OptionsDescBorder", no label shrunk, no text running into a button (at 5:4 the old shrink still applies to 3 GAME labels and 1 GRAPHICS label). 32:9 stays in a centred 21.5:9 area. Full screen test: "display: drawing 2560x1440 at (440, 0) in a 3440x1440 window, black bars around it", and the screenshot of geo_01a shows the 3D view, arms and HUD in the middle with black sides, no error.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or a real monitor. Xvfb can't switch the monitor's size, so true full screen at a size of the monitor's shape (a real video mode change) is not tested. Borderless at a smaller size of the monitor's shape still draws at the monitor's size. The HUD is not spread by this (it is the HUD thread's). The campaign page's spreading (its own thread) is only seen in `rc_probe` paintings. The drop-down list's position on wide screens is not checked by eye.
- **Next:** The user tries the three display modes and the menus on the ultrawide.

- **Changed:** `menu::apply_video` marks every camera's `Projection` changed when the window size changes. `option_settings`: the screen size list starts with FULL SCREEN and then lists only window sizes that fit the primary monitor (read in `original_menu::focus_new_page`), plus the current size. `rc_menus::screens::fit_row_labels` shrinks any row label that would reach its row's first button; `original_menu::load` runs it on every page.

- **Result:** Before the fix the log ended with "Attachments have differing sizes: depth (1280,720) vs color (1280,960)" and the game quit; after it, no error and play goes on. Log: "labels shrunk to fit before their arrows: OptionLabels[6], OptionLabels[7], OptionLabels[8]" (GAME) and "OptionLabels[5]" (GRAPHICS); screenshots show no label touching an arrow. Tests 32 + 11 + 19 pass. Script `SCRIPT DONE: 71 checks passed, 0 failed`.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or a real graphics card; full screen switching only tested in the cloud's virtual screen. Text size not yet compared with the real game. Campaign screens, HUD and making every greyed setting work are in their own threads.

## 2026-10-30: The original options pages (rc_package, rc_menus, rc_game)

- **Changed:** The four PC options classes (GAME, SOUND, GRAPHICS, CONTROLS) are drawn from the install in place of the amber options list. `rc_package::class` reads string arrays and each property's fixed array size; `rc_menus::layout` draws choosers (`MenuButtonEnum`) and picture buttons (`MenuButtonSprite`, arrows mouse only), fills fixed arrays to full length, and copies sub-widgets before and after styles as `MenuBase` describes. `rc_menus::pages`/`screens`: new commands (`switch:`, `option_left/right`, `restore_defaults`, `rebind`, `prev_set`, `next_set`), `choosers`, `keys` and `extra_buttons` in `data/ui/original_menus.toml`, greyed rows, key sets, the REASSIGN BUTTON box (`CTMapKeyMenu`). New `rc_game/src/option_settings.rs` maps rows to settings. `original_menu.rs` steps choosers, saves settings, rebinds keys through `rebind.rs`. Values change only with the arrows (or left/right); the screen size's value opens a drop-down list built from the page's own pieces (`screens::drop_down`). Our extra settings (FOV, Panini, full screen, master volume, controller look, gyro) are on a fifth tab, "PORT +", drawn with the original GAME page's look (`[pages.more_settings]` with new `texts`, `moves` and `hidden_rows`). Screen size, full screen and vsync now apply live (`menu::apply_video`). The effects volume now scales every non-music sound (`music.rs`). `rc_game --script`: 9 new checks.

- **Result:** Tests 28 + 11 + 18 pass. `rc_probe --pages`: options, options_sound, options_graphics, options_controls all "USES THE ORIGINAL". Script `SCRIPT DONE: 61 checks passed, 0 failed`; GAME page matches the `rc_probe` painting (mean difference 3.3 of 255), CONTROLS 4.2; music volume 1.0 -> 0.9 with the left arrow; JUMP rebound to K then cancelled back to SPACE. Main, pause and quit pictures byte-identical to before.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows, with a mouse on the arrows, or with a controller. Not compared with the real game by eye (a few long labels, such as TACTICAL MODE INTENSITY, touch the left arrow; unknown whether the original does too). Greyed out until built: subtitles, helmet, prompts, auto pull, visor, sound quality/3D/EAX/channels, graphics presets, brightness, detail levels, blur, bloom, projectors, shadows, FSAA, and key rows for actions the port lacks. No "keep these settings?" timer after a screen change. Campaign, load and save pages are still placeholders.

## 2026-10-30: The DC-17m's ammo counter shows the real ammo (rc_combat)

- **Changed:** New `crates/rc_combat/src/counter.rs` (plain rules, 5 tests): what the little screen on the gun shows and how far each piece's picture slides. `arms_view.rs` finds the counter pieces by material name, slides their pictures (`uv_transform`) when the ammo changes, and reads them back into `ArmsReadout::counter`. New `counter_digits` and `counter_bar` in `[arms]` of `data/weapons/dc17m_blaster.toml` and `dc17m_sniper.toml`. `rc_shoot --script` has 2 new checks. Docs: DESIGN (arms section), README.
- **Why:** The gun's screen always read 999: its `TexPanner2D` pieces were drawn unslid.
- **Tested how:** Read (never wrote) the game's `hudarms.ukx`, `HUDArmsTextures.utx`, `ctinventory.u` and `Engine.dll` from the PC: the ammo class names the pieces, the picture has a strip of 11 cells (blank, 0-9), and the engine slides each digit by `digit/11 + 2/11` and the bar by `-0.5 * (1 - magazine fill)`; the digits show all ammo carried. `cargo test -p rc_combat`, `cargo check --release --workspace --all-targets`, and `xvfb-run -a -s "-screen 0 1280x1024x24" target/release/rc_shoot --script` in the cloud with the game files copied read only.
- **Result:** `script done: 19 of 19 checks passed`, including `the gun's ammo counter shows 110, bar 83% full` after 10 shots and `shows 110, bar 100% full` after the reload. Log: `arms: dc17m_blaster: ammo counter on the gun (4 pieces) shows 120, bar 100% full`. I looked at the saved picture: the screen reads "110" and the magazine window shows its blue ticks.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing. The sniper's counter uses the same rule but no check covers it (not tested). Not done: the anti-armour's grenade digit (`PannerClip`) and the DC-15s pistol's charge bar. The game shows leading zeros ("060"); we do the same.
- **Next:** The user fires the DC-17m in `rc_shoot` or `rc_game` and checks the screen counts down.

## 2026-10-30: Auto pull maneuvers (rc_squad, rc_squad_plugin, rc_game)

- **Changed:** `rc_squad`: jobs gain `pullable` (sniper, grenade and anti-armour posts in `data/squad/squad.toml`); `Squad::auto_pull` (on by default) and `Member::marker_since`; a marker order that needs more squad mates than are free takes the missing ones off pullable posts, longest-held first (`Squad::pull_for`, new `SquadEvent::MarkerPulled`). Off, the order is refused with "recall the squad first". `rc_squad_plugin::SquadOptions` carries the setting; `rc_game` reads `auto_pull_maneuvers` from `[game]` in `settings.toml` and passes it on at start and whenever settings change. Docs: RESEARCH ("Auto pull maneuvers"), DESIGN (Context orders), README. 4 new tests.
- **Why:** The GAME options page has AUTO PULL MANEUVERS greyed out because nobody knew what it did. Thread "Make auto pull maneuvers work".
- **Tested how:** Read the game's files from the PC, read only: `User.ini` (`bAutoPullManeuvers=True`), Engine.u and XInterfaceCTMenus.u script text, class defaults with `rc_probe --classes`, and the exported function names and machine code of `ASquadMarker::GetMarkerUsability` and `FillParticipants` in Engine.dll (nothing copied). Then `cargo test -p rc_squad -p rc_squad_plugin -p rc_game` in the cloud.
- **Result:** The setting lets a new maneuver take squad mates off sniper, grenade, anti-armour (and turret) markers, the longest-running first; off, the helmet says "RECALL SQUAD BEFORE ENGAGING MANEUVER". Tests: rc_squad 34 passed (new: pulls the longest-held post, off refuses until recalled, once jobs are never pulled, shipped posts are pullable).
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing; not tested in a real level. The GAME options row is still greyed: the options rows live in draft PR #47, which hooks it up once both are in. The helmet's refusal text isn't shown on screen yet (only the log says it). Turret markers aren't a port job yet, so they can't be pulled.
- **Next:** Turn on the AUTO PULL MANEUVERS row in the options code (#47's `option_settings.rs`, reading and writing `game.auto_pull_maneuvers`).

## 2026-10-30: Smooth blending between animations (rc_anim, squad and enemies)

- **Changed:** `crates/rc_anim/src/lib.rs`: new `blend_poses` and `Crossfade` (remembers the pose on screen when the animation changes and mixes it into the new one with an S curve). `rc_model::Model` and `rc_enemies::look::DroidLook` gained `local_pose` and `posed_local`, so a mixed pose can be skinned. Squad mates (`rc_squad_plugin/src/look.rs`) and enemies (`rc_enemies/src/lib.rs`) now blend whenever their animation changes. Blend times are data: `[blend]` in `data/squad/looks.toml` and `blend_time`, `blend_time_fire`, `blend_time_death` in `[look]` of `data/enemies/battle_droid.toml` (other enemies inherit them). New check `crates/rc_model/examples/rc_blend_check.rs`.
- **Why:** Characters snapped from one pose to the next when they started or stopped moving, fired or fell.
- **Tested how:** Looked for the original's blend times in `Engine.u`, `CTGame.u` and `CTCharacters.u` (read only): `PlayAnim`/`LoopAnim` take no blend time and no script passes one, and the sequences' extra numbers don't look like times, so the blending is native code and our times are guesses. `cargo test -p rc_anim` (new `crossfade_moves_smoothly`), tests of rc_ai, rc_model, rc_enemies, rc_squad_plugin; `cargo run --release -p rc_model --example rc_blend_check -- "<game>"` and `rc_game --script` under Xvfb in the cloud with the game files copied read only from the PC.
- **Result:** `BLEND CHECK: 18 passed, 0 failed`. Largest one-frame jump at the switch, snapped then blended: commando walk to run 130 to 25 uu, run to idle 111 to 30, walk to step left 103 to 27, idle to falling down 58 to 34; droid walk to run 129 to 26, idle to crouch 50 to 12. Stretch while mixing equals the animations' own (commando 0.061, droid 0.005). First try with 0.15 s into falling down: 58 to 44 uu, faster than the fall itself, so it is 0.25 s now. `rc_game --script`: `SCRIPT DONE: 46 checks passed, 0 failed`.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not looked at on screen and not run on Windows. Times are guesses. Only squad mates and enemies blend; the `rc_character` viewer does not. Only whole-body blends: no separate upper body (for example firing while running uses one or the other).
- **Next:** The user watches the squad and droids in `rc_game` and says if any change looks too slow or too quick; the times are in the two data files.

## 2026-10-30: Level scripts direct characters (rc_scripts, rc_scripts_plugin, rc_ai, rc_enemies, rc_combat)

- **Changed:** `rc_scripts`: `Effect::Action` carries the sequence's tag (`owner`) and whether the script waits; with `Director::game_runs_characters` set, walks, turns, animations and `WaitForPlayer` hold the script until the game calls `action_done` (timeouts `move_timeout_seconds`, `anim_timeout_seconds`, `turn_timeout_seconds` and `character_grace_seconds` in `data/scripts/scripts.toml`); saves keep the action being waited on and give it again after a load. `rc_level::scripts::places` lists where scripts can send characters. `rc_game_core`: new `ScriptTags` component and `CharacterOrder` / `CharacterOrderDone` / `SquadStanceOrder` messages. New `rc_scripts_plugin/src/characters.rs` finds each action's characters (by tag, or the characters running the sequence) and handles `MoveToPoint`, `MoveToPlayer`, `TurnTowardPlayer`, `TeleportToPoint`, `PlayAnim`, `WaitForAnimEnd`, `WaitForPlayer`, `MakeInvulnerable`, `SetCannotDie`, `HurtActor`, `DestroyActor`/`DestroyPawn`, `SetHidden` and `ChangeSquadStance`. `rc_ai`: spawners pass `AIScriptTag` / `AIScriptFromFactory` to what they make; `Fighter::follow_script` with `ScriptGoal`, `teleport`, `AiEvent::ScriptDone` and `Leapt`, `Battle::following` and `remove`; new `[combat] leap_speed` (Geonosian 900: leaps to a point it can't walk to, or one over 200 uu above it). New `rc_enemies/src/scripted.rs` carries the orders out and plays scripted animations by name. `rc_combat`: `Invulnerable` and `CannotDie` components. `rc_squad_plugin` takes `SquadStanceOrder`s. `rc_script_check` counts the places scripts send characters to. `rc_game --script` has 2 new checks (the lone Geonosian in geo_01a's trench). Tests: 2 in `rc_scripts`, 2 in `rc_ai`.
- **Why:** Thread "Run script actions on characters": after #35 scripts started spawners, but every action on a character was "not done yet".
- **Tested how:** `cargo test --workspace` (all pass), `rc_script_check` over the 54 maps, and `xvfb-run -a -s "-screen 0 1280x1024x24" target/debug/rc_game --script` in the cloud sandbox with the game files copied read-only from the PC. The self-test sends `SpawnTrench02` and `LoneRangerJump` itself.
- **Result:** `SCRIPT DONE: 54 checks passed, 0 failed`; 60 of 60 after merging main (sounds, level changes, arms), with the sound files staged and a null sound card (`~/.asoundrc` `pcm.!default { type null }`). Log: `droid 11 (ScriptedSequence21 #1) runs to (3635, -2786, -1832)`, `leapt to the script's point`, `turns to the player`, `plays EmoteSeeEnemy (1.50 s)`, the second walk ends 8 uu from `KillPointForLoneRanger`, then `removed by a script`; each step is answered back to the script. 846 places on geo_01a; over all maps 287 of the 294 destinations scripts name are found. The first run, before leaping, had him stuck at the foot of the ledge (the original flies up).
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing; not looked at by eye. Leaping is a straight-line stand-in for flying (it can pass through things in its way), and the 200 uu, arc and speed are our guesses. Not done: squad mates' own scripted moves (`ChangeSquadMember`), moving the player, `SetTeamIndex`, `SetThreat`, `SetSightRadius`, `ShootTarget`, `GotoState` on characters, placed characters the game doesn't spawn yet (logged and skipped: 20 such calls in the geo_01a run), one sequence run separately per character, `ScriptPriority`.
- **Next:** Squad mates' scripted moves, and the rest of the character actions above.

## 2026-10-30: Faster collision, walk-through props, and a speed readout (rc_movement, rc_level, rc_package, rc_game)

- **Changed:** `rc_movement::triangles`: a sweep stops at once when its move is nowhere near the world's triangles, visits only the grid columns the move passes over, skips triangles whose box is outside the move's box, marks seen triangles with a per-thread stamp list instead of a new hash set, and builds each triangle's planes in a fixed array instead of a new list; new `take_counts` (sweeps, triangles looked at, time). `rc_package::static_mesh`: `section_collision` from each section's `EnableCollision`, `triangle_collides`. `rc_level::collision` leaves out triangles of sections with collision off; `rc_level::class_look::blocks_players` reads `bCollideActors` and `bBlockPlayers` (map, then class defaults); `map_collision` leaves out props that don't block and counts them (`not_solid_props`). `rc_level_plugin` logs that count. `rc_game` logs a `speed:` line every 5 s (frames per second, average and slowest frame, entities, collision work per frame). Tests: `grid_search_matches_trying_every_triangle` (3,000 random moves against trying every triangle), `section_collision_by_triangle`. Docs: DESIGN (Collision), README.
- **Why:** The user played geo_01b on main and saw bumpy, wrong collisions, missing models and very bad speed, and didn't know if it was new. Thread "Fix collisions, missing models and slowdown".
- **Tested how:** In the cloud with 263 game files copied read-only from the PC (Animations, Properties, StaticMeshes, Textures, System, geo_01a and geo_01b): `target/release/rc_game --map geo_01b --frames 200` under Xvfb before and after, a `perf` profile, `cargo test --workspace`, and `rc_game --script`.
- **Result:** Neither problem is new: every prop has been solid since the first walking build, and, from reading the code, the slow sweeps date from doors (#26) and switch-off-able parts (#27), each its own triangle world that every sweep looked into (not measured on those older builds). geo_01b standing still, before: `collision per frame: 1033 sweeps, 532686 triangles looked at, 31.8 ms`; after: `785 sweeps, 241 triangles looked at, 0.5 ms`. Load line after: `190205 collision triangles, 404 solid props (... 32 you can walk through left out)` (227,043 before); drawing unchanged at 254,283 triangles. During the first Geonosian fight (the user's report: speed drops as each fight starts and recovers after the kill), collision took about 90 ms per frame before and about 2.5 ms after. `rc_game --script`: `SCRIPT DONE: 52 checks passed, 0 failed`. Workspace tests all pass. Missing models: the counts say every prop in geo_01b loads and is drawn (436 drawn, 8 hidden by the map or scripts, 0 failing); what isn't drawn are kinds the port doesn't draw yet (animated props like bacta dispensers, particle effects such as fog, steam, sparks and fire, and 29 decals).
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or with a real graphics card: in the cloud the picture is drawn in software at 5 frames per second, so how fast the GPU side is on the PC is not measured here; the new `speed:` line will show it. Simplified collision models aren't read. Not tested by playing through geo_01b.
- **Next:** The user plays geo_01b and the log's `speed:` lines show whether drawing is now the slow part.

## 2026-10-30: Field of view setting works again on wide screens; unknown settings are warnings (rc_game)

- **Changed:** `crates/rc_game/src/player.rs`: the limit from the entry below is now a screen shape, `video.widest_shape` (2.0 by default), instead of a fixed `widest_fov` of 110 degrees. Screens longer than 2:1 see what a 2:1 screen sees along the long side. `settings.rs`: options the game doesn't know are skipped and named in a `WARNING:` in the log (`Settings::unknown_options`) instead of stopping the game; `deny_unknown_fields` removed here and from `rc_pads` `GyroSettings`. A wrong value still stops it. Tests `long_screens_stop_widening`, `every_screen_shape` (new numbers), `missing_options_use_defaults_and_typos_are_warnings`, `file_from_the_first_fish_eye_fix_still_loads`.
- **Why:** The user: "the fish eye effect is gone but i cant adjust my FOV anymore". Their log on a maximized 3440x1369 ultrawide showed every setting from 95 to 120 as `110.0 degrees across, 59.2 up and down (..., held at the widest)`. Also, the options menu had saved `widest_fov` into their `settings.toml`, so every other branch refused to start with "unknown field widest_fov" (reported by the collisions/performance thread).
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_game --bins` and `cargo test -p rc_pads --lib` in the cloud sandbox; parsed a copy of the user's own `settings.toml`.
- **Result:** 28 + 13 pass. On the ultrawide: fov 60, 85, 95, 100, 110, 120 now give six different views (85 is 107.9 x 57.4; 95 is 117.2 x 66.2; 120 is 137.9 x 91.9). The user's file loads with `fov 120` and one warning for `video.widest_fov`.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested in the game on the PC yet. Other branches still stop on `widest_fov` until this is merged into main and they take main; until then, deleting the `widest_fov` line from `settings.toml` fixes them. High settings (above about 100) on an ultrawide still look fish-eyed, because that is what the setting asks for.
- **Next:** The user tries the FOV setting maximized on the ultrawide.

## 2026-10-30: No more fish-eye on wide windows; tall phones see more (rc_game)

- **Changed:** `crates/rc_game/src/player.rs`: new `view_angle`. The screen's shorter side keeps the original's 69 degrees (for `fov = 85`), the longer side widens to fit, and it stops at the new setting `video.widest_fov` (110 by default, 0 for no limit, off while `panini` is on). Upright phones now get Hor+ turned on its side (more above and below) instead of 42 degrees across. `FieldOfView` now holds both settings. The `player: view ...` log line also gives the setting, the limit and `held at the widest`. `settings.rs`: the new option and its note in the default file. Tests `widest_view_stops_the_fish_eye` and `every_screen_shape` (14 shapes). Docs: `docs/DISPLAY_AND_INPUT.md` table, `docs/DESIGN.md`, `README.md`.
- **Why:** The user saw a strong fish-eye look with the play-test window maximized. Their `settings.toml` has `fov = 95` (set in the options menu), which is 111 degrees across at 16:9 and 125 or more on an ultrawide; a flat 3D view stretches the edges badly that wide. The camera code itself was doing what it was meant to (log: `player: view 1280x720 (aspect 1.778): 111.0 degrees across, 78.6 up and down`). The user also asked for every major screen shape: TVs, monitors, phones, flip phones.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_game --bins` in the cloud sandbox.
- **Result:** 27 of 27 pass. Table in `docs/DISPLAY_AND_INPUT.md`, e.g. 16:9 101.4 x 69.0 (unchanged), 3440x1440 110.0 x 61.7 (was 125+ x 69), upright phone 9:16 69.0 x 101.4 (was 42.3 x 69).
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested in the game on the PC or by eye; the cloud has no game files for the script run. 32:9 now shows only 43.8 up and down (the price of the limit; `widest_fov = 0` plus the Panini view keeps the full height). Phone notches and rounded corners (safe areas) are not handled.
- **Next:** The user maximizes the play test and the log shows `held at the widest`; then decide whether very wide screens should switch the Panini view on by themselves.

## 2026-10-30: First-person arms and weapon animations (rc_combat, rc_model)

- **Changed:** New `rc_combat/src/arms.rs` (plain rules: which arms animation plays, from the game's `Engine.Weapon` script; 8 tests) and `arms_view.rs` (reads each carried weapon's arms model with `rc_model`, poses it every frame, draws it with its own camera on render layer 2 at camera order 1; public `ArmsReadout`, `ArmsCamera`). New `[arms]` section in weapon files (`mesh`, `package`, `fov`, `attachment_group`, `hide_zoomed`, `brightness`), filled in for the DC-17m, sniper, anti-armour, DC-15s and Trandoshan repeater. New message `MeleeSwing` (sent on every player melee swing). `view.rs` shows the plain static model only for weapons without arms. `rc_model`: `Model::posed_from_origin`, and follows `TexPanner2D` materials. `rc_game`: menu/display camera moved from order 1 to 2, and a new self-test check. Reload times from the arms' `Reload` animations replace stand-ins: sniper 2.0 -> 2.6 s, anti-armour 1.5 -> 1.4 s, Trandoshan repeater 1.5 -> 2.17 s. `rc_shoot --script` has 5 new checks. New example `rc_model --example rc_arms_probe`.
- **Why:** The player had no first-person arms and no reload, switch or fire animations.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud sandbox with game files copied read-only from the PC. `rc_probe --characters` on hudarms.ukx; `rc_probe --class` on the weapon classes (Mesh, DisplayFOV, bZoomedUsesNoHUDArms); `rc_arms_probe` for where each animation puts the arms in view; `cargo test -p rc_combat`; `xvfb-run ... target/debug/rc_shoot --script`; `xvfb-run ... target/debug/rc_game --script` on geo_01a.
- **Result:** hudarms.ukx: 39 meshes and 25 animation sets, all read, every material of the 5 weapons textured. `rc_shoot`: `script done: 17 of 17 checks passed`, log shows `Load`, `Fire` per shot, `Reload`, `Holster` then the pistol's `Load`, and the gun corner has 827 colours, none magenta. `rc_game`: `SCRIPT DONE: 53 checks passed, 0 failed`, including `the first-person arms hold the weapon in hand ... playing ActionBreathe -> PASS` (with `Properties/*.u` staged; without them the 2 door checks fail, unrelated); walking shows `Walk` at 154 uu/s then `Run` at 395 uu/s, and `Idle` after standing still.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing. No blending between animations; arms not lit by the level (fixed brightness 0.75); the DC-17m's ammo counter shows 999 (the digit strip isn't scrolled); no muzzle flash; switching still lets you fire before the new weapon is up. The picked-up E-5 has no arms in the game files we found, so it keeps the plain model. Turret, bacta and other pick-up arms (Bowcaster, Elite Beam, etc.) aren't wired up because those weapons don't exist in our game yet.
- **Next:** Make the DC-17m's ammo counter show the real count; light the arms by the level; blend between animations once PR #34's crossfade is in.

## 2026-10-30: Level scripts play their lines of dialogue and sounds (rc_level, rc_scripts, rc_scripts_plugin, rc_combat)

- **Changed:** `rc_level::scripts` keeps which package holds each sound a map names (`MapScripts::sounds`, from the import table). `rc_scripts`: `Director::set_sound_length` / `sound_length`, so `PlaySound` with `bWaitForFinish` waits the line's real length (the old `sound_seconds` is only a fallback); switched-off (`bDisabled`) lines aren't waited for; `sound_action`; `[sound] volume` in `data/scripts/scripts.toml`. `rc_scripts_plugin`: new `sounds.rs` reads every sound the map's scripts can play while the level loads (`ScriptSounds`), measures each, and plays `PlaySound` / `PlayLocalSound` (`ScriptSound` entities); lines pause with the game and stop when the level ends. `rc_combat::read_sounds` now also reads group members kept in another package (geo_sound's `arena_cloneDeaths` takes are in character_voice). `rc_script_check` reports whether each script sound is in its package. New tests: 1 in `rc_scripts`, 2 in `rc_scripts_plugin`; 2 new `rc_game --script` steps.
- **Why:** Scripts' dialogue and sound actions were logged as "not done yet", and every waited-for line took a flat 3 s.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud with the PC's game files copied read-only. `rc_script_check` over 57 campaign maps; `cargo test -p rc_scripts -p rc_scripts_plugin`; `RC_GAME_DIR=<copy> cargo test -p rc_combat -p rc_enemies`; `xvfb-run -a -s "-screen 0 1280x1024x24" target/debug/rc_game --script` with a silent (null) ALSA sound card.
- **Result:** `1629 of 1629 script sounds found` (1,586 single sounds, 39 groups, 4 streamed music stingers). geo_01a: `106 script sounds read (304 s of sound) ... 0 missing, 569 ms`; the intro logs `ScriptedTrigger52 #13: ScriptedTrigger52 says CA1G1701 (2.0 s, volume 1.00)` then `sound CA1G1701 ... started on the sound card`; later `says CA1G1545 (2.8 s ...); stops CA1G1541`. `SCRIPT DONE: 53 checks passed, 0 failed`; after merging main (level changes) 57 of 57.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not listened to, and not run on Windows. Our choices: one line of speech per speaker at a time, effects never cut each other, a group waits for its longest take. No subtitles (the game has them in `System/subtitles_*.int`), sounds aren't placed at the speaker (all play at full loudness), sounds stored inside a map aren't read (none found in the campaign scripts), streamed stingers play once and their length isn't measured. `PlayMusic` and `SetImuseState` (music cues) are still "not done yet".
- **Next:** Subtitles from the game's own subtitle files, then placing sounds at the speaker.

## 2026-10-30: Trandoshan mercenaries are visible, with their own model, skin and animations (rc_enemies, rc_ai)

- **Changed:** `data/enemies/trandoshan.toml` `[look]` now names the game's own mesh, `TrandoshanMerc` in `GameData/Animations/clone.ukx`, and its skin `CloneTextures.TrandoshanMercTextures.TrandoshanMerc_Shader`; `throw_time` is 2.17 s (the mercenary's `ThrowGrenade`). `rc_enemies::look::DroidLook::load` finds each animation in every animation set the mesh links to (first match in the mesh's order), not only the first set. New `[look] skin` setting (`rc_ai::params::Look::skin`) wears a named material instead of the mesh's own, as the original's `Skins[0]` does. New example program `rc_enemy_look_check`. Notes in `docs/RESEARCH.md` and `docs/DESIGN.md`.
- **Why:** Trandoshans were invisible: the file asked for a mesh called `Trandoshan` in trandoshan.ukx, which doesn't exist, so the model failed to load. `rc_probe --class TrandoshanMercenary` shows `Mesh = TrandoshanMerc`, an import from the `Clone` package: the mercenary is built on the commandos' skeleton. Its own animation sets hold only cutscenes; its everyday moves are in `CloneCommandoSet`, the sixth set it links to. Its mesh's material is a grey stand-in (`TrandoshanMercProxy`); `TrandoshanMercenaryDefaults` swaps in the real skin.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud with files copied read-only from the PC. `RC_GAME_DIR=<copy> cargo run --release -p rc_enemies --example rc_enemy_look_check`; `cargo test --release -p rc_ai -p rc_enemies`; and `rc_droidview geo_01a --check` with a test copy of `data/` in which Trandoshans take the battle droids' place (geo_01a has no Trandoshans).
- **Result:** Look check `12 passed, 0 failed`; the Trandoshan: 65 bones, 1500 points, 11 of 11 named animations found (from CloneCommandoSet), skin 1024x1024 average colour R 135 G 127 B 114, standing 167 uu tall (collision height 168). rc_droidview: 5 Trandoshans drawn, 2177 pixels, average colour R 103 G 93 B 84, 0 magenta, all checks passed, 3 grenades thrown. All rc_ai (31) and rc_enemies tests pass. Battle droids load as before.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows, or on a real Trandoshan level (Kashyyyk), or in `rc_game`. The mercenary carries no gun model in its hands. The look check lists animations the Geonosian (no WalkBackward, StepLeft, StepRight) and super battle droid (no RunForward) don't have; those poses play the idle one, as before. The slaver, heavy and scavenger Trandoshans (in trandoshan.ukx) have no enemy files yet. The burnt skin (`TrandoshanMerc_Burn`) isn't used.
- **Next:** Weapons in enemies' hands, or the other Trandoshan types.

## 2026-10-30: Finishing a level goes on to the next one (rc_scripts, rc_save, rc_level_plugin)

- **Changed:** `rc_scripts`: the director reads every `Teleporter` (URL, tag, size, `bEnabled`, `bCollideActors`); walking into an open one with a URL, `ACTION_ChangeLevel`, or `ACTION_ShowDebriefingScreen` with a `NewLevel` gives the new `Effect::ChangeLevel` once, then the map's scripts stop. `ACTION_SetCollision` opens and closes exits by tag; an event with an exit's tag switches it on or off. Actions marked `bDisabled` in the map are skipped. Exits are in the `[scripts]` save section. New `level_url`, `[exits]` in `data/scripts/scripts.toml` (engine.u's Teleporter size 80 x 100). `rc_game_core`: `ChangeLevel` message and `ArrivalPortal`. `rc_scripts_plugin` sends `ChangeLevel`. `rc_save` carries the sections listed in the new `data/levels/travel.toml` (`combat`) to the next map and puts them back on its first frame, before the level-start checkpoint. `rc_level::map_collision` lists teleporters as arrival points; `rc_level_plugin` starts the player at the one the address names. `rc_script_check` lists each map's exits and follows the campaign. `rc_game --script`: 4 new checks.
- **Why:** Thread "Move on to the next level": finishing a level should load the next one, keep the player's state and save a checkpoint.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_scripts -p rc_save -p rc_game_core -p rc_level -p rc_scripts_plugin -p rc_level_plugin` (6 new tests); `rc_script_check` on all 57 campaign maps; `rc_probe --class Teleporter`; `xvfb-run -a -s "-screen 0 1280x1024x24" target/debug/rc_game --script`, all with game files copied read-only from the PC to the cloud sandbox.
- **Result:** `campaign: 56 maps from pro to epilogue`, `0 exits lead to a map that isn't there`. Self-test `SCRIPT DONE: 56 checks passed, 0 failed`: `Teleporter0: the level is over; on to geo_01b`, `[combat] carried from geo_01a to geo_01b`, health 187 -> 187 (of 200), magazine 57 -> 57, feet 0 uu from geo_01b's start, checkpoint on geo_01b with health 187.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing to an exit by hand. Not tested past geo_01b (other maps' exits only checked from the files). The squad isn't carried (they arrive fresh, up and at full health); not sure the original does the same. The debriefing screen isn't shown. Skipping `bDisabled` actions is our reading of the name. Briefing and cutscene maps may wait for events nothing sends yet.
- **Next:** Show the original debriefing screen (`CTDebriefingInfo*`), and check a whole campaign stretch by playing it.

## 2026-10-30: Grenade log says why each one went off (rc_combat)

- **Changed:** `crates/rc_combat/src/throw.rs`: the `grenades: X went off ...` log line now says why: "on touching a character", "on touching the level", "when a character came near" or "when its fuse ran out".
- **Why:** In the user's replay of geo_01a with the squad fix, the player went down twice from their own anti-armour round. Both times it went off 0.03 s after firing, about 80 uu in front of the player, and the log didn't say what it ran into.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test --release -p rc_combat` (19 + 21 range tests).
- **Result:** All pass.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not seen in a real game log yet.
- **Next:** The next play log shows what the anti-armour round hit.

## 2026-10-30: Squad mates no longer shoot through the player or each other (rc_squad, rc_combat log)

- **Changed:** `crates/rc_squad/src/fight.rs`: the "friend in the way" check before a squad mate fires now tests the line of fire against each friend's whole body (the 40-wide, 168-tall commando cylinder; a low wide shape for someone lying down), widened by `friendly_clearance` and by how far the shot can stray (aim error plus the gun's spread). New `shot_passes_friend` and test `a_shot_over_a_friends_shoulder_counts_as_through_them`. `friendly_clearance` is now the gap round the body: 20 uu (was 60 uu round the middle point). `crates/rc_combat/src/damage.rs`: every `combat: ... took N damage` log line now says who did it (`from Scorch`, `from battle droid 2`, or `from no one (script, fall or level)`). `crates/rc_squad_plugin/src/lib.rs`: logs `X hit the player by mistake` and names the squad mate hit; those no longer count as hits on enemies.
- **Why:** The user's geo_01a run (`logs/rc_game.log`, 2026-10-30 ~03:25Z) lost its health with almost no droid hits. Reading the log: the droids' 47 shots all missed (`0 hit the player`). The player lost 100 shield and then health to 10-damage Blaster hits that matched squad mates' shots (squad bolts x0.5 on the player's damage table), then 175 from the level script (`ScriptedTrigger3 #6 HurtActor: the player takes 175 damage`, the original's intro "anti-infantry round"), then a last squad bolt put the player down (`Scorch KILLED 740v1`). Scorch revived the player to 50% = 100/200. Fixer also hit Sev about 40 times from where he stood. Cause: the old check measured only from the friend's middle point (84 uu up), but the squad's shots leave at eye height, so a line passing at shoulder or head height (about 110 uu above the middle) went "clear" and straight through the body.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test --release -p rc_squad -p rc_combat -p rc_squad_plugin` in the cloud sandbox.
- **Result:** All pass: rc_squad 30 (1 new), rc_combat 19 + 21 (range), rc_squad_plugin fight_room and jobs_room. The new test uses the exact positions and direction from the log (Fixer at (-1409, -720, -2251) firing along (0.98, -0.17, 0.06) past Sev at (-1090, -758)), which the old check let through.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not re-run in `rc_game` on the PC yet. The scripted 175 damage is the original level's own event and is kept. Whether the original lets squad bolts hurt the player at all is not known (the damage table says x0.5, so we keep it). Squad mates hold fire more often now; they don't yet step aside to get a clear line.
- **Next:** The user replays geo_01a; the log should show no `hit the player by mistake` lines and the damage lines should name every attacker. Done 2026-10-30 04:48Z on the PC: 0 squad hits on the player in a 3-minute fight (321 droid shots, 10 droids destroyed).

## 2026-10-30: Geonosian warriors fight with their force pike (rc_ai, rc_combat, rc_enemies)

- **Changed:** `rc_ai`: `[combat]` gains `melee_range`, `melee_damage`, `melee_damage_type`, `melee_delay` and `lunge_distance`; new `Maneuver::Charge` (rush at the enemy, straight if the way is clear, else along the paths) and `AiEvent::Melee`; an enemy with `weapon = ""` never shoots. `[look] melee` names the swing animation. `rc_combat`: new public message `MeleeAttack` and system `melee_attacks` (the player's melee rule, skipping the attacker's own team, scaled by the target's damage table). `rc_enemies` sends it, plays the swing, counts `melee_swings`. `data/enemies/geonosian.toml`: no gun, force pike 350 / 125 `CTDamageMeleeWarrior`, `LungeDist` 800. The enemies test room now puts each spawn point at its enemy's own height (it used to start the taller Geonosian 35 uu inside the floor). Tests: 3 in `rc_ai`, 1 in `rc_enemies/tests/fight.rs`.
- **Why:** The original's Geonosian warriors carry only a force pike; ours shot a stand-in blaster.
- **Tested how:** `rc_probe --class` (ForcePikeDefaults, GeonosianWarrior, StateGeonosianWarriorAttack) and `rc_character --list` on geonosian.ukx, read-only copies in the cloud sandbox. `cargo test --release --workspace --locked --no-fail-fast`.
- **Result:** In the test room a Geonosian 1500 uu away starts its charge at 0.5 s, swings at 3.3 s (`melee by ... hit ... at 308 uu for 125 (125 x1 by its table, CTDamageMeleeWarrior)`), then every 1.6 s, and fires no shots. rc_ai tests: it doesn't swing through a wall; a fighter with a gun only rushes in within its lunge distance.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested in `rc_game` on a real map with Geonosians, or on Windows, or by eye. When to rush and swing are our rules. The hit lands at the start of the swing animation. Warriors don't fly, jump (`JumpDist`, `Lunge`) or climb walls. Pike melee doesn't stagger or knock back.
- **Next:** Geonosian flying and jumping, or the drone and elite types.

## 2026-10-30: Enemies throw grenades (rc_ai, rc_enemies)

- **Changed:** `rc_ai`: new `[grenades]` settings (`GrenadeUse`: grenade file, count, `probability`, `min_time`, `min_range`/`max_range`, `seen_within`, `throw_time`, and the arc's speed, gravity and radius), `AiEvent::Throw`, `Fighter::grenades_left`, and `brain::throw_arc` (aims a lob, or a flat throw under a low ceiling, and checks the level doesn't block it). `[look] throw` names the throw animation. Saves keep each enemy's grenades left (`grenades_left`, only for types that carry them). `rc_enemies` turns a throw into `rc_combat::ThrowGrenade`, reads the grenade file's flight numbers to aim, plays the throw animation, and logs `droid N threw ...` and a grenade count in the summary. New `data/grenades/thermal_detonator_enemy.toml`; `data/enemies/trandoshan.toml` carries two; `battle_droid.toml` documents the section (none). Tests: 4 in `rc_ai`, 2 in `rc_enemies/tests/fight.rs`.
- **Why:** The original's Trandoshans throw thermal detonators; ours didn't throw at all.
- **Tested how:** `rc_probe --class` on the install's script packages and `rc_character --list` on trandoshan.ukx (copied read-only to the cloud sandbox). `cargo test --release -p rc_ai -p rc_enemies`.
- **Result:** rc_ai 20 of 20, rc_enemies 7 of 7. In the test room a Trandoshan 1500 uu away throws at its first decision (0.5 s, a 1198 uu/s lob), the grenade bounces 4 times and goes off 147 uu from the player: `blast damage 115.0 (x0.82 by distance, x0.7 by its table)` plus 1 s of burning. No second throw within 20 s. Arcs land within 15 uu of where they are aimed; none is thrown through a wall, at under 600 or over 2500 uu, or onto a friend.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested in `rc_game` on a real map with Trandoshans, or on Windows. When to throw is our rule (the original's is compiled). The grenade leaves the hand at the start of the throw animation, not at its release point. Super battle droids' crouch rocket and the Geonosian drone's spit are not added. The Trandoshan's look still fails to load (no `Trandoshan` skeletal mesh in trandoshan.ukx; found while listing its animations), so the throw animation isn't seen yet.
- **Next:** The Geonosian warrior's force pike (melee for enemies).

## 2026-10-30: Level scripts start enemy spawners (rc_ai, rc_enemies, rc_scripts_plugin)

- **Changed:** `rc_ai::spawns` reads more of each `PawnFactory` and `SpawnPoint` (spawn and "all gone" events, the tag, death event and first-hurt event each droid gets, `SpawnIntervalMax`, `MaxInWorld`, player range) and uses the class defaults from CTGame.u when a value isn't stored (capacity 2, one alive at once, spawn at the factory itself). `rc_ai::Battle` follows the original's spawner rules: an event named like the spawner's tag switches it on and the same event again off; it makes one at once, then one per interval; it sends `SpawnEvent`, `ExhaustedEvent`, and each droid's death and first-hurt events as `BattleEvent::Event`; `trigger`, `stop` and `reset` carry out the level's events and the scripts' orders. With `Battle::scripted` set, spawners no longer start themselves when the player comes near. New shared messages in `rc_game_core::events`: `ScriptEvent` (moved there from `rc_scripts_plugin`, still re-exported), `RaiseEvent` (another part sends an event into the scripts) and `FactoryOrder`, plus the `LevelScriptsRun` marker. `rc_scripts_plugin` turns `StopFactorySpawning` and `ResetFactory` into `FactoryOrder`s and passes `RaiseEvent`s into the director. `rc_enemies` hears both, logs every spawner it runs, removes droids a script removes, and sends droid and spawner events back. The `[enemies]` save section gains each spawner's "off for good" and next spawn point and each droid's spawn point and "already hurt" (old saves still load). `rc_game --script` has 4 new checks on geo_01a's small bunker.
- **Why:** Thread "Run the rest of the level scripts", first step: enemy spawns. Until now spawners only scripts could start came on when the player walked near, and nothing told the scripts when droids died.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_ai` (23 tests, 7 new), `cargo test -p rc_enemies` (6, 1 new), `rc_droidsim geo_01a` (no scripts: 7 of 7 checks), and `xvfb-run -a -s "-screen 0 1280x1024x24" target/debug/rc_game --script` in the cloud with the game files copied read-only from the PC. The self-test sends `SpawnSmBunkerDroids` (the event the map's own trigger sends later in the level), waits for the droids, destroys them and waits for the scripts to hear `DeadGunnerDroid`.
- **Result:** `SCRIPT DONE: 52 checks passed, 0 failed` (48 on main plus 4 new). Log: 4 bunker spawners switched on by the event, 4 droids each 0 uu from their spawner, 3 `droid N (destroyed) sends DeadGunnerDroid` lines, and the scripts' log shows all 3 arriving. On geo_01a, 40 of 58 spawners make enemies we have (36 battle droid, 4 Geonosian); 18 make clone troopers, Delta 62 and a seeker droid, which no part runs yet.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by playing through. The self-test sends the bunker event itself rather than walking to the bunker's trigger. Not done: `bCovert` (spawn only out of sight), `bStartTimerAfterDeath`, `bAlwaysUseFactorySettings`; `SpawnEvent` is only sent when a droid is actually made (the original also sends it when the player is out of range). Scripts that act on droids by tag (`HurtActor`, `DestroyActor`, `MoveToPoint`, `PlayAnim`) are still "not done yet": droids now carry the tags, so that comes next.
- **Next:** Merge, then dialogue and sounds (`PlaySound`), character actions by tag, and level changes, one PR each.

## 2026-10-30: Grenade explosion sounds, enemy voices, and the repeater's sound

- **Changed:** `rc_combat::read_sounds` now reads `SoundMultiple` groups as well as single sounds; `WeaponLibrary.sounds` holds a `GameSound` (one or more takes) and each playing picks one. Grenade files got `blast` sounds (the game's explosion effect sounds). The four enemy files got the game's voice lines from `character_voice.uax`: `spotted`, `hurt`, `death`, and new `hurt_<kind>` / `death_<kind>` keys (explosion, electrical, fall, fire, melee) plus `hurt_interval` (`rc_ai::Sounds::by_kind`, `for_damage`). `rc_enemies` picks the line for the kind of damage, says a hurt line at most every 2 s per enemy, and uses the killing damage's kind for the death line. `DamageKind::name`. `EnemySounds::load_type` (moved out of `load_fight`). New tests `crates/rc_enemies/tests/voices.rs` and `fight.rs` `droid_says_the_games_lines`; `rc_combat` `real_game_sounds_and_model` also checks grenade sounds and groups; new `rc_ai` test `sound_lines_by_damage_kind`. Notes in `docs/RESEARCH.md`.
- **Why:** Grenades went off silently and enemies never spoke. The Trandoshan repeater was silent because its `Uzi_Fire` is a group, which the reader skipped (it was in the right package all along).
- **Tested how:** In the cloud with gen_sound.uax, character_voice.uax and the script packages copied read-only from the PC: `RC_GAME_DIR=<copy> cargo test --release -p rc_combat -p rc_enemies -p rc_ai`, and the whole workspace.
- **Result:** `sounds loaded: 14`, no missing names, `Uzi_Fire` 1 take, `exp_empGren` 2. Voices: battle droid 10 groups / 49 takes, super battle droid 9 / 46, Trandoshan 11 / 41, Geonosian 7 / 30, all found. In the fight room a droid logs `says BD1_SpottedEnemy` at 0.0 s, one `hurt (blaster) sound BD1_HurtSmallArms` for 4 hits, and `death (blaster) sound BD1_DieSmallArms`. 241 workspace tests pass with the game files, and the new ones skip without them (as on GitHub).
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not listened to, and not run in `rc_game` on a map (the test reads the sounds but nothing plays them aloud). Not tested on Windows. Which take plays and the 2 s hurt gap are our choice. Lines for other events (heard something, saw a grenade, retreat, taunts) and the grenade ticking loop aren't played. Explosions use one sound whatever surface they hit.
- **Next:** The user listens in `rc_game`; then the other voice events once the AI has them.


## 2026-10-30: Saves keep doors, lifts and the level scripts (rc_movers, rc_movers_plugin, rc_scripts, rc_scripts_plugin)

- **Changed:** Two new save sections. `[movers]`: `rc_movers::MoverSet` gained `snapshot` and `restore` (each mover's key, phase, direction, held/used, and each touch trigger, matched by map name); `rc_movers_plugin/src/save.rs` writes and reads it, and `LevelMovers` gained `restore`. `[scripts]`: new `rc_scripts::save` (`Director::snapshot`/`restore`: clock, random state, each script's next action and what it waits for, triggers, counters, conditions, props, pending events); `rc_scripts_plugin/src/save.rs` adds level parts switched off or hidden, bacta dispensers, objectives and held checkpoints, and marks the level as started. The scripts don't run while `RestoringSave` exists. `rc_game --script` gained two checks after the quickload, and its "saving again gives the same state" check now leaves `[movers]` and `[scripts]` to those (they run on clocks). Docs: DESIGN ("Saving, loading and checkpoints", movers, level scripts), README.
- **Why:** After a load, the level scripts restarted from `levelstart` (the intro played again, the barricade logic reran) and every door and lift was back where the map starts.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_movers -p rc_movers_plugin -p rc_scripts -p rc_scripts_plugin`, `cargo test --workspace`, `cargo build -p rc_game`, and `xvfb-run -a -s "-screen 0 1280x1024x24" target/debug/rc_game --script` in the cloud sandbox with 238 game files (902 MB) copied read-only from the install.
- **Result:** rc_movers 13 tests (2 new: a door saved halfway open loads halfway and then opens, waits and closes frame for frame like the original; unknown names and keys past the end are skipped or clamped). rc_movers_plugin 4 (3 new: every phase through the file and back; a half-open door through a world save and load; unknown movers and phases). rc_scripts 9 (2 new: a script waiting on a timer and a half-counted counter carry on identically after a restore, without the level start; unknown names reported). rc_scripts_plugin 2 (new: a small geo_01a with the barricade blown, the volume off and a checkpoint held goes through the file and back; after the load the parts stay off, the level counts as started, and the script finishes on `BactaUsed`).
- **In the game:** `SCRIPT DONE: 47 checks passed, 0 failed` (45 before plus the two new ones). After the quickload on geo_01a: `scripts: put back t=64.2s: 93 scripts (12 finished, 73 waiting), 6 parts not solid, 4 hidden, 1 objectives; level start not sent again`; 0 events since the load, `levelstart` sent 0 times, all 12 finished scripts still finished, the barricade still not solid and both its drawn parts hidden. Doors: 2 movers saved, both at the same key after the load (both were shut, so geo_01a doesn't show an open door coming back; the unit tests cover that).
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows. Parts are saved by map export number, which is only right for the same map file. Drawn parts a script showed again (`SetHidden` off) aren't un-hidden on screen (that was so before). The log counters (`history`, `stats`) restart after a load.
- **Next:** The user runs `rc_game --script` on the PC; it should end `47 checks passed, 0 failed`.

## 2026-10-29: Pause and campaign pages can use their original menus (rc_menus::pages)

New `rc_probe --pages` probes and paints every listed page and says which would be used and why not; the file's reader lives in `rc_menus::pages` (rc_menus now uses serde and toml). `Sprite`, `Text` and `Button` in `rc_menus::layout` gained `Default` (for tests).

## 2026-10-30: Droids no longer spot the player right after a load (rc_game player and self-test)

- **Changed:** `crates/rc_game/src/player.rs`: the player's camera now starts at the body's eyes when the player is spawned (before, it started at the map's centre, 0,0,0), and loading the `[player]` section moves the camera along with the body. `place_camera` uses the same `eye_view` helper. New unit test `loading_a_save_moves_the_camera_with_the_player`. `crates/rc_game/src/script.rs`: before the quicksave the script waits until no squad mate has moved for 3 frames ("squad mates standing still"), and a new check "no droid spots the player just after the load" fails if a droid that was idle in the save is alerted 0.3 s after loading.
- **Why:** Droids look for the player at the camera's position (`rc_enemies::player_target`). The camera only follows the body at the end of each frame, so on the first frame after a quickload the droids looked at the old camera spot, "saw" the player from about 4100 uu away and switched to alerted. That made the self-test check "saving again after the load gives the same state" fail in some runs. On the user's PC the same check failed for a second reason: a squad mate was saved mid-step and walked on 56 uu after the load.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_game` (14 tests). `xvfb-run -a -s "-screen 0 1280x1024x24" target/debug/rc_game --script` in the cloud with the game files copied read-only from the PC, 10 runs of each build, one at a time.
- **Result:** With this change: `SCRIPT DONE: 46 checks passed, 0 failed` in 10 of 10 runs. Same script on main's player code: droids spotted the player ("droid 1 spotted the player 4079 uu away", 0.1 s after the load) and 2 checks failed in 6 of 10 runs. An earlier try ran the "same state" check in the same frame as the load: that always failed, because the squad and droids are put back during the next frame, so it was dropped.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not re-run on Windows yet. Aiming (`rc_combat` fire, squad orders) also reads the camera; they now get the right spot after a load too, but that isn't checked separately. On the PC run no droids had spawned by the time of the save, so the droid check passes there without testing much.
- **Next:** The user re-runs the self-test on the PC.
## 2026-10-29: Context orders checked against the game's files

- **Changed:** `rc_squad::map::read` now reads the order markers from CTMarkers.u (`MarkerDoorBreach`, `MarkerHackDoor`, `MarkerHackTerminal`, `MarkerDemolition`, `MarkerSnipe`, `MarkerGrenade`, `MarkerAntiArmor`) and pairs them with their anchors and `SquadMarkerSpot`s; grenade, anti-armour and most sniper positions have no spot and were missed before. `MarkerSpot` gained `marker_class` and `target` (a grenade marker's target area). Jobs gained `preferred` (who goes when free) and `target_radius`. The game's numbers replace guesses in `squad.toml`: demolition 256 damage in 256 uu after 10 s, breach 200 in 256 after 2.5 s, thermal detonator 400 in 512 after 2 s, sniper accuracy 1.0. New example `marker_survey` lists every marker in a folder of maps.

- **Tested how:** `rc_probe --classes ctmarkers.u` and `--class` on the install's script packages; `marker_survey` on all 50 campaign maps; `cargo test --release -p rc_squad` (29) and `-p rc_squad_plugin` (fight_room, jobs_room); `rc_squad_sim` on geo_01a; all files copied read-only to the cloud sandbox.
- **Result:** Every bacta, breach, slice, demolition, sniper, grenade and anti-armour marker in the 50 maps gets its job (240 bacta spots, 159 breach spots at 54 doors, 101 slice, 82 demolition, 179 sniper, 30 grenade, 46 anti-armour). geo_01a: `SIM DONE: 33 checks passed, 0 failed`, with `Scorch healed to 400` at a bacta dispenser. jobs_room: Scorch takes the demolition, `BLAST ... 256 damage within 256 uu` 10 s after setting it.
