After a breach the original's grenadier throws a thermal detonator into the room; we don't. Doors still don't open until the level scripts part listens for `SquadJobDone`. Not tested on Windows or by eye.
- **Next:** Hook `SquadJobDone` into the level scripts (doors), then traps, mines and turrets.

## 2026-10-29: Squad context orders: bacta, demolition, breach, slice, sniper, grenade (rc_squad, rc_squad_plugin)

- **Changed:** New `crates/rc_squad/src/jobs.rs` and `[job.*]` sections in `data/squad/squad.toml`: each kind of squad marker now does its own thing. Bacta heals the squad mate who uses it. Demolition and breach set a charge that blows after a fuse (blast damage to everyone near) and then "sets off" the marker's anchor. Slice takes longer and sets it off without a blast. Breach is a group job: a squad mate goes to every spot at the door, and work starts when all are in place. Sniper, grenade and anti-armour positions are posts: the squad mate stays until recalled (F4), the sniper with longer sight and single shots from `dc17m_sniper`, the grenadier throwing a grenade every 6 s. `rc_squad_plugin` heals, turns blasts into `rc_combat::Damage` (kind Explosion), and sends a new `SquadJobDone` message for the level's doors and scripts. New test `crates/rc_squad_plugin/tests/jobs_room.rs`.
- **Why:** Morning queue item: the original's squad context orders at map markers.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test --release -p rc_squad` (27 tests, 8 new) and `cargo test --release -p rc_squad_plugin` (fight_room and the new jobs_room, no game files) in the cloud sandbox; `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`.
- **Result:** All pass. jobs_room log (`logs/jobs_room.log`): `Fixer working at SquadMarkerSpot0` at t=3.45, `Fixer healed to 400` at t=6.47 (was 100); `Sev set the charge at SquadMarkerSpot1: 5.0 s` at t=15.72, `BLAST (Demolition, Sev) ... 500 damage within 512 uu` at t=20.72, the stand-in droid next to it died, and `SquadJobDone` named `DemolitionCharge0`.
- **Still broken / not tested:** The marker class names and settings have **not** been checked against the install yet (no folder access tonight); the job matching words come from earlier notes (`MarkerSnipe`, `MarkerGrenade`, `MarkerDoorBreach`). Fuse times, blast sizes, slice time, grenade timing and sniper numbers are guesses. Doors don't open yet: `SquadJobDone` is sent and logged, but nothing listens until the level scripts part is merged. No grenade flight or explosion effects (the combat PR #25 adds grenades). Not tested on Windows, in `rc_game`, or on geo_01a.

## 2026-10-29: Fuller combat: the original's numbers, damage tables and grenade rules

- **Changed:** Every number in the grenade, attachment, melee and new enemy files is now read from the game (`rc_probe --class`, read only), each marked `game:` on its line. New `rc_combat::damage_types` (`DamageTypes`, `DamageTable`) and `data/combat/damage_types.toml`: the 53 damage types' parents and all 41 `DamageVariance` tables; shots, melee and blasts are multiplied by the target's table. Grenades follow `CTProjExplosive`: fuse plus `after_impact`, detonate on touch, sticking, proximity checks, actor bounce, status effects (`[status]`: burn, electrocution, stun, with immunity from the table). Weapons gain `shot.damage_type`, `[melee]`, `fire.zoom_fovs` (steps) and `fire.zoom_damage`; the sniper is instant hit. `Stunned` gains `kind` and `stops`; `DamageKind::Fire`. `rc_ai` gains `combat.damage_table`; `rc_enemies` gives each enemy its table and only stops it for stopping effects. New weapon files `sbd_blaster.toml`, `trandoshan_smg.toml` (the Trandoshan drops it).
- **Why:** The task: read every number from the game and keep them in data files.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test --release --workspace --locked` and `rc_shoot --script` under Xvfb in the cloud sandbox, with the game's System, Properties and Animations folders staged read-only from the user's PC (no sound or texture packages).
- **Result:** 176 tests pass; `rc_shoot --script` 12 of 12. Logged numbers checked: a zoomed sniper shot on a battle droid does 175 x 1.72 x 0.4 = 120.4; rifle melee 220 x 3 = 660; a thermal 200 uu from a droid does the falloff x 0.75; droid bolts on the player do 24 x 0.5 = 12; an EMP electrocutes a droid at 50 a second and kills it in about 2 s; a flash stuns an organic but not a droid; a thermal sets an organic alight at 15 a second without stopping it; a sonic stuck on the floor waits, then goes off within 0.5 s of a droid coming within 400 uu; the anti-armour round does 400 x 0.75 on a direct hit.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested in `rc_game` on real maps or on Windows. Reload times of the attachments and repeater, grenade explosion sounds and droid voice names are still placeholders or empty. The picked-up E-5 uses `CTDamageEnergy` (our choice: the game's type is ignored by droids). The Trandoshan uses the `Trandoshan` mesh (the game's `TrandoshanMerc` isn't in `trandoshan.ukx`). Geonosian warriors shoot a stand-in blaster (the original's force pike is melee) and don't fly. Enemies don't throw grenades. Sound names weren't checked (no sound packages in the cloud).
- **Next:** Check the new enemies on maps that have them, read the sound packages, and give squad mates `DV_OrganicCTSquadDamage`.

## 2026-10-29: Controllers, gyro aiming, button prompts, rebinding and the Panini view

- **Changed:** New crate `rc_pads`: controller kinds, button names (`data/input/button_names.toml`), gyro clean-up, and an SDL3 reader (SDL built from source with only its controller, HID, sensor and rumble parts) that replaces gilrs when it starts. `rc_game_core`: `InputPrompts`, actions `gyro_ratchet` and `gyro_toggle`. `rc_game`: prompts follow the last-used device, per-stick dead zones and curves, trigger threshold, `[gyro]` settings, rebinding saved as changes to `my_bindings.toml` (`rebind.rs`), `--log-input`. New tool `rc_padtest`. New crate `rc_panini` and `video.panini`: the optional Panini view. `.gitignore` tracks our own `.wgsl` shaders.
- **Why:** The user's goals: extensive controller support with rebinding, dead zones and curves; gyro aiming; dual input with prompts that follow the device; no edge stretching on ultrawides.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test --workspace` in the cloud sandbox, and on Windows and Linux in the GitHub checks. Pretend controllers drive the real input systems in a small Bevy app. SDL's own virtual PS5 controller (with a gyro and an accelerometer) goes through the SDL reader. `rc_game --frames 120` starts with SDL. `panini_check` measures cubes in 32:9 screenshots.
- **Result:** Input tests 9 of 9 and controller tests 13 of 13 pass. The PS5 left stick reads 1.0, a 0.5 trigger fires, a 0.1 trigger doesn't. Full right stick turns 0.0417 rad in a 60th of a second (2.5 rad/s). A 1 rad/s gyro turn for 4 ms readings gives 0.024 rad at sensitivity 2. Ratchet held gives 0, and toggle and aiming mode work. Rebinding Reload to G and Crouch to the Deck's L4 saves `reload = ["KeyG"]` and `crouch = ["LeftPaddle1"]`, reads back, and warns that L4 was the gyro ratchet. Prompts: Xbox fire RT, PS5 R2, Switch ZR, Deck R2. Log: `pads: SDL 3.4.18 reads the controllers and gyros`. Panini 0.2 at 32:9 keeps the middle cube at 54 px; the edge cube's stretch goes from 4.67 to 2.98 times.
- **Still broken / not tested:** **Not tested with any real controller or gyro** (the cloud has none). Not run on the user's PC. Panini not tested inside a level (no game files in the sandbox this time). No rebinding screen, no button pictures, no rumble, no aim assist. Phone gyro not started. Building now needs CMake.
- **Next:** Run `rc_padtest` with each controller the user has. The menus part adds the controls page that sends `StartRebind`. Phone gyro with the Android app.

## 2026-10-29: Levels run their own scripts (rc_level::scripts, rc_scripts, rc_scripts_plugin)

- **Changed:** New `rc_level::scripts` reads every actor's values and every script's action list from a map. `map_collision` and the level drawing keep props and blocking volumes with a tag of their own apart (`part_triangles`, own `MoverPart` batches); `rc_level_plugin` makes them `LevelParts`, solid until switched off. New crate `rc_scripts` (no Bevy): `Director` runs scripted triggers and sequences, touch triggers, counters, conditions, checkpoints and destructible props as events and tags; tool `rc_script_check`. New crate `rc_scripts_plugin`: `ScriptsPlugin` runs it in `rc_game` and carries out doors, barricades, damage to the player, bacta dispensers, prompts, objectives and checkpoints; `ScriptEvent` and `CheckpointReached` messages. `rc_movers` gained `release`; `rc_movers_plugin` gained `release` and `TriggersRunElsewhere`. Settings in `data/scripts/scripts.toml`. Script checks added to `rc_game --script`. Docs: DESIGN ("Level scripts"), PLUGINS, README.
- **Why:** Next-steps item 1: levels should play as missions. geo_01a's landing-zone barricade never opened, so the player couldn't reach the droids.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_scripts` (7 tests), `rc_script_check` on all 79 maps, `rc_script_check geo_01a --secs 90 --fire BactaUsed@60`, and `xvfb-run -a -s "-screen 0 1280x1024x24" target/debug/rc_game --script` in the cloud sandbox with the game files copied read-only.
- **Result:** All 79 maps read: 2,842 scripts, 19,041 actions, 87 action kinds, 0 unreadable (25 empty action slots skipped). 144 checkpoints, matching the save thread's count. In the game: `SCRIPT DONE: 37 checks passed, 0 failed`. Chain on geo_01a: `levelstart 0.0s, Rappel 7.9s, CheckpointLevelStart 18.8s, StartBactaTraining 30.0s, BactaDispenserActive 51.9s, BactaUsed 53.3s, RetreatToGammaSector 57.6s, DialogGetToGunship 61.6s`; player hurt 175, healed 75; `BactaBarricadeBlockingVolume` switched off; `PathToGunshipBarricade` destroyed and hidden (2 of 2 parts); a box swept through the gap stopped at 41% before and passes after.
- **Found in the game's data:** the barricade is a prop (`Blockade01`, health 49,000) plus a blocking volume, opened by `BactaUsed`, which the bacta dispenser sends from its `BactaRecharging` struct. Dispensers also send `BactaActive.Event`. The first PlayerStart's `Event` starts the level (`levelstart`, `Startup`, `SetupLevel`, ...).
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows. About 100 actions in geo_01a's first minute are logged "not done yet" (dialogue, animation, characters, factories, squad changes, camera, music). Droid factories don't spawn on their events yet (the droids part spawns its own way), so later fights aren't paced by the script. Sound lengths are a fixed 3 s. Objective numbers aren't shown. The script's player is moved to the dispenser, not walked there. Not compared with the real game by eye.
- **Next:** Hook factories (`ScriptEvent`) into the droids part, `ChangeLevel` to go on to the next map, and real sound lengths.
- **Merged with save and load (#21):** the map's checkpoints now send the save part's `rc_game_core::save::CheckpointReached`, so reaching one really saves (geo_01a's `checkpoint0` saves 19 s in). The self-test's "level's start was saved" check accepts that a later checkpoint saved over the start. After a load the level scripts still start from the beginning: they aren't saved yet (a `scripts` save section is the next step).

## 2026-10-29: Doors and lifts run in the game (rc_movers_plugin)

- **Changed:** New crate `crates/rc_movers_plugin` (`MoversPlugin`, `LevelMovers`, `MoverHappened`). `rc_level_plugin` now hands over the map's movers and their triangles as `LevelMoverData`. `rc_game` adds the plugin, its player movement calls `LevelMovers::step` before its own step, and the script checks that the map's movers run. Docs: DESIGN ("In the game"), PLUGINS (doors and lifts row), README.

## 2026-10-29: The self-test ignores the real mouse (rc_game --script)

- **Changed:** `crates/rc_game/src/script.rs`: a scripted run clears the window's pointer position and mouse clicks every frame before the menus look at them, so only the script moves the menus. The music-volume step starts from full volume, rather than from whatever the last run saved.
- **Why:** On the user's PC, `rc_game --script` chose Resume instead of "Quit to main menu" and 12 checks after it failed. The mouse was over the window, and pointing at a menu button selects it. Each run also saved a lower music volume, so after about ten runs the "left lowers the music volume" check would always fail.

After it, `SCRIPT DONE: 32 checks passed, 0 failed`, also when starting from a saved music volume of 0.

- **Next:** The user re-runs the self-test on the PC.

## 2026-10-29: Saving, loading and checkpoints (rc_game_core::save, rc_save)

- **Changed:** New `rc_game_core/src/save.rs`: the `SavePart` interface each part fills in, `add_save_part`, the messages `SaveGame`, `LoadGame`, `CheckpointReached`, `SaveResult`, and `RestoringSave` and `SaveList`. New crate `rc_save` (`SavePlugin`): readable TOML save files written safely in a save folder, quicksave and quickload, a checkpoint save at each level start and at each new `CheckpointReached`, loading by reloading the map and handing each part its section on the first frame. Sections: `[player]` (rc_game), `[combat]` (rc_combat: health, shield, weapons, ammo), `[enemies]` (rc_enemies, with new `rc_ai::Battle::snapshot`/`restore` and `Fighter::alert`), `[checkpoints]`. New actions `quick_save` (F5) and `quick_load` (F9), a `[save] folder` setting, and save steps in `rc_game --script`. New `rc_level::checkpoints` reads a map's `Checkpoint` actors and what fires them.
- **Why:** Next-steps item 5: save and load like the original, with one interface every part implements.
- **Found in the game's data (read only):** `DefaultCtrl.ini` binds `F5=QuickSave` and `F9=QuickLoad`, so our keys match. `CTGame.Checkpoint` is a placeable actor: when an event with its `Tag` fires, it waits until the player exists and isn't incapacitated, then autosaves. The 50 campaign maps with checkpoints hold 144 of them (1 to 7 per map; the prologue and epilogue have none). 56 are fired by a walk-in `Trigger` whose `Event` is the checkpoint's tag; the other 88 by level scripts. Many maps have one for the level's start (tags like `CheckpointLevelStart`, `SavePointStart`, `InitialSave`), which matches our level-start save. The level scripts part will send `CheckpointReached` when those events fire (agreed with that thread).
- **Tested how:** `cargo test --workspace --locked` in the cloud sandbox, including 10 tests in `rc_save` and 1 in `rc_ai`. `xvfb-run rc_game --script` in the cloud sandbox on geo_01a, with 268 game files (827 MB) copied read-only from the install. A checkpoint scan of all 50 campaign maps with `rc_level::checkpoints`.
- **Result:** All unit tests pass. Script: `SCRIPT DONE: 39 checks passed, 0 failed` (32 before, plus 7 save checks). The level start was saved as a checkpoint. A reached checkpoint was saved. The quicksave held 4 sections, with the player 0.00 uu from the live player. After walking 422 uu away and quickloading, the player was 0.00 uu from the saved spot and 0.000 rad off its facing, and the DC-17m had 57 of 60 shots as saved. Saving again after the load matched the first save: player, combat and checkpoints off by 0.000, and enemies off by 0.253 (the battle clock moves on). A save takes about 1.5 ms and is 3 KB.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows. No droids were alive during the scripted save, so living droids being put back is tested only by the `rc_ai` unit test. The squad isn't saved (its thread is changing that state; agreed to wait). Doors, lifts and level scripts aren't saved. There's no Load Game screen yet (the menus thread owns it). Droids come back with fresh plans. Grenades and attachments will need adding to `[combat]` once they exist.
- **Next:** Add `[squad]` once the squad PR lands, and a `[movers]` section once doors are in the game (PR #26). Hook the level scripts' checkpoint events into the save.

## 2026-10-29: The squad fights and revives (rc_squad, rc_squad_plugin)

- **Changed:** New `crates/rc_squad/src/fight.rs`: squad mates pick the nearest enemy they can see, fire DC-17m bursts at it after a reaction time, and hold fire when the player or another mate is in the line; downed mates get the nearest free mate to run over and revive them, the player too, and the player revives a mate by holding Use beside them. New `[fight]` and `[revive]` sections in `data/squad/squad.toml`. `rc_squad_plugin` fills `SquadThreats` from every living `Team::Separatist` body, sends the shots as `rc_combat::FireShot`, gives health back and removes `Dead` on a revive, and counts shots, hits, kills and revives (`SquadFightStats`, a `fight:` log line every 5 s). `rc_game`'s player can't move while down. New test `crates/rc_squad_plugin/tests/fight_room.rs`; `rc_squad_sim` gained a fight and revive part.
- **Why:** Next-steps item 4: a smarter squad that shoots back and revives, as in the original.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test --release -p rc_squad` (19 tests, 7 new) and `cargo test --release -p rc_squad_plugin --test fight_room` (real squad and combat plugins in the movement test room, three stand-in droids that stand still and don't shoot, no game files) in the cloud sandbox.
- **Result:** All pass. Test room log (`logs/fight_room.log`): first shots 0.5 s after spotting; all three droids killed by t=8.2 s (Sev 2, Fixer 1), 65 shots, 15 hits; Fixer and Scorch held fire 33 times for the player in the way; the player's health stayed 200/200. Sev knocked down at t=16.0, Scorch ran 121 uu and revived him at t=19.3 (down 3.3 s) with 200/400 health. The player knocked down at t=24.0, Scorch revived them at t=27.5 (down 3.5 s) with 100/200.
- **Found in the game's data:** squad mates' `Accuracy` is 0.9 and `SightRadius` 5000 (Commando07/40/62DeltaDefaults), now in `squad.toml`. Reviving uses the bacta healer (`CTInventory.BactaHealer`, 5 charges recharging 1 a second); how long a revive takes and how much health comes back are in compiled code, so those stay guesses (3 s, half health).
- **geo_01a run:** `rc_squad_sim` with the map, 5 static-mesh packages and 9 script packages copied read-only from the install: `SIM DONE: 33 checks passed, 0 failed`. Fixer revived Sev in 3.0 s. Three stand-in droids 1000 uu away were killed in 2.5 s (21 shots, 17 hits; Scorch 2 kills, Fixer 1). Scorch revived the player (down 3.5 s, back at 100/200). The player revived Scorch by holding Use for 3.0 s. A first run failed one check: Sev, already 92 uu from Scorch, revived him before the player could, which is what the rules say to do. The sim now tells the others to stand back for that part.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by eye. Revive time and revived health are guesses. Droids only shoot at the player (`rc_ai::Battle` has one target), so mates only get hurt by stray bolts. No revive or firing animations: the bodies are still capsules.
- **Next:** Read the original numbers from the install and run `rc_squad_sim` on geo_01a; then the context orders and the real models.

## 2026-10-29: Doors and lifts move (rc_level::movers, rc_movers, rc_walk)

- **Changed:** New `rc_level::movers` reads every mover (door, lift, hatch) and trigger in a map: keys, timing, easing, what it does when blocked, what sets it off, tag, event and sounds, from the map and then the class defaults (`ClassLook` now keeps all of a class's default values). New crate `rc_movers` runs them: moves them key to key, moves their collision with them, carries a player standing on a lift, pushes or backs off when the player is in the way, runs player-touch triggers and chained events. Our own choices are in `data/movers/movers.toml`. `map_collision` keeps mover triangles apart; the level viewer's scene gives movers their own draw batches (`MoverPart`); `rc_walk` moves them every frame, logs every mover event, and has `--ride`. New program `rc_mover_check` runs every mover in every map without a window.
- **Why:** Overnight item 1 (all levels): doors and lifts stood still, so routes through levels were shut.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_movers` (11 tests), `rc_mover_check` on all 79 maps, `rc_walk --map geo_05a --ride --script`, `rc_walk --script` in the test room, `cargo build --workspace`.
- **Found in the game's data:** RC keeps one time per step (`MoveTimes[]`), has its own states (`TriggerLoop`, `Locked`, `Looping`, `None`) and four events (`OpeningEvent`, `OpenedEvent`, `ClosingEvent`, `ClosedEvent`). Movers have up to 24 keys. Double doors take their values from `MarkerProperties.u`. Engine triggers fire once by default.
- **Result:** All 79 maps: 941 movers (877 with collision), 1,675 triggers, no unknown states; 863 of 863 movers opened and closed in their `MoveTimes` (72 over 60 s not run); 33 of 33 lift rides passed (25 skipped where the level is in the way). Walker on geo_05a: the player rode `Mover37` 12,176 units up in 18 s, 5 of 5 checks passed. Unit tests 11 passed, 0 failed: a triggered door opens in 1.50 s (MoveTime 1.5), waits 2 s, closes in 1.50 s; a lift carries the player up 400 units and back down, feet never more than 1 unit off its top; a closing door turns back when the player stands in it; walking into a bump door opens it and the player gets through. Test room: `SCRIPT DONE: 21 checks passed, 0 failed`.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows. Not compared with the real game by eye. Sounds are logged, not played. 472 movers wait for a tag nothing in the map sends (scripts, squad orders), and `Locked`/`None` doors stay shut, since we don't run the game's scripts. Triggers that other triggers switch on and off aren't handled. `bAutoDoor` isn't acted on. Turning movers collide approximately. The smooth easing curve is our reading of UE2, not measured.
- **Next:** Scripted doors (the game's scripted sequences and squad door orders), then sounds.

It reads each map's squad markers and squad-mate spawners. New settings file `data/squad/squad.toml` with every number, editable by mods. New crate `crates/rc_squad_plugin`: `SquadPlugin` for the game program (orders from `ActionState`, collision from `LevelCollision`, writes `SquadReadout`, logs to `GameLog`) and the `rc_squad_sim` example, a windowless test run on a real map. Routes come from the enemies thread's `rc_nav`.

Then `cargo run -p rc_squad_plugin --example rc_squad_sim` on geo_01a (map, static meshes and script packages copied read-only from the install into the cloud sandbox): the scripted player drops from the gunship, presses F2, runs, aims at the floor and presses F3, walks off, presses F4, F1, F2, then goes to the nearest bacta dispenser and orders it, and finally takes all of Sev's health and walks away.

Numbers from `logs/rc_squad_sim.log`: after a 3 s run the squad set off once 837-880 uu behind (the 768 uu tether) and stopped 193-208 uu from the player; secure position: all within 129 uu of the spot, still there after the player walked 1,064 uu away; cancel: back within 206 uu; Scorch crossed 3,751 uu of the map by the path network to SquadMarkerSpot1 in 12.3 s, worked 3 s, finished, and the squad regrouped within 337 uu. Sev at 0 health: shown down on the squad panel, moved 0 uu while the player walked off, and Fixer and Scorch followed to within 164 uu.

Squad mates have health and shields (`rc_combat`) and go down at 0, but don't shoot yet, and nothing revives them. Search and destroy only moves towards enemies the game tells it about (`SquadThreats`), and nothing tells it yet. Marked jobs don't do anything to the world (no charge appears, no healing). Squad mates don't bump into each other or the player. The formation slots, the "stop when stuck" rule and the 3 s job time are our own choices. A route on geo_01a through a BlockedPath (a path the level opens later) gets stuck at a barricade; reported to the enemies thread. Sev stopped 41 uu short of one secure spot on a raised rock (logged as "stopped short").

While the game is loading it reads the chosen map's collision with `rc_level::map_collision::load` (BSP, props, props and double doors whose model comes from their class, blocking volumes), inserts `LevelCollision` and `PlayerStart`, draws the map with the level viewer's code, and spawns the sky camera (camera order -1) and makes the player's camera let it through (`SkyViewer`, no clearing). Everything it spawns is removed when leaving the level. `rc_game` uses it, and the temporary `stand_in_level.rs` is deleted. `rc_game --script` gained 3 checks: the level's counts, the sky camera, and a screenshot looking up 60 degrees. An optional `levels/lighting.cfg` in a data folder sets brightness. `docs/DESIGN.md` and `docs/PLUGINS.md` describe it. Built on the game shell branch (PR #11) with the levels branch (PR #14) merged in.

As a control, one script run with the sky camera switched off.
- **Result:** Unit tests 21 passed, 0 failed. Script: `SCRIPT DONE: 20 checks passed, 0 failed`, exit code 0. geo_01a: 149,767 collision triangles, 380 props (25 with their class's model), 27 blocking volumes, start feet (2102.0, 1829.6, -2081.8), loaded in 0.4 s and drawn in 2.3 s (debug build). Looking up: 72% of the screen lit with the sky, average colour of the top third (144, 117, 85), an orange-brown; with the sky camera off 17% (the game clears to black), so the check fails as it should. Walking 1 s moved 376 units. All 6 other maps loaded with a sky, a player start from the map and 0 unreadable props or volumes (for example ras_04c: 465,033 triangles, 98 class-model props, 3.7 s to draw). After merging the shell's Hor+ field of view: unit tests 23 passed; script 20 of 20 at 1280x720 and at 3440x1440 ultrawide (117.3 degrees across; looking up 65% lit).

Doors stand closed and don't open; the 9 maps with sky windows but no sky box show black there. Music wasn't copied to the sandbox this time, so it wasn't played. Not compared with the real game by eye.

## 2026-10-29: The sky (rc_level_viewer, rc_walk)

- **Changed:** New `rc_level_viewer/src/sky.rs`: a second camera stands at the map's `SkyZoneInfo`, inside its small sky box, and turns with the player's view without moving. It draws first; the player's camera draws the level over it without clearing. `scene.rs` puts the sky zone's polygons and props on their own render layer and leaves out "fake backdrop" polygons (the windows onto the sky). Found the zone of a point with a new `Bsp::point_zone` (UE2's tree walk). The viewer has `--pitch DEG` and `--no-sky`; `rc_walk --map` gets the sky too. `spawn_level` now also returns every entity it spawned.

- **Result:** 67 of 79 maps have a sky box (each 6-12 polygons with textures like `BigSkyPanel`, `SkyPanel`, `Stars`). Looking up in geo_01a, the window was 41% background colour without the sky and 0% with it; ctf_siege 85% and 0%; yyy_01b 18% and 0%. The geo_01a capture shows an orange Geonosis sky above the spires. Walker: 3 passed, 0 failed on geo_01a; 21 passed, 0 failed in the test room.

- **Next:** Doors and lifts.

## 2026-10-29: Invisible walls (blocking volumes) are solid

- **Changed:** New `rc_level::volumes` reads every `BlockingVolume`: its brush shape (a small BSP model) placed with `PrePivot`, `Rotation` and `Location`, plus whether it stops players, the AI and shots. `rc_level::collision::add_blocking_volumes` adds the ones that stop players to the collision; `rc_level::map_collision::load` (used by `rc_walk`) now does this. The viewer and `rc_level_check` log the volumes and two checks. New `crates/rc_walk/examples/volume_walk_test.rs` runs a commando into volumes with the game's movement.
- **Why:** The level designers used these to keep the player in bounds. Without them you could walk off cliffs and through gaps the real game blocks.
- **Tested how:** `rc_level_check` on all 79 maps; `cargo run --release -p rc_walk --example volume_walk_test -- GAMEDATA geo_01a geo_01b ras_01a yyy_01b geo_04c ras_04c`; `rc_walk --map geo_01a --script`; 2 new unit tests.
- **Result:** 1,553 volumes in 67 maps, all read, all with scale 1. Only 11 of 8,942 AI path points are inside a volume (107 if `PrePivot` is ignored, 115 if turned a quarter wrong), and 974 volumes sit within 512 units of a path point (145 if left unplaced). Walk test: of 70 runs at a volume, 0 got inside with volumes in the collision and 18 got inside without. `rc_walk --map geo_01a --script`: 3 passed, 0 failed.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Volumes aren't drawn (they're invisible in the game too). Shots and the AI don't use them yet; the flags are there for those threads. Not tested on Windows.
- **Next:** The sky.

## 2026-10-29: Every map loads; lightmaps fixed on 11 more maps (rc_level_check)

- **Changed:** New program `rc_level_check` (in `crates/rc_level_viewer/src/bin/`) builds every map the way the viewer does, without a window, and logs each one to `logs/rc_level_check.log`. Fixed the lightmap reader (`rc_level/src/lightmap.rs`): each lightmap entry ends with a compact index and a 4-byte number, not five compact indexes. `ConstantColor` and `FadeColor` materials now show as a flat colour. Map collision loading moved from `rc_walk/src/level.rs` to `rc_level::map_collision` so the droid and squad work can share it.
- **Why:** Only `geo_01a` and a few others had been tried. The goal is every level.
- **Tested how:** Copied all 79 maps plus the texture, static mesh and script packages from the install (read only) into the cloud sandbox and ran `rc_level_check`.
- **Result:** `79 of 79 maps loaded, 0 failed, in 13.2 s` (release build). Before the fix 12 maps had unreadable lightmaps (`shadow bits count ... doesn't fit`); after it 78 read and pass every lightmap check (each corner lands in its own rectangle). Missing materials dropped from 47 to 36, of which 34 are surfaces with no material at all.
- **Still broken / not tested:** `entry.ctm` (the 6-polygon boot map) still has unreadable lightmaps: its page has no pixels and the bytes after it don't match. 21 maps have a few BSP corners 1-7 units off their plane (the check allows 1); they draw fine as far as the numbers show. One `TexPanner2D` and one odd `TexPanner` aren't shown. Not tested on Windows.
- **Next:** Invisible walls, then the sky.

## 2026-10-29: Any screen shape, dual input, stick curves; menus marked as placeholders

- **Changed:** `rc_game`'s field of view is now "Hor+": `video.fov` is the angle across a 4:3 screen (the original's 85), turned into a fixed angle up and down, so wider screens see more at the sides instead of losing the top and bottom. The HUD and menus are sized by the screen's smaller side (`Val::VMin`, `FontSize::VMin`), and the HUD stays in a centred 16:9 area on wider screens (new `video.hud_max_aspect`). Sticks: the dead zone now stretches the rest of the range back to 0..1, and a new `controls.stick_look_curve` (default 1.5) gives finer aim near the middle. `rc_game_core::ActionState` gained `last_device` (keyboard and mouse, gamepad or touch) for button prompts. The amber menus and HUD are marked as placeholders in the code, `data/ui/theme.toml`, README and DESIGN. New `docs/ORIGINAL_MENUS.md` (how the original builds its menus and HUD, and the plan to use them) and `docs/DISPLAY_AND_INPUT.md` (screen shapes, controllers, gyro).
- **Why:** New project goals from the user: any resolution and aspect ratio with proper ultrawide, dual input, wide controller support and gyro aiming; and use the original game's menus as much as possible.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_game -p rc_game_core` (new tests for the field of view numbers and the stick shaping). `rc_game --script` in the cloud sandbox at 1024x768, 1280x720, 1720x720, 2560x720 and 540x960, with screenshots. For the menus: read the install's `XInterfaceCTMenus.u`, `XInterface.u`, `CTGame.u`, `GUIContent.utx`, `WarfareFonts.utx`, `OrbitFonts.utx` and `HUDTextures.utx` with a throwaway tool built on `rc_package` (not committed).
- **Result:** Tests 18 passed. The script passed 17 of 17 at all five sizes. Logged view angles: 85.0, 101.4, 117.3, 135.5 and 42.3 degrees across, 69.0 up and down every time. Screenshots: on 32:9 the HUD sits in the centred 16:9 area; on the upright phone shape the menu and HUD fit inside the screen. Menus: `CTMenuMain`'s 34 default values read cleanly, including "MAIN MENU" at 0.24, 0.575 and its eight buttons; the HUD (`CTHUD`) is drawn by native code, so only its textures can be reused.
- **Still broken / not tested:** At 32:9 the edges of the 3D view are visibly stretched (normal for a 135-degree view; needs an optional Panini view). No real gamepad, touch screen or gyro tested. No gyro support (gilrs can't read gyros). No in-game rebinding screen.
- **Next:** Separate threads: the original's menus (plan in `docs/ORIGINAL_MENUS.md`), gyro and controller pictures (`docs/DISPLAY_AND_INPUT.md`).

## 2026-10-29: Recoil message, and a trial run with the weapons and squad parts

- **Changed:** `rc_game_core` gained `ViewKick` (weapon recoil: pitch and yaw in radians); `rc_game`'s player adds it to the view. `docs/PLUGINS.md` lists it.
- **Why:** The weapons part sends recoil per shot and needs something to turn the view without depending on the player code.
- **Tested how:** In a throwaway copy (not pushed), merged this branch with the weapons branch (`rc_combat`, commit 285dd3e) and the squad branch (`rc_squad_plugin`, PR #13), added both plugins to `rc_game`, and ran `rc_game --script`. Then `cargo test` and the script again on this branch alone.
- **Result:** Together: `SCRIPT DONE: 17 checks passed, 0 failed`. The display showed `DC-17M BLASTER 60 | 60`, health 200, shield 100, and Sev, Fixer and Scorch at 100% on "Form up" (screenshot checked). Only conflicts were the workspace member list, MODLOG and DESIGN (both sides add lines). This branch alone afterwards: 16 unit tests and the script, 17 passed, 0 failed.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Recoil isn't sprung back yet. Firing wasn't exercised by the script. The other parts are wired in only after their pull requests are merged.
- **Next:** After merging, add the four plugins to `rc_game` (one line each) and remove the stand-in level.

## 2026-10-29: Android compile check, difficulty setting (rc_game)

- **Changed:** `crates/rc_game/Cargo.toml` gained two Android-only settings (BLAKE3 without C code, Bevy's native Android start-up). New `docs/ANDROID.md`: what works on Linux and what an Android app still needs. `settings.toml` gained `[game] difficulty` (easy, medium, hard; the original's damage factors 0.25, 1, 1.75), also in the options menu.
- **Why:** The port must run on Linux and Android; check how far an Android build gets. Difficulty is needed by the weapons part.
- **Tested how:** `cargo check --target aarch64-linux-android -p rc_game` in the cloud sandbox. Then `cargo test -p rc_game` and `rc_game --script` again.
- **Result:** The Android check first failed in `blake3` (needs an ARM C compiler) and then in `android-activity` (needs a start-up feature); with both settings it finished with no errors. Tests 11 passed; script 17 passed, 0 failed.
- **Still broken / not tested:** No Android app: nothing linked, packaged or run on a phone (needs the Android NDK, a library entry point, a folder picker and app-folder paths; listed in `docs/ANDROID.md`). Wayland window: built, not run.
- **Next:** Wire in the other parts' plugins.

## 2026-10-29: The game program: menus, settings, controls, display, music (rc_game)

- **Changed:** New crate `crates/rc_game`, the one game program the other parts plug into. Files: `settings.rs` (the player's `settings.toml`, written with defaults and notes on first run), `input.rs` (devices to actions, from `data/input/bindings.toml`: the original PC keys from `DefaultCtrl.ini`, a gamepad layout, touch), `player.rs` (`rc_movement` body and a separate eye camera), `levels.rs` (mission names and order read at run time from the game's `LevelLoadingInfo.int`, grouped by `data/levels/campaigns.toml`), `menu.rs` (main, campaign, levels, options, pause, loading screen), `hud.rs` (visor brackets, crosshair, shield and health, ammo, squad, messages), `music.rs` (the game's `.ogg` music, chosen by `data/music/music.toml`), `theme.rs` (`data/ui/theme.toml`), `stand_in_level.rs` (loads maps like `rc_walk --map` until the level part plugs in), `script.rs` (`--script` self-test). `rc_game_core` gained `PlayerStart` and `MusicMood`. Wayland window support is on alongside X11. `.gitignore` unchanged: the new data files are `.toml` under `data/`.
- **Why:** The overnight plan's fifth part: the game itself, so levels, weapons, enemies and squad have one program to plug into, and the game folder is a setting instead of a Windows path.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_game -p rc_game_core` (16 tests). In the cloud sandbox, with the needed map, texture, mesh and music files copied read-only from the install (outside the repo), ran `xvfb-run rc_game --script` twice.
- **Result:** Both runs: `SCRIPT DONE: 17 checks passed, 0 failed`, exit code 0. The game's list gave 77 maps (2 shown in the sandbox, which only had `pro` and `geo_01a`). `geo_01a`: 139,481 collision triangles, drawn in 2.1 s (debug build, software rendering); walking forward for one second moved 430 units; paused, 0.000 units. Main menu screenshot 0.71% amber; level screenshot 64% lit with 96 amber crosshair pixels at the centre. Menu music and the prologue track decoded and started on a fake ("null") sound card.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows, with a gamepad, with a touch screen, on Wayland or on Android. Music heard: not tested (the sandbox has no speakers). The prologue's player start is in the air (it's a cut scene in the original), so the player falls. No weapons, enemies or squad until those parts plug in. Options saved from the menu lose the first-run file's notes.
- **Next:** Plug in the other parts as they land, check an Android build, run on the user's PC.

## 2026-10-29: Battle droid decision making and a headless droid test (rc_ai, rc_droidsim)

- **Changed:** New crate `crates/rc_ai` (no Bevy): `params.rs` reads `data/enemies/battle_droid.toml` (new, every number marked "game" or "guess"); `spawns.rs` reads the map's spawners (`PawnFactory`), their spawn points and walk-in `Trigger`s; `brain.rs` is one fighter: notice the enemy (sight radius, view cone, "notice anyway" radius, line of sight), react, keep track of it, hear shots, take cover facing the enemy when shot in the open (crouch between bursts), move for a clear shot, keep between ready and safe distance, fire bursts with aim error from its accuracy, forget after 15 s. New tool `rc_droidsim [MAP]`: droids against a stand-in player on a real map, no window, log in `logs/rc_droidsim.log`. `rc_nav`: `BlockedPath` nodes start blocked (class default `bBlocked` is true), so routes avoid barricades; `set_blocked_by_tag` opens them.
- **Why:** The enemies part of the overnight plan. Plain Rust so it can be tested without a window and reused by the game plugin.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_ai` (8 tests: settings file equals the built-in values, typo errors name the line, spots then fires bursts after the reaction time with aim error within 6 degrees, no seeing through walls or behind its back, turns towards a heard shot, forgets after 15 s, takes the cover point that faces the enemy and crouches between bursts, aim scatter stays in its cone). `cargo test -p rc_nav` (7 tests, one new for blocked nodes). `rc_droidsim geo_01a --seconds 60` in release mode on the real map copied read-only into the cloud sandbox.
- **Result:** All tests pass. geo_01a: 141,477 collision triangles, 36 battle droid spawners (9 with walk-in triggers), the stand-in walks into the SpawnGunshipArea trigger at 5.4 s, 5 droids spawn there plus 1 from a nearby scripted spawner, first spotted 5.4 s, first hit 5.9 s, 14 droid shots (3 hit, accuracy 0.4 at 1,800-2,800 uu), 32 routes walked (25,764 uu), every droid stays on the floor, 0.7 ms average and 4.5 ms worst per frame for all droids and the player. All 7 CHECK lines pass.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Level scripts aren't run: 27 of 36 droid spawners start only from scripts, so they switch on when the player comes within 3,000 uu (a stand-in rule), and the 6 barricade path points are opened by the test. No droid reached a cover point in the geo_01a run (none near that fight face the player); cover is only proven by the unit test. The original's actual decision rules are compiled code we don't read; ours are written from its settings and named states. "Guess" values in the data file (reaction time, aim error, burst rest, advance step, hearing radius) are not compared with the game. Not run on Windows. No Bevy yet.
- **Next:** The Bevy plugin (`rc_enemies`): animated droids in the game, shots through the weapons thread's `FireShot`, damage and death through its `Damage` and `Died`.

## 2026-10-29: Path network and route finding for computer characters (rc_nav)

- **Changed:** New crate `crates/rc_nav` (no Bevy): reads a map's path nodes and the editor's links between them ("reach specs"), finds shortest routes (A*), and turns a route into the same move input the player uses, so droids and squad mates walk with the player's physics (`rc_movement`). New tool `rc_navcheck [MAP]` logs and checks a map's network (`logs/rc_navcheck.log`). New `crates/rc_package/src/class_library.rs`: searches several script packages together and merges a class's defaults with its parents' (on top of the weapons thread's `class_defaults.rs`). One line added to `class_defaults.rs`: a list with an array position of 256 or more is a false start (fixes CTBot's list).
- **Why:** Enemies and the squad both need to walk the level's paths. Built once, shared (agreed with the squad thread).
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_nav` (6 tests: shortest walkable route, fly-only links skipped, unreachable node, avoiding a node, nearest node on the same floor, a droid-sized body walking up the test room's stairs along a route). `rc_navcheck` on geo_01a, geo_01b and geo_01c copied read-only from the install into the cloud sandbox.
- **Result:** 6 passed. geo_01a: 358 nodes, 1,224 links, 0 unreadable, every link's stored distance within 1 uu of the straight line between its nodes, biggest walkable island 257 nodes, 5 long routes (up to 127 nodes, 46,783 uu) in 0.8 ms. geo_01b: 386 nodes, 1,371 links, one island. geo_01c: 180 nodes, 659 links. CTBot now gives EnemyMemoryTime 15, DecisionInterval 0.5, CoverBoredomTime 15 instead of a junk list.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Link flag 4096 (seen on 7-9 links per map) is Republic Commando's own; meaning unknown, treated as walkable. Jump links are treated as walkable but nothing jumps yet. Not run on Windows.
- **Next:** Battle droid brain (rc_ai) using these routes, then the Bevy plugin.

The weapon sounds now load straight from the game's `.uax` packages. Gun parts without a texture are drawn grey instead of left out. New test `real_game_sounds_and_model` (runs only when `RC_GAME_DIR` is set). Docs: `docs/DESIGN.md` and `docs/RESEARCH.md` got a weapons section, README says how to run it.
- **Why:** To see and hear the weapons working with the real game files, and to check them without a person watching.

- **Result:** `script done: 12 of 12 checks passed`. 5 sounds loaded; the DC-17m model has 2 parts and the pistol 1, all textured. The picture shows the textured DC-17m in the lower right and the crosshair on the droid; the gun's corner has 590 colours and no magenta. The far droid's 2 hits took the shield from 100 to 52 and left health at 200. 22 tests pass.

## 2026-10-29: Weapons, shots, health and damage (rc_combat)

- **Changed:** New crate `crates/rc_combat` with `CombatPlugin` (= `HealthPlugin` + `WeaponsPlugin`), weapon files `data/weapons/dc17m_blaster.toml`, `dc15s_pistol.toml`, `e5_blaster.toml`, `e5_blaster_tm.toml`, and the player's numbers in `data/combat/player.toml`. The player fires from the input actions (fire, reload, next/previous weapon); bolts fly with the game's acceleration and top speed, hit the level or a character's cylinder, and send `Damage`; shields take damage first and recharge after a delay; `Died` is sent at 0 health. Anyone (droids, squad) can fire with a `FireShot` message. Took the enemies thread's one-line fix to `class_defaults.rs` so both branches match.
- **Why:** Overnight goal 2: fire the blaster with the game's own numbers, and a shared health and damage system for the enemies and squad.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_combat`: 13 rule tests and 8 tests that run the plugin in a Bevy app with no window, in the movement test room.
- **Result:** 21 passed. The DC-17m fires 10 shots a second, a magazine of 60 then a 1.5 s reload; 5 hits of 20 take a 100-health droid down; a bolt reaches a droid 830 uu away in 10 frames; shots land within the game's aim error (15 uu per 1000 uu); the pistol fires once per press and recharges 1.5 shots a second; 5 droid bolts of 24 take the player's 100 shield and 20 health, and the shield waits 3 s then refills at 20 a second.

Not done: hit-location and damage-type multipliers, melee, grenades, the weapon's switch and draw times. Not run on Windows.
- **Next:** A test range program with sound and the model, run on the real game files.

## 2026-10-29: Read a script class's default values (rc_probe --class)

- **Changed:** New `crates/rc_package/src/class_defaults.rs` (finds the default values stored at the end of a class in a `.u` script package) and `rc_probe --class NAME` (`crates/rc_probe/src/classes.rs`), which logs the defaults of a class and of each parent to `logs/rc_class.log`.
- **Why:** The weapon numbers (fire rate, damage, ammo, spread) are class defaults. The movement thread found them by hand; this makes it a repeatable tool.
- **Tested how:** One unit test on a made-up class. Then ran it on the real `ctinventory.u`, `engine.u`, `ctgame.u`, `ctcharacters.u`, `gameplay.u` and `core.u`, copied read-only from the install into the cloud sandbox.
- **Result:** `rc_probe --class DC17mBlaster` gives 37 values for DC17mBlaster, 41 for Weapon, 12 for Inventory, 30 for Actor. It also reproduces the movement thread's hand-found numbers (CloneCommando Health 100, Shields 100). Classes found: every weapon, ammo and projectile class tried (about 30).
- **Still broken / not tested:** Engine's `Weapon` class shows one junk value at the front (`OldSamples[2924]`): the method can't always tell where the list starts. Values of structs other than vectors and rotators are shown as raw bytes. Not run on Windows.
- **Next:** Put the weapon numbers into data files and fire them.

## 2026-10-29: Shared game pieces for every part to plug into (rc_game_core)

- **Changed:** New crate `crates/rc_game_core` and `docs/PLUGINS.md`. The crate holds what the whole game shares: the screens (`GameState`: boot, main menu, loading, in game; `Play`: running or paused), the folders (`GamePaths`, with mods searched before `data/`, and file names matched without caring about case for Linux and Android), the input actions (`Action`, `ActionState`), the numbers the on-screen display shows (health, ammo, squad), loading hand-off (`LoadingGate`), level collision, the player and camera markers, unit conversion and one shared log.
- **Why:** Five parts of the game are being built at the same time (levels, weapons, enemies, squad, menus). They need one agreed way to plug into a single game program without calling each other.
- **Tested how:** `cargo test -p rc_game_core` (5 tests: action names, press/release tracking, unit conversion, finding files with mods first and any letter case, and pausing stopping gameplay without leaving the level).
- **Result:** 5 passed, 0 failed.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Nothing uses it yet. The game program itself (`rc_game`) is the next step.
- **Next:** `rc_game` with the settings file, menus, input bindings, on-screen display and music.

## 2026-10-29: Props and doors that take their model from their class (rc_level, rc_level_viewer, rc_walk)

- **Changed:** New `rc_level::class_look`: when a map gives an actor no `StaticMesh`, it reads the actor's class defaults (walking the class and its parents across script packages such as `Properties/StaticPropProperties.u`, `KarmaPropProperties.u` and `CTInventory.u`) and fills in `StaticMesh`, `DrawScale`, `DrawScale3D` and `bHidden` where the map leaves them out. Classes whose `DrawType` isn't "static mesh" are left alone. The level viewer, `rc_level_check` and `rc_level::map_collision` (so `rc_walk`) use it. Added `rc_package::class_defaults`, copied unchanged from the weapons thread (commit f4d8880) so both branches carry the same file.
- **Why:** Most props (barrels, lamps, wall pieces, bacta dispensers) and the double-door movers (`MoverDB_*`) store their model only in their class, so they were missing from the levels and you could walk through where they stand.
- **Tested how:** `rc_level_check` on all 79 maps, before and after; `rc_walk --map geo_01a --script`; `rc_walk --script`.
- **Result:** 3,460 more props across the 79 maps (27,521 to 30,981; drawn 27,446 to 30,234), every one of their models read, 1.1 million more triangles. `79 of 79 maps loaded, 0 failed`. Floor test: 7,853 of 8,942 path points have a floor below (7,850 before); 4 more points in geo_03a, yyy_01c and yyy_01d now have something closer than 80 units below (28 in all, 24 before), most likely AI paths that run over one of the new props. Walker: 3 passed, 0 failed on geo_01a (25 props there use their class's model); 21 passed, 0 failed in the test room.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not compared with the real game by eye. The doors stand closed where the map puts them and don't open yet. Not tested on Windows.
- **Next:** Doors that open, and the level plugin for the game.

## 2026-10-29: The sky (rc_level_viewer, rc_walk)

- **Changed:** New `rc_level_viewer/src/sky.rs`: a second camera stands at the map's `SkyZoneInfo`, inside its small sky box, and turns with the player's view without moving. It draws first; the player's camera draws the level over it without clearing. `scene.rs` puts the sky zone's polygons and props on their own render layer and leaves out "fake backdrop" polygons (the windows onto the sky). Found the zone of a point with a new `Bsp::point_zone` (UE2's tree walk). The viewer has `--pitch DEG` and `--no-sky`; `rc_walk --map` gets the sky too. `spawn_level` now also returns every entity it spawned.

- **Result:** 67 of 79 maps have a sky box (each 6-12 polygons with textures like `BigSkyPanel`, `SkyPanel`, `Stars`). Looking up in geo_01a, the window was 41% background colour without the sky and 0% with it; ctf_siege 85% and 0%; yyy_01b 18% and 0%. The geo_01a capture shows an orange Geonosis sky above the spires. Walker: 3 passed, 0 failed on geo_01a; 21 passed, 0 failed in the test room.

Not compared with the real game. Not tested on Windows. The sky camera draws the whole sky layer every frame (small: 1-2 draw batches).
- **Next:** Doors and lifts.

## 2026-10-29: Every map loads; lightmaps fixed on 11 more maps (rc_level_check)

- **Changed:** New program `rc_level_check` (in `crates/rc_level_viewer/src/bin/`) builds every map the way the viewer does, without a window, and logs each one to `logs/rc_level_check.log`. Fixed the lightmap reader (`rc_level/src/lightmap.rs`): each lightmap entry ends with a compact index and a 4-byte number, not five compact indexes. `ConstantColor` and `FadeColor` materials now show as a flat colour. Map collision loading moved from `rc_walk/src/level.rs` to `rc_level::map_collision` so the droid and squad work can share it.
- **Why:** Only `geo_01a` and a few others had been tried. The goal is every level.
- **Tested how:** Copied all 79 maps plus the texture, static mesh and script packages from the install (read only) into the cloud sandbox and ran `rc_level_check`.
- **Result:** `79 of 79 maps loaded, 0 failed, in 13.2 s` (release build). Before the fix 12 maps had unreadable lightmaps (`shadow bits count ... doesn't fit`); after it 78 read and pass every lightmap check (each corner lands in its own rectangle). Missing materials dropped from 47 to 36, of which 34 are surfaces with no material at all.
- **Still broken / not tested:** `entry.ctm` (the 6-polygon boot map) still has unreadable lightmaps: its page has no pixels and the bytes after it don't match. 21 maps have a few BSP corners 1-7 units off their plane (the check allows 1); they draw fine as far as the numbers show. One `TexPanner2D` and one odd `TexPanner` aren't shown. Not tested on Windows.
- **Next:** Invisible walls, then the sky.

## 2026-10-29: Automatic build checks on GitHub (CI)

- **Changed:** New `.github/workflows/ci.yml`. `.gitignore` gained one whitelist line, `!.github/workflows/*.yml`. A short section at the end of `docs/DESIGN.md`.
- **Why:** So every pull request is built and tested on Windows and Linux automatically, which catches breakage when several branches are merged.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud sandbox with no game folder set: `cargo test --workspace` (all 57 unit tests pass, none read game files, so no test needed a skip). `cargo fmt --all --check` and `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` both find problems today (72 files need reformatting, about 20 clippy messages), which is why that job only reports. The run on GitHub itself is checked on the draft pull request.
- **Result:** First run on draft PR #15: Linux build and test passed (about 6 min), Windows passed (about 12 min, first run without a cache), formatting and clippy job reported its problems without failing.
- **Still broken / not tested:** The Bevy programs' windowed `--script` checks are not run on GitHub. No Android build. Not tested on macOS (not a goal).
- **Next:** Once the code is formatted and the clippy messages are fixed, make that job fail the run too.

## 2026-10-29: The walker draws levels textured and lit (rc_walk, rc_level_viewer)

- **Changed:** `rc_level_viewer` is now also a library (`src/lib.rs`). Its drawing code moved out of `main.rs` into a new `src/draw.rs` (`spawn_level`, `BACKGROUND`) with no change in what it does. `rc_walk --map` uses it, so the map has its textures, baked light and real-time lights instead of plain grey. The camera now uses the same exposure, no tonemapping, and a far distance of 5000 Bevy units, like the viewer. The test room keeps its own sun and ambient light. If the look can't be built, rc_walk falls back to the grey collision mesh and logs why.
- **Why:** On the user's PC the walk showed a grey foreground and a white background. The walking itself worked: the PC's log shows a 1154 uu/s landing from the gunship and 0.01 ms of physics per frame at 144 frames per second.
- **Tested how:** In the cloud sandbox, using `geo_01a` plus 15 texture packages copied read-only from the install. Ran `rc_walk --map geo_01a --frames 400` and took screenshots. Re-ran `rc_walk --script` (room), `rc_walk --map geo_01a --script`, and `rc_level_viewer`.
- **Result:** `map look built in 1449 ms (61 draw batches, 2 lightmap pages, 31 lights)`. Drawn: 141,477 triangles, 60 batches textured, 8 with a lightmap and 50 with baked corner colours. The screenshot shows textured rock and sand floor with baked shading.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows yet. The map is read twice, once for drawing and once for collision (about 1.5 s in the debug build). There is still no sky.

## 2026-10-29: Walking a real level, geo_01a (rc_walk)

- **Changed:** New `crates/rc_walk/src/level.rs` loads a map's collision (BSP polygons plus every visible static mesh prop, via `rc_level::collision`) and its PlayerStart. `rc_walk` gained `--map NAME|path.ctm`, and `--script` with a map walks a square (with a jump and a crouch) and checks it stays on the ground. Fix in `rc_movement/src/world.rs`: a move that only grazes a surface no longer counts as hitting it, with a new unit test (`full_jump_while_pushing_into_a_wall`). README and DESIGN updated.
- **Why:** Roadmap step 5: walk a real level in first person.
- **Tested how:** 21 unit tests in rc_movement. In the cloud sandbox (Xvfb, software rendering, debug build), `rc_walk --script` (test room) and `rc_walk --map geo_01a --script` with `geo_01a.ctm` and its static mesh packages copied read-only from the user's install.
- **Result:** Room: `SCRIPT DONE: 21 checks passed, 0 failed`. geo_01a: loaded in 152 ms (249 BSP nodes, 355 props, 0 unreadable), collision grid built in 43 ms, `SCRIPT DONE: 3 checks passed, 0 failed`. Physics took 0.37 ms per frame on average, 5.2 ms at worst. The first walk found a bug: jumping while pressing into a wall rose only 87 of 103 units, because each slide along the wall "hit" the wall again. After the fix the jump on the map peaks about 101 units up in the log's samples (taken every 6 frames; the unit test gets 102.6). The PlayerStart is about 690 units up, inside the gunship, which isn't solid yet, so the player first drops to the floor (lands at 1154 uu/s, no damage).
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or by hand with keyboard and mouse. Not solid yet: BlockingVolumes (27 in geo_01a), movers and vehicles. Per-prop collision settings are ignored. The map is drawn plain grey (no textures or lights in rc_walk). Only geo_01a tried.
- **Next:** BlockingVolumes, then sharing the textured, lit level drawing with `rc_level_viewer`, then the blaster.

## 2026-10-29: Collision against triangles, ready for real levels (rc_movement)

- **Changed:** New `crates/rc_movement/src/triangles.rs`: `TriangleWorld`, a `World` made of loose triangles (what the level reader produces for BSP polygons and static meshes), filed in a 256-unit grid so each sweep only looks at nearby triangles. The plane sweep in `world.rs` was split out so both worlds share it. DESIGN updated.
- **Why:** Real levels are triangles, not boxes. This lets the movement code walk a map once `rc_level` (draft PR #8) is merged, without changing the movement code.
- **Tested how:** 3 new unit tests (20 in rc_movement, all passing): a box dropped on a single triangle, and the test room cut into triangles run lane by lane next to the original box version (30 and 40 steps, stairs, both ramps, low ceiling standing and crouched).
- **Result:** Landing on a triangle hits at exactly the expected point (time 0.5). In every lane, the player ends within 1 uu of where the box version puts it.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on a real map yet (needs PR #8), and speed with 141,477 triangles (geo_01a) is not measured. Not tested on Windows.
- **Next:** After PR #8 merges: load geo_01a's collision triangles into `rc_walk` and walk it, logging speed per step.

## 2026-10-29: First-person movement with the game's own numbers (rc_movement, rc_walk)

- **Changed:** New crate `crates/rc_movement` (player walking, running, strafing, jumping, falling, crouching, step and slope handling, box collision; plain Rust, no Bevy) and `crates/rc_walk` (Bevy 3D program: first-person test room, keyboard/mouse or `--script` input). New settings file `data/movement/player_commando.toml`, and a `.gitignore` line allowing `data/**/*.toml`. Workspace `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock` updated. Research written up in `docs/RESEARCH.md` ("Player movement"); design in `docs/DESIGN.md`; README updated.
- **Why:** Roadmap step 5: walk a level in first person. Built against a test room first, since level loading is being done in parallel.

Ran `rc_walk --script` in the sandbox (Xvfb, software rendering); it drives the player through the test room and checks 21 results. Also ran it with a misspelt settings file, and in keyboard mode for 30 frames.
- **Result:** Script: `SCRIPT DONE: 21 checks passed, 0 failed`. Run 450.00, walk 225.00, strafe 337.50, back 270.00, crouched 225.00 uu/s; stopped below 10 uu/s within 0.3 s (1.69); jump 102.56 high (expected 475²/2200 = 102.6); 30-high step climbed (feet 30.10), 40-high step blocked (0.10) and jumped onto (40.08); stairs 128.10; 30° ramp 200.10; 50° ramp not climbable; low ceiling blocks standing, crouching goes under and stays crouched; 700-high fall lands at 1241.02 uu/s (expected 1241) with 3.42 damage (expected 3.4). Camera matched the eye every logged frame. First version of the fall check failed because the small landing right after spawning was counted; fixed by taking the hardest landing, and landing speed is now measured at the moment of contact (it was 1246.7, off by one frame of gravity). A bad settings file stops with `line 2: unknown setting `jump``, exit code 1.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows (not built there; the Windows build can't be checked from the sandbox). Not tested by hand: nobody has walked it with a real keyboard and mouse. Guesses, because the game does them in compiled engine code: diagonal blend of the back/side speed limits, braking (Unreal Engine 2 style; the game's DecelRate of 600 is unused), mouse turning speed. Fall damage is reported, not applied. Only test-room collision; no level collision yet.

## 2026-10-29: Light a real level with its baked lightmaps and prop colours

- **Changed:** New `crates/rc_level/src/lightmap.rs` reads everything after the BSP vertex pool (zones, leaves, render sections, lightmap rectangles with their world-to-lightmap matrix, and the 512x512 lightmap pages) and works out each wall corner's place on its page; it has its own checks. `rc_level/src/actors.rs`: placements now carry the `StaticMeshInstance` reference, and `read_vertex_colours` reads a prop's baked corner colours. `rc_level_viewer`: walls get a second set of texture coordinates and Bevy's `Lightmap`; props get their corner colours; the map's dynamic lights are added; new `--lighting FILE` and `--capture FILE.bmp` options. `lighting.rs` moved from `rc_viewer` (where nothing used it) to `rc_level_viewer`, with new settings `baked_light`, `lightmap_gain`, `vertex_colour_gain`, and `live_lights` now defaulting to `dynamic`. DESIGN, README updated.
- **Why:** Roadmap step 2: light a real level.

Ran the reader and the viewer (Xvfb, software rendering) on `geo_01a`, `pro`, `ras_01a` and `yyy_01b`, and saved the checked frame to look at. All unit tests pass (`cargo test -p rc_level -p rc_level_viewer -p rc_viewer -p rc_package -p rc_image`); clippy is clean for the changed crates.

Rectangles / pages: 126/2, 81/2, 361/1, 182/2. `geo_01a`: `prop baked light: 355 drawn props have corner colours, 0 have none, 0 have a colour list of the wrong length`; 8 lightmapped batches, 50 coloured, 3 full-bright; 2 real-time lights, 29 left to the baked light. On-screen check PASS on all 4 maps. Average screen colour on `geo_01a` R 57 G 59 B 54 lit vs R 111 G 102 B 89 with `baked_light = false`; the captures show shaded rocks, dark cave ceilings and patches of light on `ras_01a`'s walls.
- **Windows run:** The user ran it on their PC (`logs\rc_level_viewer.log`): `lightmap checks: all passed`, 355 of 355 props with corner colours, level built in 259 ms, on-screen check PASS on the second capture with the same average colour as the cloud run (R 57 G 59 B 54), steady 144 frames per second while flying.

Shadow bitmaps and the pages' alpha channel are read past, not used. Only `geo_01a`'s props were checked (the other maps' mesh packages weren't copied).

## 2026-10-29: Load a whole level and fly around it (rc_level, rc_level_viewer)

- **Changed:** New crate `crates/rc_level` (no Bevy): `bsp.rs` reads a map's BSP model (the level's own walls and floors: vectors, points, nodes, surfaces, vertex pool) and turns each node into a polygon with texture coordinates; `actors.rs` reads the `Level` object (actor list, map URL, BSP model) and each actor's placement (Location, Rotation, DrawScale, DrawScale3D, PrePivot, StaticMesh, Skins, bHidden), plus the Unreal rotation maths; `collision.rs` gives world-space collision triangles (solid BSP plus placed static meshes, each with its source) for the first-person thread. 3 unit tests. New program `crates/rc_level_viewer`: loads one map (default `geo_01a`), draws the BSP and every placed static mesh with their textures, batched by material, with a free-fly camera starting at the PlayerStart. Logs to `logs/rc_level_viewer.log`. README and DESIGN updated.
- **Why:** Roadmap step 1: load a whole level and fly around it.
- **Tested how:** Copied 5 maps and the 15 packages `geo_01a` uses (read-only) into the cloud sandbox. Read the BSP and all actors of all 5 maps. Ran the viewer on `geo_01a` 4 times with Xvfb and software rendering. New checks: every BSP index in range, every polygon corner on its plane, the arrays end where the next begins; a "floor test" (PathNodes and PlayerStarts must have a floor a little below them and nothing inside them); an on-screen check (how much of the window is level, how many colours).
- **Result:** 5 of 5 maps: BSP checks all pass, all 3,042 actors read. `geo_01a`: 249 BSP polygons, 355 static mesh actors (60 different meshes), 141,477 triangles in 60 draw batches, 55 of 56 materials textured (the missing one's package wasn't copied). Floor test 292 of 293 points have a floor 0-150 units below (median 104), 0 closer than 80. Deliberately turning props the wrong way made 14-20 points land inside props, so the test notices. On-screen check PASS 4 of 4: 100% of the window covered, 576 colours.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows, or on maps other than `geo_01a` in the viewer. Not looked at by eye: the scene is only checked by numbers. Drawn unlit (no lights or lightmaps), no bump maps, no sky box treatment, no movers, characters, emitters or terrain. Two dead ends logged: a first state-frame layout that only fitted StaticMeshActors (the lighting thread found the right one: a 2-byte field, not 4), and on-screen captures that showed only the background colour (now retried like black ones). The real-world scale (50 Unreal units per Bevy unit) is still to be measured against a character.
- **Next:** Test on the user's PC; then lights and lightmaps (roadmap step 2).

## 2026-10-29: Fix the pink clone trooper in rc_character

- **Changed:** `crates/rc_character/src/main.rs`: the camera now uses `Tonemapping::None` (and `DebandDither::Disabled`). `crates/rc_character/src/check.rs`: the on-screen check now also compares the colour balance on screen with the skin picture's.
- **Why:** The user ran `rc_character` on Windows: the clone moved correctly but was pink. Cause (my mistake): when I switched `rc_character` to the same Bevy feature list as `rc_viewer` to share one build, I dropped `tonemapping_luts`. Bevy's default colour grading needs those tables and draws every lit pixel magenta without them. The outline check only compared shapes, so it still passed.
- **Tested how:** Read `logs\rc_character.log` from the user's PC: `character pixels' average colour R 253 G 1 B 254`, outline match 0.989 PASS. Reproduced it in the cloud sandbox (R 253 G 1 B 253, plus Bevy's log error "TonyMcMapFace tonemapping requires the `tonemapping_luts` feature"). Then fixed it and re-ran.
- **Result:** Clone R 167 G 166 B 165 on screen vs skin R 142 G 139 B 137; colour balance difference 0.005 (BattleDroid 0.013, SuperBattleDroid 0.001), all PASS. With the fix removed on purpose, the new check reports 0.331, `colour WRONG`, FAIL, exit code 1.
- **Windows re-run:** The user re-ran it on Windows ("he's beautiful"). Their log: `colour balance differs from the skin's by 0.005`, `on-screen check PASS: outline match 0.993 ..., colour ok`.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Nothing new.
- **Next:** Merge PR #9.

## 2026-10-29: Characters: skeletal meshes, animations, and an animated clone trooper in 3D

- **Changed:** New readers `crates/rc_package/src/skeletal.rs` (SkeletalMesh: bones, points, triangles, texture coordinates, bone weights) and `crates/rc_package/src/animation.rs` (MeshAnimation: sequences, packed key tracks). New crate `crates/rc_anim` (no Bevy): poses a skeleton from a sequence and moves the mesh's points with it (skinning). New `rc_probe --characters` mode (`crates/rc_probe/src/characters.rs`, logs to `logs/rc_characters.log`; `--meshes` is the static-mesh mode from PR #4). New 3D Bevy program `crates/rc_character`: shows one character with its skin texture, playing one animation, with an on-screen self-check. Small shared edits: `Reader::i16` in `reader.rs`, two `pub mod` lines in `rc_package/src/lib.rs`, workspace members in `Cargo.toml`.
- **Why:** Roadmap step 3: load a character with animation.
- **Tested how:** 6 new unit tests (all 23 unit tests in the workspace pass). Copied the 20 animation packages plus `clonetextures.utx` and `droidtextures.utx` from the install into the cloud sandbox and ran `rc_probe --characters` on them and `rc_character` under a virtual screen (software rendering). Not run on the user's PC yet.
- **Result:** `skeletal meshes: 131 read, 0 with problems, 0 failed`; `animation sets: 165 read, 2 with problems, 0 failed; 1414 sequences, 127873 frames`. Rotation convention checked by playing every sequence of every mesh (226 mesh/set pairs, 8 frames each): average edge stretch 0.035 with the convention UE Viewer describes, 0.857 with the opposite one. CloneTrooper: 65 bones, 1556 points, 3008 triangles; WalkForward 48 frames at 30 fps, height 169.1-174.2 units throughout, root moves 364.7 units per loop (removed for display). `rc_character`: `on-screen check PASS: outline match 0.991` for the clone (0.993 on 6 of 6 runs of RunForward), 0.988 for BattleDroid, 0.994 for SuperBattleDroid; comparing against a pose 22 frames off gives 0.773 and 0.858, so the 0.95 pass mark catches a wrong pose.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows (needs one run on the user's PC). The 2 "problem" animation sets are real data: an empty sequence named `None` with 0 frames (`SecCameraASet`, `TranTurretSet`). 29 bytes at the end of every mesh are not understood (UE Viewer skips them too). Only the skin's colour picture is drawn: bump maps, shininess and the material's other layers are ignored. Only the first material slot is used (the clone has one; some hud arms have up to 7). Animation notifies (footstep sounds, gun fire) are read but not used. Skinning runs on the CPU. Pitch and roll of the mesh's stored rotation are ignored (all zero in the files tested).
- **Next:** Run `rc_character` on the user's PC. Later: GPU skinning through Bevy's own skinned meshes, more material slots, blending between animations.

## 2026-10-29: Why 17 textures' small copies don't match

- **Changed:** DESIGN "How we check pictures" rewritten with the findings. No code changes.
- **Why:** 17 textures failed the mip check; we needed to know whether our decoder was wrong.
- **Tested how:** Throwaway program comparing every mip with the one before, measuring how varied each mip is and its average colour, and trying the small mips sideways and upside down.
- **Result:** The decoder is fine: the same formats give under 5 on 2,000 other textures, including 159 other non-square ones, and other orientations made things worse. The game's files store different pictures in these mips: 5 arena textures turn exactly flat grey (127, 125, 127) from mip 3 down, one blast door has a different picture on every level, 7 tiny glow sprites are likely just compression error (a guess), and the rest are screens, noise and two menu buttons.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not checked by eye.
- **Next:** Use the game's own mips in Bevy rather than generating new ones.

## 2026-10-29: Decode the two bump-map formats (12 = V8U8, 14 = L6V5U5)

- **Changed:** `crates/rc_image/src/lib.rs`: new `PixelFormat::V8U8` and `PixelFormat::L6V5U5`, decoded to normal-map colours (L in alpha), 2 new unit tests. `rc_package::texture::format_name` names them. `rc_probe` and `rc_viewer` decode them. DESIGN gains a "Bump maps" section; README updated.
- **Why:** 133 textures (the game's bump maps) couldn't be decoded.
- **Tested how:** Tried six candidate layouts on every bump map and compared each decoded mip 0, shrunk, against mip 1. Then ran `rc_probe --textures` on all 57 texture packages (copied read-only into the cloud sandbox). All 21 unit tests in the workspace pass (after bringing in PR #4).
- **Result:** Clear winners, as a fraction of the difference between unrelated pictures: format 12 as V8U8 0.06 (next best 0.36), format 14 as L6V5U5 0.08 (next best 0.28). UE Viewer's unverified guess (CxV8U8 for 14) scored 0.34. Scan: `decoded: 2312  failed: 0`, formats `29 V8U8`, `104 L6V5U5`. Bump-map mip difference median 1.8, one over 10 (11.2). `rc_viewer` shows them and its on-screen check passes: `Wookiee_Bump` (L6V5U5) 3.53, `Wookiee_HairBump` (V8U8) 7.20, and `DC15Blaster` still 3.77.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Which way V ("up") points for Bevy: not tested. What L means in format 14: unknown (maybe shininess). Not run on Windows.
- **Next:** Use them for lighting in Bevy.

All plain 16-bit PCM WAV. 291 `SoundStreamed` and 1,208 `SoundMultiple` read with nothing left over. `rc_soundtest`: rifle `wep_GEN_dc17Fire_02` 44100 Hz, 1 ch, 58069 frames, 1.317 s, peak 0.977, checksum ac6be1b553909337; ambience `amb_KAM_siege_lp_01` 22050 Hz, 2 ch, 219982 frames, 9.977 s, peak 0.534, checksum 8beac96a49db50df. With no sound card: `NOT PLAYED`, exit code 1, as intended.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Not tested on Windows or on a real sound card, so nobody has heard it yet, and the "1.00x real time" check is untested. Music (`.ogg`) is not played. 13 `amb_RAS_*` streamed loops point at files missing from the PC install. The second list inside `SoundMultiple` is always empty and its meaning is unknown. No positional (3D) sound yet.
- **Next:** Run `rc_soundtest` on the user's PC. Then play an `.ogg` music track, and later place sounds in 3D with the level work.

## 2026-10-29: Static meshes: read them, and show the DC-15 blaster in 3D

- **Changed:** New `crates/rc_package/src/static_mesh.rs` (reads `StaticMesh` objects: vertices, normals, UVs, triangles, sections, materials, and checks them), plus `Reader::f32`. New `rc_probe --meshes` (`crates/rc_probe/src/meshes.rs`, logs to `logs/rc_meshes.log`). `rc_viewer --mesh` (`crates/rc_viewer/src/mesh.rs`) shows one model with its texture, by default `DC15Blaster` from `weapons.usx`. It follows `Shader` materials to their `Diffuse` texture. Shared screenshot code in `rc_viewer/src/main.rs` was moved into `read_capture`. Bevy feature `bevy_pbr` added. 1 new unit test (one triangle built byte by byte). README and DESIGN updated.
- **Why:** Next milestone: show a game model in 3D, starting with the DC-15 because its texture is already known to be good.
- **Tested how:** Copied all 58 `.usx` packages plus `weapontextures.utx` and `hudarmstextures.utx` read-only from the install into the cloud sandbox. Ran `rc_probe --meshes` over them. Ran `rc_viewer --mesh` 3 times on DC15Blaster and once each on DC17mBlaster, DC15Pistol and ThermalDetonator, with Xvfb and software rendering. The viewer's new on-screen check draws the model itself in plain Rust and compares the outline and the colours with Bevy's screenshot. Texture mode re-run as a regression check. All 17 unit tests pass.
- **Result:** All 2,447 static meshes read, 0 failures, 0 check problems (2.09 million vertices, 1.57 million triangles, 3 s). DC15Blaster: 470 vertices, 342 triangles, 1 section, size 72.5 x 13.3 x 22.2 Unreal units, UVs 0.006 to 0.998, material `WeaponTextures.CloneWeapons.DC15Blaster` (a plain texture). 342 of 342 triangles face the same way as their stored normals after the axis swap. On-screen check: outline match 0.996, colour difference 1.04 of 255 (flipped texture: 22.88), PASS in 3 of 3 runs. DC17mBlaster 0.86, DC15Pistol 0.32, ThermalDetonator 2.12, all PASS.
- **Still broken / not tested:** Dead end: on one run the first capture showed only the background (the model wasn't drawn yet), which failed the check. Fixed by retrying, the same way as an all-black capture. 11 meshes have a few normals that are exactly zero, which the check now allows. Not tested: running on Windows. The collision data after the mesh streams isn't read. Only a Shader's `Diffuse` texture is used: no bump maps, transparency or lighting yet. The model is drawn unlit.
- **Next:** User runs `cargo run --release -p rc_viewer -- --mesh` on their PC. The level thread will reuse `rc_package::static_mesh`.
